The user approved all six Orchestrator recommendations:
1. **2.1 B7.** Schedule a narrow fix task for B1 only: slow sliding in ball-wide lanes of end-limited level guides. B2 is accepted as a known limit: dense level coils build in up to about 0.3 s, load time only, and no worse than 329c843. The marginal new sloped rejection (a C ramp with 45 mm between its facing ends) is accepted.
2. **3.2.** The Orchestrator's pass ruling is confirmed. Plunger launch strength is to be checked on real analog hardware before any estimator change.
3. **Rules-script engine budgets confirmed:** 100 000 instructions and 2 MiB per callback for Lua. Subphase 2.5 must build backends with `LuaConfig::engine()` and `WasmConfig::engine()` (via `load_backend_with`) and spawn the sim thread with at least `MIN_HOST_STACK_BYTES` of stack. These go into the 2.5 brief. Revisit the numbers once real tables exist.
4. **Loader guide rules (4.1), all warnings, never errors:**
   - (a) Uneven through-point spacing is warned about and never resampled.
   - (b) Through-point spacing at or above the smallest radius of curvature is warned about.
   - (c) A table that combines the XL ball profile with sloped guides tighter than that ball's radius is warned about.
   - (d) Lints for a hairline gap between a guide's facing open ends (under 1 mm, above the 1e-6 m snap) and for an open end that nearly touches its own wall (`GuideCurve::reach() == 0`). The message says "close the guide or open the gap".
5. **Wasm compile-cost limits** are accepted as they are. Revisit them when tables are ported to Wasm in 6.3.
6. **Security hardening sweep** before Wave 4 builds on the loader:
   - **4.1 review round 2:** N-a (Zip64 count), N-b (archive-level ratio), N-c (PNG decode peak), N-d (glTF buffer budget by bytes actually read).
   - **3.3 review round 2:** N10, which caps the resampler reservation.
   - **3.3 review N3:** an aggregate decoded-audio budget per bundle, which must charge decoded frames. pin_audio provides the budget API; whoever loads a bundle's sounds (2.5/6.0) must use it.
   - Non-security review notes are folded into the briefs of the subphases that next touch those crates.

**Fix-task assignment.** The B1 task goes to Opus, which wrote the code it changes (the other-model attempt), and Fable reviews it. The original 2.1 implementer, Fable, already used its attempts on B7. The 4.1 and 3.3 tasks go to their owners (Opus), and Fable reviews both.

## 4.1 — fix task H (hardening N-a…N-d, guide lints)

- **N-a Zip64 count.** Whenever a Zip64 locator precedes an end-record candidate (whatever its 16-bit count), the Zip64 record is read and its count checked against `max_entries` before the central directory is parsed (O(1), no scan). The record must be exactly where the locator says, end exactly at the locator, be single-disk with equal counts, and have room for its declared records before it; otherwise the zip reader would search the whole archive for another record (prepended-data support), so the archive is refused (`Zip`, or `TooManyEntries` when the impossible count is over the cap). Consequence: Zip64 archives with prepended data are not accepted as bundles. More than 16 locator candidates is `Zip`. `Vfs::from_zip_bytes` has the archive-size cap of `open` (`max_total_uncompressed` + 1 MiB → `TotalTooLarge`). Measured (debug test profile): the reviewer's 2 M-record archive (180 MB) is refused in 10 ms with no extra allocation.
- **N-b archive ratio.** At open, the running total of declared uncompressed sizes may exceed `max_ratio` × archive length only while it is ≤ `zip::ARCHIVE_RATIO_FLOOR` = 8 MiB; otherwise `CompressionRatio { path: "(whole archive)" }` (checked after each entry's data range, so lying sizes still report as `Zip`). Any archive thus yields at most max(8 MiB, 100 × its length) bytes; per-entry rules unchanged.
- **N-c image decode.** New `GltfLimits::max_image_decode_bytes` (per image, default 256 MiB): the decoder's native buffer (from the header's colour type and bit depth) plus the RGBA8 copy when the native type is not RGBA8, checked from the header before any pixel is allocated; the decoder's `max_alloc` is that peak + 1 MiB (was 3 × RGBA8 + 1 MiB). `max_image_bytes` stays the per-scene RGBA8 budget. 8192² RGBA8 fits; 8192² 16-bit (768 MiB) does not. An encoded image file over the per-image budget + 1 MiB is not read.
- **N-d glTF buffers.** A buffer file is sized through the VFS first (`Vfs::file_size`), refused if shorter than its `byteLength`, and only its first `byteLength` bytes are read/inflated (`Vfs::read_prefix`; no CRC check on a partial read). Every source is charged the bytes actually held (a `data:` URI or the BIN chunk its whole decoded length) against `max_buffer_bytes`, on top of the existing check of the declared sum.
- **Guide lints (human ruling item 4), warnings at `splines[i]`:** (a) unchanged (`splines[i].points`). (b) longest through-point spacing ≥ smallest radius of curvature. The radius is the minimum over 64 evenly spaced parameters per segment plus a golden-section refinement of the largest sample; curvature is the larger of the space curve's and the plan view's (a sloped guide's wall follows its plan view). Guides only (wireforms do not use the guide sampling bounds). (c) any profile named in `balls.profiles`, `balls.restrict` or dispenser contents whose radius exceeds a sloped guide's smallest radius of curvature (in practice only `jumbo_xl_resin`; no profile name is special-cased); one warning per guide, naming the widest such profile. (d) level guide, open ends < 1 mm apart (not snapped) → "close the guide or open the gap"; otherwise a level guide with `reach()` < 1 µm (exactly 0 for an end within ~0.1 mm of its wall; a self-crossing curve can come out at nanometres) → same advice when open, "move the through-points apart" when closed.
- **Spec test conflict.** `spec_manifest::uneven_spline_spacing_is_a_warning_not_an_error` asserts exactly one warning under `splines[0]` for a guide through (0,0), (10,0), (100,0), (110,50) mm. That curve doubles back at its start (a cusp from the uneven spacing), so rulings (b) and (d) correctly add two warnings at `splines[0]`. The test predates the ruling; it should count only `splines[0].points` (Tester's file, not edited).

## 3.3 — fix task 3.3-H (decode-memory hardening: N10, N3, N11–N13)

- **N10 reservation.** The resampler's output buffer no longer reserves from the WAV header or the last Ogg granule: the first reservation is at most `INITIAL_RESERVE_FRAMES` = 65 536 frames (512 KiB, less when the claim is smaller), then it grows on demand by `max(produced, 1024)` frames with `reserve_exact`, clamped to the 10-minute cap, so capacity ≤ max(512 KiB, 2 × output) and is trimmed on finish. A 3.8 KB stream claiming 10 minutes now peaks at < 1 MiB per allocation (was 230 MB at 48 kHz, 1.8 GB at 384 kHz). Outputs are bit-identical (only allocation changed).
- **N3 budget API.** `pin_audio::mixer::DecodeBudget` (bytes of decoded f32, `BYTES_PER_FRAME` = 8; `DEFAULT_BYTES` = 512 MiB ≈ 23 min of 48 kHz stereo; `new`, `unlimited`, `limit_bytes`, `used_bytes`, `remaining_bytes`, `release(&Sample)`) and `decode_with_budget(bytes, rate, &DecodeBudget)`. Each growth step of the output buffer is charged atomically before it is allocated (never from headers), partial grants allowed; the unused tail is refunded on success and everything on any error or panic (`Drop`). So after each call `used` = Σ 8·frames of the returned samples, and a decode succeeds iff its output fits the remainder (exact for sequential decodes; concurrent decodes may fail early on another's spare capacity, never overcommit). Exhaustion is the new `DecodeError::BudgetExhausted` (additive variant). `decode` = `decode_with_budget` with an unlimited budget. `Sync`, shareable via `Arc` across I/O workers.
- **Hand-off.** Wiring the budget into bundle loading belongs to 2.5/6.0: whoever loads a bundle's `[[sounds]]` creates one `DecodeBudget` per bundle (default unless configured), decodes every sound with `decode_with_budget`, reports `BudgetExhausted` as a load error, and calls `release` when a sample is dropped (e.g. hot reload).
- **N11–N13 docs.** `DecodeError::Unsupported` now lists the source-rate range and chained/multiplexed Ogg; module docs state that header-only Ogg and empty-data WAV give `Ok` with 0 frames, trailing bytes after the last page / data chunk are ignored, and a stream's leading-sample discard is trimmed at the end rather than the start.

## 4.1 — fix task H, review round 1 (B1–B4 and follow-ups)

- **One end record, no reader searches (B1, B2; supersedes the N-a bullet above).** `zip::find_end` picks the last end-of-central-directory signature whose comment ends exactly at the end of the archive (searching the reader's own 64 KiB window); trailing bytes are refused. The zip reader is then opened with `ArchiveOffset::Known(0)` on a view of the archive that ends at that record with an empty comment, so it never searches for another candidate, a moved directory or a moved Zip64 record. Every check the reader would make before falling back is made first and failing one refuses the archive: single disk; Zip64 (when a field is saturated and a locator precedes the record) exactly at the locator's offset, ending at the locator, extensible sector ≤ `zip::MAX_ZIP64_EXTENSIBLE` = 64 KiB, room for its records; the directory ends exactly at the (Zip64) end record and starts with a central-header signature; count ≤ `max_entries`. Effect: all reviewer probes (2 M records with or without the off-by-one comment, 64/400 MiB signature floods, 200 candidates, 200 MiB extensible sector) are refused in ≤ 27 ms with no extra allocation (debug build); archives with prepended data (self-extractors) are not bundles and are refused; a comment containing `PK\x05\x06` no longer confuses the check. The 16-locator cap is gone (only one record is ever looked at).
- **glTF JSON cap (B3).** `GltfLimits::max_json_bytes` (default 16 MiB): a `.gltf` file's size, or a GLB's JSON chunk length, is checked before the file is read; a GLB file is at most that plus `max_buffer_bytes` + 64 bytes. `data:` URI keys borrow from the document (no copies), the GLB `BIN` chunk is cut out of the file bytes in place, and the JSON text is dropped once parsed. Peak while loading ≈ 2 × the JSON + the decoded URI (an 11.5 MiB `data:` buffer in a 15 MiB `.gltf`: 31 MiB extra; 40 MiB and 240 MiB files: refused at 0 MiB).
- **Images.** The header is read with `gltf::IMAGE_HEADER_ALLOC` = 4 MiB for the decoder's own buffers (the PNG decoder keeps that allowance for ancillary chunks during the decode, so a 250 MiB `iCCP` bomb stops at 4 MiB and the profile is dropped). An image file over 1 MiB has its header read from its first 1 MiB (`IMAGE_HEADER_PREFIX`) and is read whole only when its size is ≤ that image's decode bytes + 1 MiB. Before the pixel buffer is allocated, the encoded size must be plausible for the dimensions (PNG: filtered scanlines / 1200, deflate's limit being about 1032:1; JPEG: w·h / 1024, at least one bit per 8×8 luma block halved), so a truncated 69-byte file claiming 8192² no longer allocates 256 MiB before failing.
- **Guide lints (B4 and follow-ups).**
  - (b) is per bend: each segment's tightest bend (64 samples + refinement) is compared with the spacings next to it (its own and its neighbours', wrapping on closed guides); one warning per guide, at the worst ratio, naming the spacing (`points[j] to points[j+1]`) and the bend's segment. A tangent turning more than 90° between neighbouring samples is a cusp or reversal, radius 0 (catches collinear reversals, whose curvature is zero).
  - (c) honours `balls.restrict`: a restricted profile counts only on its listed layers.
  - (d) end near wall is measured directly: the distance from each open end to the polyline (32 samples per segment) beyond 4 mm along the curve; under 1 mm (the hairline rule's threshold) gives "an open end is only X mm from the guide's own wall near points[k]; close the guide or open the gap", unless the curve gets there through a cusp or sub-millimetre bend (then it just doubles back). Otherwise, if any two stretches at least 4 mm apart along the curve come within 1 mm in plan view (sweep and prune, ~linear) or the reach is below 1 µm: "the curve doubles back or touches itself near points[k] …; even out the through-point spacing or move the points apart" — no gap advice. A J tip 0.3–0.9 mm from its wall now warns; at 1.1 mm it does not.

## 2.1 — fix task B1: ball-wide lanes of end-limited level guides

**Decision:** supersedes the "Table precision" bullet of "2.1 — fix task B7, other-model attempt" (everything else there stands: proven bounds, half-line reach, bit identity on guides that are not end-limited, no new rejection).
- **Reach no longer lowered by tolerances.** Which guides are end-limited (and get a table) is decided exactly as before, from the first end search (2 %), so classification and bit identity are unchanged. But the reach itself: where `0.9 ×` the end terms fall below the sampled estimate, the ends are searched again with a *target* (`with_target`: pieces settle only once proven at or above `sampled / 0.9`), skipped when a curve point behind the end already lies within the target (the end binds); and the table is built to prove the reach found so far (same mechanism), so `smallest()` only lowers it where an entry truly binds. B1 spiral (pitch 40 mm, 3 turns, 101 points): reach 13.98 → 17.86 mm, bit for bit the sampled value (unit test).
- **New run-time bound: the lane.** Each table interval also stores `R` (smallest own-neighbourhood bound `1/κ` of the curve pieces its search skipped) and `D` (proven distance from the interval to every other piece, searched as `D²`; less 10 µm). Proof (`TangentBalls::lane`): the own neighbourhood stays outside both balls of radius `R` touching the wall at the nearest point; everything else is at least `D` from it. The level cast takes `min(rolling_ball_time(R), (D − distance − radius − floor)/speed)` (aimed at half the skin, so a sliding ball stays within 5 µm). A tangent ball in a lane is at most half the lane wide, so a ball barely narrower than the lane crawled; the lane bound advances at the pace of the wall's own bend. Away from the ends only (`END_BAND`).
- **Sharper table bounds (second order).** `TableProbe` bounds 3 (ball, decomposed along/across the slot), 4 (part with its middle) and 5 (a `Rod` per tree node: segment through the node's middle along its tangent, radius `κδ²/2`), all proven in the type's comment; validated against sampled pairs (unit test, tightest ratio 0.9999). Tolerances: `ρ_±` 30 % as before; `D` 4 % up to 40 mm, growing linearly to 30 % at 100 mm (precision only matters where a pinball fits snugly; the XL ball gets no lane bound, as it had none before); `D` below 10 mm stored as zero.
- **Cost.** Refinement costs one budget unit; per-slot budget 80 → 72 (only guides that exhaust it are affected; those reached 0 before and still do, except the 12-turn r0.3 coil, which now gets a full table, reach 17.64 mm, N1). Construction, release, against the pre-task code (wall time, interleaved, best of 8–20): the review's coils 0.73–1.11×, the other-model attempt's report table 0.61–1.14×, B1-band spirals 0.88–1.16×; every level guide within 2 × 329c843 + 2 ms (worst 0.70 of that limit). Above +10 %: 12-turn r0.3 coil 1.11× (it now builds its whole table), 300-segment hook 1.12× (+0.2 ms), 2000-segment uneven 0.2 mm-gap ring 1.14× (+3.5 ms), 2000-segment 30 mm spiral 1.14× (+3.7 ms) (HUMAN_QUEUE).
- **Results.** B1 scenario: 0/191 slow ticks (worst 0.998), cast 1.0 step vs 10.0 (329c843). Pitch band 34–44 mm, steel/mini/jumbo, 3–12 turns: 0 slow ticks anywhere the lane table is complete (see review N1/N2: budget-exhausted outer turns and coarsely sampled spirals keep the 329c843 behaviour) (329c843: jumbo crawled everywhere, steel at 34–36 mm), deepest −7.6 µm; casts 0.05–0.38× the cost of 329c843.
- **Tests:** `world_open_ends.rs` `a_steel_ball_slides_along_a_ball_wide_spiral_lane_at_full_speed` (fails on the previous code: 185/191 slow), `balls_slide_along_spiral_lanes_of_34_to_44_mm_at_full_speed`, `casts_along_spiral_lanes_never_sweep_into_the_wall`; `wireform.rs` `lanes_of_spirals_rings_hooks_and_js_hold`, `the_b1_spiral_keeps_the_reach_its_sampled_pairs_justify`; `curve_bounds.rs` `table_bounds_are_lower_bounds_of_the_pairs_they_cover`. Re-baselined assertions: none.
**Rationale:** human ruling (end of Wave 3), item 1; brief fix_2_1_b1 targets.

## 4.1 — fix task H, review round 2 (B5)

- **Self-proximity search (lint d) is a uniform grid, not an x-sweep.** The polyline now uses pieces of about 0.5 mm (at least 4 per segment, at most 2^17 pieces in total, longer pieces beyond), so piece lengths are comparable. Grid cell = longest piece (plan-view extent) + 1 mm; each piece goes into the cells its gap-grown box touches (≤ 2 × 2), so any two pieces within 1 mm share a cell. Inside a cell pieces are in curve order; each is measured only against pieces ≥ 4 mm further along (binary search), and the search stops at the first pair under 1 mm (only existence and a location are needed). The work is linear in the pieces whatever the orientation or clustering. Unit tests assert a deterministic work count (pairs measured): 4096-point vertical, diagonal and zig-zag guides ≤ 1 per piece, a 4096-point blob in 1 mm² ≤ 64 in total, two 2000-point legs 1.5 mm apart ≤ 32 per piece. An `#[ignore]` timing report (`lint_timing_report`) asserts < 50 ms for all lints. Measured: 17–26 ms in the test profile (opt-level 1), 12–19 ms in release, for 4096 points. Whole `load_table` runs for the reviewer's p_* cases: 11–29 ms (before: 2.5 s, 41 s and > 300 s).
- **At most 4096 through-points per spline** (`MAX_SPLINE_POINTS`), as an error at `splines[i].points` ("split it into several splines or use fewer points"). Real guides and wireforms have tens of points; 4096 at 1 mm spacing is 4 m of rail, longer than any playfield, and the cap bounds the physics build and the lints.
- Messages: a glTF JSON over the cap suggests exporting a .glb or keeping buffers and images as separate files; an image header beyond the first 1 MiB suggests stripping embedded metadata.

## Orchestrator — 2.1-B1 construction "now + 10 %" overrun accepted
The 2.1-B1 brief, written by the Orchestrator, capped construction at the pre-task time + 10 %, so that the B2 limit the user accepted would not grow. Four guides go over that cap:

| Guide | Slower by | Extra time |
|---|---|---|
| r0.3, 12-turn coil | 1.11× | — (now builds a full table: reach 17.64 mm, was 0) |
| 300-segment hook | 1.12× | +0.2 ms |
| 2 000-segment uneven ring | 1.14× | +3.5 ms |
| 2 000-segment spiral | 1.14× | +3.7 ms |

Every one stays inside 2 × 329c843 + 2 ms (worst 0.70 of that bound) and far below the accepted worst case of about 0.3 s, so the user's ruling is respected. The overrun is accepted, and it is not a review blocker.

## Orchestrator — 2.1-B1 review pass; residual limits N1/N2

- Fable review APPROVED 7ec28b2 (no blockers): 5.49 M lane checks, 0 violations; bit-identical on all 239 shapes not end-limited; regression tests fail on 05dd386.
- N1 (budget-exhausted outer turns of 12-turn/600-segment and 20-turn coils still crawl) and N2 (spirals sampled below ~25–30 points per turn are not classified end-limited and behave exactly like 329c843) are literal misses of the pitch-band target on subclasses outside the task's scope. Neither is worse than 329c843 except N1, which matches 05dd386. Ruling: accepted as known limits for now, queued in HUMAN_QUEUE; closing N2 needs lane tables for all level guides, which changes trajectories (bit identity) and is a human decision.

## Human ruling — decoded-audio budget (512 MiB, user setting)

- **Ruling (user):** the per-bundle decoded-audio budget defaults to **512 MiB** (`DecodeBudget::DEFAULT_BYTES`, ≈ 23 min of 48 kHz stereo f32) and is a **user setting**, not a fixed constant. It is a safety cap against untrusted bundles (D25), not a reservation: only audio actually decoded counts.
- **4.4 (config.toml / menu):** add `audio.decoded_budget_mib` to `config.toml`, default 512, persisted like the other settings. Accept 64…8192 MiB; out-of-range or unparsable values fall back to 512 with a logged warning. Expose it in an advanced/audio section of the menu; a change applies to the next table load.
- **2.5 / 6.0 (bundle loader):** create one `DecodeBudget::new(setting × 1 MiB)` per bundle, decode every sound through `decode_with_budget`, `release` dropped samples. On `BudgetExhausted` the load fails with a message naming the sound that exceeded the budget, the budget, and the setting to raise.
- **Follow-up (queued):** stream music and store mono as mono before real content replaces placeholders, so authored tables stay well under the default.

## Orchestrator — Wave 4 brief decisions

- **Seam task 2.2-S before 2.3, 2.4 and 2.7.** All three need the ball solver to see something it cannot see today: flippers and moving mech parts (kickers, plunger tip, spinner flap) are moving colliders that must take part in the ball's CCD inside `BallSolver::step`, and remanence needs each magnet's position, power and range plus per-ball magnetisation that lives as long as the ball. Three subphases editing `ball/solver.rs` at once in one working tree would collide, so one seam task lands first: (a) a generic moving-body interface in `pin_physics::ball` (bodies with their own motion over the tick, swept sphere test, resting contacts, surface velocity, inverse effective mass at a point, and an impulse back to the body for the two-body impact; a new `ImpactTarget` variant), and (b) an external-force context that gives hooks the magnets and a per-ball lifecycle (added/removed), so 2.7 can keep per-ball state without owning the ball arena. With no bodies and no hooks, every 2.2 trajectory stays bit-identical. Implementer: Opus 5.5 (wrote the solver code on the branch, 2.2 other-model attempt); Reviewer: Fable 5.2. The plan's "per-ball magnetisation state ... via a contract-change request" (2.7 deliverable 2) is met by this seam, since the ball arena is in `pin_physics`, not `pin_core`.
- **Scheduling.** The container has 4 cores and 15 GB of RAM. At most three subphases build at the same time (plus the seam task); every cargo command uses `CARGO_BUILD_JOBS=2`. Batch A: 2.2-S, 3.4, 4.3. Batch B as slots free up: 4.6, 5.1, then 2.3, 2.4, 2.7 once 2.2-S has passed review. Each subphase's Implementer and Tester share `target/sp_X_Y`, which is deleted when the subphase passes.
- **Ownership amendments.**
  - 2.4 owns `pin_physics/src/mech/`, an additive `pin_core` CR for kind-specific `[[mechs]]` parameters (1.1 review N7), and a new mech section in `physics_defaults.toml`.
  - 2.7 owns `pin_physics/src/ball_ext/` and whatever `pin_assets` validation change is needed to accept `low_rem_steel`.
  - 3.4 owns `pin_audio/src/ssf/`, `pin_audio/src/telemetry/`, `pin_input/src/haptics/`, a new `pin_app/src/platform/rumble.rs` (SDL rumble output) with its one `mod` line, and the 3.3-H small cleanups R5, R6 and R9 in `pin_audio/src/mixer/decode.rs`.
  - 4.3 owns `pin_tablegen` (library and CLI) and the 4.1-H follow-ups in `pin_assets/src/validate/` (coordinate bound, warning location, 2.1-B1 N4 lint text).
  - 5.1 owns `pin_render/src/pbr/` and the 1.4 follow-ups that are in `pin_render`: round-2 N-a (`try_configure` logging) and N-c (small debug circles), and gate-fix N5 (bounded device wait). The 1.4 items in `pin_app` (round-2 N-b, gate-fix N2–N4) wait for the next `pin_app` subphase (4.4 or 6.0).
  - Every subphase may add exact pins to the root `[workspace.dependencies]` under a "Wave 4 additions" comment, one line per crate naming the subphase. `versions.toml` stays unchanged, as in Wave 3.
- **SSF channel map (3.4).** The SMPTE 7.1 order is FL, FR, FC, LFE, BL, BR, SL, SR. Playfield exciters use SL/SR for the front corners (near the flippers, low y) and BL/BR for the back corners (high y). The panner is equal-power bilinear over the playfield rectangle given at construction. FL/FR/FC/LFE are left to the music/backbox mix.
- **Commits.** Agents do not commit. The Orchestrator commits each subphase's owned paths as `[sp X.Y]` commits after each role finishes, and writes WIP snapshots when needed.

## 3.4 — Haptics bus, SSF mixer and UDP telemetry (Implementer)

- **Decision (rich haptic event, CR-003).** New `pin_core::haptics::{HapticEvent, HapticKind}` (`CoilFire{coil}`, `EosHit{flipper}`, `Bumper{mech}`, `BallDrop{ball, position_mm}`, `BallRoll{ball, position_mm, mass_kg, speed_m_s}`, `Impulse{source}`); `HapticKind` is `#[non_exhaustive]`. The old `HapticsTelemetryEvent` converts into `Impulse` and every impulse kind converts back (`to_telemetry_event`; `BallRoll` is not an impulse). Source ids pack a 4-bit class over a 12-bit element number (coil 0, EOS 1, bumper 2, ball drop 3, ball roll 4), documented in docs/TELEMETRY_PROTOCOL.md §5. **Rationale:** one `u16` id per source for telemetry and per-source rumble settings without a lookup table. **Alternatives:** a separate id table at table load (more state on three threads); free-form source ids (collide between coils and flippers).
- **Decision (one SPSC ring per consumer).** `pin_input::haptics::haptics_ring<E>` wraps `pin_core::spsc` with a shared relaxed drop counter (full ring drops the newest); `HapticsBus` fans one sim-thread push out to up to four rings (rumble on the main thread, SSF on the audio thread, ...). The ring is generic over the event type (default `HapticEvent`) so the legacy event works too. **Alternatives:** an MPMC broadcast ring (locks or allocation); a single ring with the main thread re-forwarding (adds a hop and latency to the audio thread).
- **Decision (rumble mapper).** Per-kind effect (low/high peak, duration in ticks) plus up to 32 per-source overrides, scaled by clamped strength and master; linear decay to exactly 0 at `t₀ + duration`; overlapping envelopes merge by per-motor max; 32 envelope slots, a full table replaces the weakest envelope only if the new one is stronger. The SDL side (`pin_app::platform::rumble`) sends a command when the 16-bit levels change, refreshes non-zero levels every 50 ticks with a 100 ms SDL duration (a stalled main thread falls silent by itself). Slings and flippers both arrive as `CoilFire`; tables tell them apart by per-source overrides. **Alternatives:** hold-then-release envelopes (step at release); summing (exceeds 1).
- **Decision (SSF ↔ 3.3 mixer interface).** The SSF mixer renders its own interleaved 7.1 buffer (exciters on SL/SR/BL/BR, FL/FR/FC/LFE silent); on a 7.1 device the audio callback renders the 3.3 stereo mix into a scratch buffer and `SsfMixer::render_with_stereo` adds it onto FL/FR before the final hard clip. The 3.3 mixer stays stereo and unchanged. Roll loop: one voice per ball slot (16), gain `roll_gain · s · m` and pitch `pmin + (pmax − pmin) · s` with `s = speed/3 m/s`, `m = mass/0.0806 kg` (≤ 2), 10 ms ramps, fades after 40 ms without an event; built-in loop is a 35 Hz sine table (a loaded sample can replace it). Impulses: 45 Hz sine thumps with a 60 ms exponential decay, 16 voices, oldest stolen; non-positional kinds are panned at positions registered per source id (centre otherwise). Gains computed with `libm` in f64. **Alternatives:** a 7.1 3.3 mixer with per-voice panning (rewrites a reviewed subphase); noise-based roll (needs a seeded generator for determinism, no audible gain on exciters).
- **Decision (telemetry encoder).** `TelemetryState` holds 256 switches, 256 coils, 256 lamps, `GI_COUNT` = 32 GI strings and `MAGNET_COUNT` = 32 magnets; full state = 832 entries (5 datagrams). Full state on the first tick, after `force_full_state`, when the tick goes backwards, and when ≥ 1000 ticks have passed since the previous full state (anchored to the last full state, not to `tick % 1000`); impulses ride along in full-state ticks and are capped at 64 per tick (counted). `DatagramBatch` holds up to 6 encoded datagrams inline. The sim thread pushes the batch through a 64-datagram SPSC ring to `TelemetrySender` (owns the `UdpSocket`, own thread, parks 1 ms when idle); `push_batch` returning `false` tells the sim loop to force a full state. **Alternatives:** encoding on the I/O thread from a state ring (copies 1.8 KB of state per tick instead of ~30 bytes of changes).
- **Decision (environment).** This container lacked the SDL3 build headers the CI installs (`.github/workflows/ci.yml`), so `sdl3-sys` could not build; the Implementer installed the CI's package list with apt (cmake, X11/Wayland/udev/dbus/ALSA/Pulse dev headers). No repository change.

## 4.3 — Procedural table generator (`pin_tablegen::layout`)

- **Decision (frame and units of `playfield.glb`).** Table frame (`x` across, `y` up the table, `z` the playfield normal, the physics frame), **metres**, identity node transforms, one node per element with one triangle primitive. No glTF Y-up conversion. **Rationale:** the loader and physics already use this frame in SI; a Y-up swap would have to be undone everywhere. **Alternatives:** glTF Y-up (needs a fixed swap in 5.1/6.0); millimetres (breaks the glTF metre convention).
- **Decision (what collides how).** Floor (one two-triangle mesh, layer 0, `wood`), decks (one triangulated outline each, layer `layer`), walls (zero-thickness extruded polyline ribbons to `wall_height`) and posts (inscribed n-gon prisms with ≤ 0.02 mm sagitta, `rubber` or `steel`) are trimesh colliders: extras `collider: true`, `collision_layer`, `material` (also the glTF material name = `Material::name()`); the node mesh is its own collision shape. Lanes become two **level guide `[[splines]]`** (`tablegen:lanes[i].left/right`) at ±width/2 from a natural spline through the centre points, with visual-only ribbon nodes of the same name (extras `guide`, `collider: false`) sampled from the loader-built spline within 0.02 mm. Ramps reference a base `[[splines]]` entry; the node `ramps[i]` is visual only (wire tubes or a guide ribbon, extras `spline`), and a non-zero-layer ramp gets a `[[layer_transitions]]` `tablegen:ramps[i].entry` (0 → spline layer, a box `entry_width` wide around the first through-point). Inserts are lamp-bound, non-colliding flat shapes 0.1 mm above the floor. **Rationale:** polyline walls have real corners a C² guide cannot follow within 0.1 mm; lanes are curved rails, which guides model best; `table.toml` has no collider section, so splines and transitions are the only declarations to emit. **Alternatives:** walls as guides with filleted corners (changes authored geometry); lanes as trimesh ribbons (no guide clearance machinery).
- **Decision (rail through-points).** Equal arc-length spacing on each rail of min(20 mm, 0.4 × the rail's tightest radius), ≥ 0.5 mm, ≤ 4096 points; then the rail is built exactly as the loader builds it and must construct, have reach ≥ 1 µm, keep spacing < 0.9 × its tightest radius and a spacing ratio ≤ 2.5 (halving the spacing up to 3 times), else the lane is refused. A rail on the inside of a bend tighter than 1.25 × half the width is refused. Lane centre ends within 1e-3 mm close the lane (snapped exactly, built periodic); ends < 1 mm apart otherwise are refused (hairline). So generated guides never get a loader error or authoring warning.
- **Decision (re-runs).** Generated `table.toml` entries have ids starting `tablegen:`; `generate` drops any such entries from the base before appending, so running on its own output is idempotent (byte-identical). `meta.playfield_mesh` is forced to `playfield.glb`. The written `table.toml` is the serialised manifest with a two-line header comment (hand-written comments of the base are not kept).
- **Decision (input bounds).** Coordinates within ±5000 mm; sizes (playfield, wall height, deck z, post radius, lane width, insert size, ramp entry width) from `MIN_SIZE_MM` = 0.1 mm to 1000 mm (playfield 5000); ≤ 1024 points per element; closed walls and deck outlines must be simple polygons; consecutive repeated points are errors. Below 0.1 mm a ramp entry zone collapses in f32 (Tester defect D1); the zone is also checked min < max explicitly.
- **Decision (determinism).** The GLB JSON is written by hand in fixed key order, floats in shortest round-trip form, transcendental functions from `libm`; no hash-map iteration.
- **Decision (4.1-H follow-ups).** (a) Spline through-points farther than 10 m from the origin are an error at `splines[i].points[j]`; a spline longer than 65 m (through-point chords, then the built curve's 8-chord-per-segment estimate) is an error at `splines[i].points`. 65 m < 2^17 × 0.5 mm keeps every lint polyline piece ≈ 0.5 mm, which is what keeps `closest_far_pair` linear; its doc comment now says so instead of "whatever the orientation". (b, c) The physics API cannot tell a near-touching tip from a coil whose lane table ran out of budget (both are `reach() == 0`), so the warning raised by the reach alone (no geometric finding) names no location and covers both: "no clearance could be proven … (close the guide or open the gap) … or a long coil whose clearance table ran out of budget (split it …)". Geometric doubling back keeps its "near points[k]" text.

## 4.6 — Placeholder asset kit (pin_assetgen)

- **Decision (stable ids and layout).** `generate_kit` writes 36 files and `assets.toml`: sound effects `sfx_*` (the 9 `SFX_IDS`) at `audio/<id>.wav`; one music loop `music_main` at `audio/music_main.wav`; ten callout motifs `callout_extra_ball`, `callout_jackpot`, `callout_multiball`, `callout_tilt`, `callout_game_over`, `callout_ball_saved`, `callout_replay`, `callout_high_score`, `callout_mode_start`, `callout_mode_complete` (`sounds::MOTIF_IDS`) at `audio/<id>.wav`; font sheets `font_dmd_5x7` / `font_dmd_8x16` at `fonts/dmd_*.png`; images `img_backglass_base`, `img_backglass_logo`, `img_backglass_logo_mask` at `backglass/base.png`, `logo.png`, `logo_mask.png` (exactly the A.2 example's layer paths, so the example `backglass.toml` works on a kit unchanged), `img_playfield` at `art/playfield.png` (514 × 1070 px, 1 px/mm of the A.3 playfield), and `img_insert_<name>` at `art/inserts/<name>.png` for extra_ball, shoot_again, jackpot, super_jackpot, multiball, lock, special, x2, x3, x5. `KitManifest::sound_defs()` gives `[[sounds]]` entries (sfx → `sfx`, music → `music`, motifs → `callout`).
- **Decision (manifest format).** `assets.toml`: `schema_version = 1`, `generator = "pin_assetgen"`, `generator_version`, a `[table]` record (title, palette as `#rrggbb`, seed as a decimal string because TOML integers cannot hold every u64), and `[[entries]]` with the `KitEntry` field names (`id`, `path`, `kind` lowercase, `generator_version`, `sha256_or_hash`). The hash is the plain lowercase-hex SHA-256 of the file (own FIPS 180-4 implementation, `pin_assetgen::sha256_hex`, so no new dependency). `KitManifest::from_toml` rejects unknown keys, other schema versions, duplicate ids and non-bundle paths.
- **Decision (what varies).** Sound effects, motifs and font sheets are fixed (independent of the metadata, so they keep the same hash on every table); the music loop and every image derive from `meta_seed` = SplitMix64(FNV-1a of length-prefixed title, palette and seed). The palette's first colour biases the music mode (dark → minor, 1 in 4 flipped by the seed). Empty palette → the A.1 example colours; palette entries cycle.
- **Decision (audio).** Mono 16-bit PCM WAV at 48 kHz via `hound`, each sound peak-normalised to 0.89 (≈ −1 dBFS; measured RMS −6 to −19 dBFS). `render_sfx`/`render_motif`/`render_music` accept 8–384 kHz (pin_audio's source-rate range) and return `None` outside it or for an unknown id. The ball-roll loop (2 s) is periodic by construction (whole-cycle partials, whole-cycle LFO, circularly filtered noise); documented seam bound `BALL_ROLL_SEAM_BOUND` = 0.02 of full scale at 48 kHz (measured 0.003) and never larger than the loop's largest neighbouring-sample step at any tested rate. The music loop is 4 bars at 100–140 BPM with release tails folded onto the start (seamless too).
- **Decision (fonts).** 5×7 glyphs are the generic dot-matrix letter forms, entered as row bitmaps; 8×16 is derived at compile time (rows doubled into rows 1–14, columns two pixels wide: a bold 6-px body centred in 8). Out-of-range characters (`glyph` → `None`) render as a hollow-box fallback filling the cell. `render_text` lays out one line with 1 px spacing; empty text is `(0, font height)`. `blit_text` composites scaled text into a byte buffer for 5.3.
- **Decision (PNG).** Own encoder: 8-bit grey/RGB/RGBA, filter Sub on every row, zlib level 6 through `flate2` (zlib-rs), one IDAT, no ancillary chunks.
- **Decision (scenes).** The kit provides no `[[scenes]]` files and has no Scene kind (the brief's `AssetKind` has none): the DMD scene format is 5.3's. Verification 5 therefore covers the sounds the kit claims; the A.1 example's `sfx_bumper` resolves to `audio/sfx_bumper.wav`.
- **Alternatives.** Ogg output (no encoder without a new dependency); `image`'s PNG encoder (its output may change with `png` crate updates; ours is pinned by our code); seeding the SFX from the table (would make the "SFX set unchanged" property impossible); Ogg/compressed music (left to the audio follow-up in the human queue).
- **Correction / review polish (4.6 N1–N6).** The "what varies" decision above overstates the seeding: the music loop, `img_backglass_base` and `img_playfield` derive from `meta_seed` (title, palette and seed); `img_backglass_logo` and `img_backglass_logo_mask` depend only on the title and palette (no seed); the insert labels depend only on the palette. Polish: playfield lane chevrons now pass through the same contrast check as the title (`readable` against the lane background); every tone and noise burst has at least a 0.5 ms linear attack (`MIN_ATTACK`), so no sound starts with a step; `pin_assetgen --help`/`-h` prints usage on stdout and exits 0 (errors stay on stderr, exit 2); implementer tests write under `CARGO_TARGET_TMPDIR` and remove their directories.

## 2.2-S — moving-body and force-context seams

API reference for Subphases 2.3 (flippers), 2.4 (mechs) and 2.7 (Magnus, remanence). The full rustdoc contract is in `pin_physics::ball::body` (module docs) and on `ExternalForce`/`ForceContext`; this entry is the summary those briefs may rely on. Everything here is in `pin_physics::ball` (re-exported from `ball::body` and `ball::types`).

### (A) Moving bodies

- **Decision (plumbing: bodies are lent to each step, not registered).** `BallSolver::step_with_bodies(&mut self, geo: &Geometry<'_>, bodies: &mut [&mut dyn MovingBodies], dt: f32, events: &mut BallEvents)`; `step(geo, dt, events)` is exactly `step_with_bodies(geo, &mut [], dt, events)`. A body *set* is identified by its index in that slice (keep the order fixed, e.g. `[&mut flippers, &mut mechs]`), a body by its index in the set. **Rationale:** the owner (2.3/2.4) keeps its state and its integration and can read/drive it between steps (coil torques, EOS switches, render poses) without downcasting from the solver; nothing is stored across the borrow. **Alternatives:** `Box<dyn …>` registered in `BallSolver` (owner loses direct access, needs `Any` downcasts); a field in `Geometry` (struct literals in the 2.2 tests and other crates would break, and `Geometry` is `Copy`/shared).
- **The trait** (all `time` = seconds since the start of the current tick, in `[0, dt]`; `body` in `0..body_count()`):
  ```rust
  pub trait MovingBodies {
      fn body_count(&self) -> usize;
      fn begin_tick(&mut self, dt: f32);                 // predict [0, dt]
      fn end_tick(&mut self, dt: f32);                   // commit pose/velocity at dt
      fn layer_mask(&self, body: usize) -> u8;           // world::layers bits
      fn material(&self, body: usize) -> Material;       // pair table key
      fn swept_bounds(&self, body: usize) -> (Vec3, Vec3);   // AABB for the rest of the tick
      fn max_surface_speed(&self, body: usize) -> f32;       // bound, rest of the tick
      fn closest_point(&self, body: usize, time: f32, point: Vec3) -> Option<BodyPoint>;
      fn velocity_at(&self, body: usize, time: f32, point: Vec3) -> Vec3;
      fn inverse_mass_at(&self, body: usize, time: f32, point: Vec3, direction: Vec3) -> f32;
      fn apply_impulse(&mut self, body: usize, time: f32, point: Vec3, impulse: Vec3);
      fn cast_sphere(&self, body: usize, time: f32, center: Vec3, radius: f32,
                     velocity: Vec3, window: f32) -> Option<BodyCast> { conservative_cast(..) }
  }
  pub struct BodyPoint { pub point: Vec3, pub normal: Vec3 /* out of the body */, pub distance: f32 /* signed, <0 inside */ }
  pub enum BodyCast { Hit { time: f32 /* from cast start */, point: Vec3, normal: Vec3 }, Advance { time: f32 } }
  pub fn conservative_cast<B: MovingBodies + ?Sized>(set: &B, body: usize, time: f32, center: Vec3,
                                                     radius: f32, velocity: Vec3, window: f32) -> Option<BodyCast>;
  pub const BODY_SKIN: f32 = 1.0e-5;          // m, contact gap of a cast hit
  pub const BODY_REST_SPEED: f32 = 1.0e-3;    // m/s, slower = resting contact
  pub const MAX_CAST_ITERATIONS: u32 = 64;
  ImpactTarget::Body { set: usize, body: usize }   // new variant
  ```
- **Call order per step (the contract).** (1) `begin_tick(dt)` on every set, slice order, before anything else (even with no balls); the body predicts its trajectory over `[0, dt]` from its committed state and this tick's drive, keeping obligation 3 below (speculative stops): `ω₁ = ω₀ + α·dt`, then `ω₁ ← min(ω₁, (θ_stop − θ₀)/dt)` toward a stop, then `θ(t) = θ₀ + ω₁·t`; position must be continuous in `t`. (2) Newton chains, then the balls' event loop, which makes any number of `&self` queries and `apply_impulse` calls. (3) `end_tick(dt)` on every set, slice order, after the last ball event; then sensors. A non-finite or non-positive `dt` calls nothing.
- **Impulses and re-prediction.** `apply_impulse(body, time, point, J)`: the body keeps `[0, time]` and re-predicts `[time, dt]` from its pose at `time` with velocity changed by `M⁻¹J` (1-DOF rotor about unit axis `a` through pivot `c`: `Δω = ((point − c) × J)·a / I`). Impulses arrive in non-decreasing `time`; after an impulse at `time` **no query is made for an earlier time** (the solver brings every ball to `time` and re-casts them). Several impulses may arrive at one `time` (accumulated-impulse solve), including negative ones; they add. A body reporting zero inverse mass for a push must not move because of it (it is still told).
- **Owner obligations (binding on 2.3/2.4; fix round 1 made it three, see "2.2-S — fix round 1").** (3) Speculative stops: never predict more travel toward a stop than the stop allows within the tick (at `begin_tick` and after each impulse). (1) `inverse_mass_at(..., direction)` takes the direction of the impulse the *body* would receive (the solver asks with `−n` when the normal impulse grows, `+n` when it is taken back, `n` from body to ball, and along the slip/friction change). "Engaged" = at the stop and not moving away. On an engaged end stop it is 0 for a push into the stop and the free value away from it. `velocity_at` must be the real velocity: 0 on an engaged stop, **also at the very instant an impulse drove it into the stop** (otherwise the contact solve keeps taking back impulses and the ball stalls until the event budget runs out; this was found and fixed in the reference rotor).
- **How the solver uses a body.** Casts: between sub-step boundaries a ball's next impact with a body comes from `cast_sphere` together with the static/guide/chain/ball casts (earliest event first); bodies are cast even for a ball at rest; a frozen ball (impact budget exhausted in a sub-step) is not cast and is corrected at its next boundary. Sub-steps: a ball whose path (padded 2r) meets a body's `swept_bounds` adds `max_surface_speed·dt` to its travel per tick. Contacts: at each boundary every body within 20 µm (by `closest_point`) joins the ball's contact list as a surface contact and is solved by the same projected Gauss–Seidel accumulated-impulse solve as static, guide, rail, ball and captive-ball contacts; penetration is projected out by moving the ball only. Body contacts are not warm-started. Events: an impact (not a resting contact) at ≥ `IMPACT_REPORT_SPEED` is `BallEvent::Impact { ball, other: ImpactTarget::Body { set, body }, normal_speed }` with the *relative* normal speed. Switches are the owner's (it sees every impulse). `SurfaceVelocity` is not consulted for bodies.
- **The two-body impulse.** `w_n = inverse_mass_at(−n)`, `w_t` along the slip. Normal: `J = (u_target − u_n)/(1/m + w_n)` on `u_n = (v_ball − v_body(point))·n`, restitution from the pair table (ball material, `material(body)`) with the 2.2 ramp; `−J·n` to the body. Rolling resistance acts on the ball's spin only. Friction: Coulomb on `v + ω×r_c − v_body(point)` (body velocity re-queried after the normal impulse) with effective mass `1/(7/(2m) + w_t)`, clamped to `μ·J`; its reaction goes to the body. When `w_n = w_t = 0` the contact is solved exactly as 2.2's static surface moving with `v_body` (including static rest), so a kinematic body behaves like geometry. Verified: free ball on a free-pivot rod matches `J = (1+e)v/(1/m + d²/I)` and angular momentum within 0.5 % (fixed cases and a proptest over d, v, I and all profiles).
- **Default CCD (`conservative_cast`).** Steps by `gap / (|v_ball| + max_surface_speed)`. At `s = 0`, within `BODY_SKIN`, it is a hit only if closing faster than `BODY_REST_SPEED`, otherwise `None` (resting contact, left to the boundaries); later, the first in-skin closing point is a hit; grazing creeps by `BODY_SKIN/bound`; after 64 steps it returns `Advance` (the solver moves the ball and casts again; this counts toward the ball's 12 events per sub-step). No tunnelling as long as `closest_point` is exact and `max_surface_speed` is a true bound. Owners may override `cast_sphere` with an exact cast (e.g. the 2.3 brief's local-frame advancement); it must not report a touching, non-closing sphere at `s = 0`.
- **Guarantees.** No allocation in the solver path (owners must not allocate either); deterministic order (sets, then bodies by index, balls by handle); with an empty slice, bit-identical to HEAD 84c88eb (recorded digests of five scenarios, `tests/ball_seam_bitident.rs`). With a finite-mass body pushed, every ball is brought to that instant, so a ball far from the body may take a slightly different floating-point path than without bodies.
- **Reference bodies (tests only, `tests/ball_seam_ref.rs`):** `Slab` (kinematic translating boxes) and `Rotors` (1-DOF capsules with inertia, drive torque, inelastic stops). 2.3/2.4 may copy them. Proven in `tests/ball_seam_bodies.rs`: no tunnelling (plate and rotor sweeps, ball speeds up to 100 m/s, body speeds up to 25 m/s, plus proptests), two-body exchange, no energy gain at a kinematic body at rest (proptest, all materials), stable rest on a registered kinematic floor, a ball rolling down a held bat and staying cradled for 2 s, a flip launching a cradled ball while the ball slows the bat, call order, layer masks, set/body indices in events, conveyor drag, 10-run bit stability; allocation guard with bodies and hooks in `tests/ball_seam_alloc.rs`.
- **Performance (release, this container, noisy):** 2 rotors + kicker + 4 balls: mean 6.2 µs, p99 31 µs per step (target ≤ 50 µs mean); the same table without bodies 2.9 µs. No-body `ball_perf` probes before/after within noise (4 balls / 300 colliders 3.05–4.8 µs HEAD vs 3.07–3.18 µs now over three alternating runs).

### (B) Force context

- **Decision (extend the trait with defaulted methods; keep `force`).**
  ```rust
  pub trait ExternalForce: Send {
      fn force(&mut self, handle: BallHandle, ball: &Ball, profile: &BallProfile, dt: f32) -> Vec3 { ZERO }
      fn force_in(&mut self, ctx: &ForceContext<'_>, handle: BallHandle, ball: &Ball,
                  profile: &BallProfile, dt: f32) -> Vec3 { self.force(handle, ball, profile, dt) }
      fn ball_added(&mut self, handle: BallHandle, ball: &Ball) {}
      fn ball_removed(&mut self, handle: BallHandle) {}
  }
  pub struct ForceContext<'a> {
      pub magnets: &'a [MagnetState], // index = MagnetId
      pub step_index: u64,            // steps completed before this one
      pub tick_dt: f32,               // dt of this step
      pub tick_time: f32,             // substep · h, seconds into the tick
      pub sim_time: f64,              // Σ dt of completed steps + tick_time
      pub substep: u32, pub substeps: u32,   // h = tick_dt / substeps = the `dt` argument
  }
  pub struct MagnetState { pub magnet: Magnet, pub power: f32, pub remanence_tau_s: f32 }
  impl MagnetState {
      pub fn in_range(&self, position: Vec3, layer: u8) -> bool; // layer bit, 0 < d ≤ range (power ignored)
      pub fn pull(&self, position: Vec3) -> Vec3;   // K·d̂/max(d³, d_min³) toward the pole, 0 outside range
  }
  BallSolverConfig::magnet_remanence_tau_s: f32       // from_defaults: magnet_remanence_tau_ms / 1000 (0.060)
  BallSolver::set_magnet_remanence_tau(id: MagnetId, tau_s: f32)  // NaN/negative/unknown ignored
  BallSolver::magnets(&self) -> &[MagnetState]
  BallSolver::step_count(&self) -> u64;  BallSolver::sim_time(&self) -> f64
  ```
  The solver calls `force_in` once per ball sub-step, in handle order within a boundary time, after gravity and the magnets, and adds `F·h/m`. The solver's magnet force is `c·P·pull`; the §3.2 residual force is `c·ρ·M·pull`. **Rationale:** existing 2.2 hooks and tests compile unchanged; a new hook implements only `force_in`. The magnet τ is stored per magnet in the solver (not in `Magnet`, whose struct literals the 2.2 tests use). **Alternatives:** a new trait (two hook lists); passing the magnets as a fourth argument (breaks every implementation).
- **Decision (ball lifetime).** `add_ball` calls `ball_added` on every hook; `remove_ball` calls `ball_removed` (only when a ball was there); `add_external_force` calls `ball_added` for every ball already present (ascending handles). A handle is reused by the next `add_ball` after a removal, so per-ball state keyed by handle must be reset in `ball_added` (`Dispenser::dispense` goes through `add_ball`). This meets 2.7 deliverable 2 ("per-ball magnetisation state") without a `pin_core` change: the ball arena is in `pin_physics`.
- **Decision (an exactly-zero total force is not added).** `if any && force.is_finite() && force != Vec3::ZERO`. **Rationale:** adding `0·h/m` can turn a `−0.0` velocity component into `+0.0`, so a hook with zero coefficients would not be bit-identical to no hook (2.7 verification 1/3 need that). The five HEAD digests (including one with an old-style hook) are unchanged by this. Proven by a prototype remanence hook in `tests/ball_seam_force.rs`: ρ = 0 is bit-identical to no hook, ρ = 0.5 leaves the magnet measurably later.

### Edits outside the new files

- `tests/spec_ball_determinism.rs`: one mechanical line, `ImpactTarget::Body { set, body } => (5u8, set, body).hash(h),` in its exhaustive match. No assertion changed.

## Orchestrator — 4.3 review round 1: VFS defect O1 assigned to the 4.3 fix round

## Orchestrator — 2.7 starts during the 2.2-S fix round
**Decision:** 2.7 (Magnus and remanence) starts now instead of waiting for 2.2-S to pass. The 2.2-S reviewer passed the force-context half (N5). Both blocking findings concern the moving-body half, which 2.7 does not use, and the fix round keeps every signature. 2.3 and 2.4 still wait for the 2.2-S pass. 2.7 shares `target/sp_2_2S` with the fix round to save disk.

## 3.4 — fix round 1 (review B1, N1–N6)

- **B1 (rumble mapper after a tick goes backwards).** **Decision:** slot reuse is judged by the *event's own tick* (`t₀ + d ≤ e.tick`), not by the largest tick ever seen; any tick (fed or queried) more than `RESTART_THRESHOLD_TICKS` = 1000 (1 s) behind the latest tick seen restarts the timeline (all envelopes dropped, latest tick := that tick), which also recovers from a bogus far-future tick such as `u64::MAX`; `reset()` now clears the latest tick too. **Rationale:** a new sim session restarts at tick 0, but the main thread may legitimately query a few ms ahead of late-arriving events, so small steps back must not wipe running envelopes; 1 s is far beyond any main-thread lag and far below a session. **Alternatives:** restart on any `e.tick < now` (would drop envelopes whenever an event arrives after a slightly-ahead `output_at`); only fixing `reset()` (callers would have to know about sessions).
- **N6.** An envelope whose end overflows `u64` is never expired (checked add instead of saturating), so `active()` counts it while it sounds.
- **N1 (SSF phase wrap).** The impulse table increment is clamped to Nyquist (half a table per frame), so the wrap is one subtraction; the roll wrap uses a remainder (a voice's increment may predate a shorter loop from `set_roll_loop`). Absurd frequencies (1e9 Hz … ∞) now render in constant time per frame.
- **N2 (correction to "3.4 — Haptics bus…", telemetry encoder).** `MAX_DATAGRAMS_PER_TICK` is **5**, not 6: the worst tick is 832 state entries + 64 impulses = 896 entries = 5 datagrams. The const comment and TELEMETRY_PROTOCOL.md §4.5 are corrected.
- **N3.** TELEMETRY_PROTOCOL.md §6: listeners ignore datagrams below the last full-state tick *unless the datagram is itself a full state* (a new session, §4.4).
- **N4/N5.** TELEMETRY_PROTOCOL.md §4.6–§4.7 and the `TelemetryTx::push_batch` / `TelemetrySender::spawn` docs now state the back-pressure loop the sim loop must wire (`push_batch` false → `force_full_state`) and that wire latency is up to the spawn poll interval; 6.0 should use `poll ≤ 1 ms`.

## 2.7 — Magnus effect and magnetic remanence

Module `pin_physics::ball_ext` (`magnus.rs`, `remanence.rs`), on the 2.2-S force-context seam only (no `ball/` or `pin_core` change).

- **Decision (API).** `Magnus` (unit struct, stateless) and `Remanence` implement `ExternalForce::force_in`. `register_all(&mut BallSolver) -> Remanence` registers `Magnus` first, then `Remanence` (forces summed in that order, after any earlier hook) and returns a clone of the registered `Remanence`. `magnetisation(&Remanence, BallHandle) -> f32` (also `Remanence::magnetisation`) keeps the brief's signature: the first parameter is any clone of the registered hook. Public helpers for tests/tools: `magnus_force`, `magnus_step_force`, `magnetisation_step`, `remanence_decay`, `residual_force`. **Rationale:** a hook boxed into the solver cannot be read back; the brief's signature still works because the state is shared by clones. **Alternatives:** a separate `RemanenceReader` type (changes the fixed signature); storing `M` in the solver (edits `ball/`, not owned).
- **Decision (shared state).** `M` per ball slot lives in `Arc<[AtomicU32; MAX_BALLS]>` (f32 bits, `Relaxed`), allocated once in `Remanence::new`. Reads and writes are lock-free and allocation-free; clones share it. Only one instance may be registered (two registered clones would each advance the same `M`); documented on the type. The per-ball "source magnet" index is private to each instance.
- **Decision (magnetisation law).** Per sub-step `h`: (1) the force uses `M` at the start of the sub-step; (2) if the ball is in range of any magnet (`MagnetState::in_range`: layer bit and `0 < d ≤ range`, power ignored), `M ← P + (M − P)·e^(−h/τ)` toward the strongest such magnet's power (lowest index on a tie), with that magnet's τ; otherwise `M ← M·e^(−h/τ)` with τ of the magnet that last held the ball, read live from the context. Clamped to [0, 1], NaN → 0; `τ = 0` is instant. A ball with `c = 0` is not magnetisable (`M` stays 0). `M` is reset to 0 on `ball_added` and `ball_removed`. **Rationale:** one first-order law with the magnet's τ for both rise and decay is deterministic, needs one `expf` per ball sub-step, and gives exactly `e^(−5) < 1 %` after 5τ; a coil switched off with the ball still on the pole decays by the same law (target 0). **Alternatives:** a per-ball-per-magnet `M` (the plan says each ball carries one `M`); a separate rise constant (no data to set it).
- **Decision (residual force acts whatever the coil power).** Every magnet whose range holds the ball pulls with `c·ρ·M·pull(position)`; the solver adds the coil's own `c·P·pull` separately, so with the coil on the ball feels `c·(P + ρM)`. **Rationale:** the remanent magnetisation is attracted by the pole iron whether the coil is on or not; superposition avoids a discontinuity at `P → 0`. With `ρ ≤ 0.05` (built-in profiles) the extra hold is ≤ 5 %. **Alternatives:** residual only while `P = 0` (step change in force at small P).
- **Decision (Magnus integration).** `F = m (R v − v)/h`, where `R` rotates `v` about `ω` by `φ = S_m|ω|h/m`: the exact solution of `m dv/dt = S_m (ω × v)` over the sub-step with `ω` held. `cos φ − 1` is computed as `−2 sin²(φ/2)` (libm `sinf`). To first order it is `S_m (ω × v)` (lateral acceleration within 1 %: relative error ≤ φ, ≈ 4e-4 for a Glo-Ball, S_m = 1e-5, at 1000 rad/s), and `|v|` is unchanged up to rounding. **Rationale:** the explicit force adds `(F h/m)²` to `|v|²` every sub-step (`φ²` ≈ 4e-6 relative per tick at S_m = 5e-5, 1000 rad/s), which is real energy gain; the rotation has none. **Kinetic-energy bound (verification 2):** free flight, no gravity: `||v_end| − |v_0|| ≤ 4 ε` per tick (tested at 200 ticks and in a proptest); with gravity and a vertical spin axis the translational KE equals the run without Magnus within 1e-6 relative over 0.5 s (the vertical component is bit-identical). **Alternatives:** the explicit force (energy creep at high spin).
- **Decision (exact "no force").** A hook with nothing to add returns `-0.0` in every component (`Vec3::splat(-0.0)`), the identity of IEEE addition, so even the solver's sum `coil + hook` keeps every bit (`+0.0` would turn a `-0.0` coil component into `+0.0`). With `magnus_coeff = 0`, `ρ = 0` or `c = 0` the hooks are bit-identical to no hooks (tested on three busy 2.2 scenarios with zeroed profiles and on the `low_rem_steel` release).
- **Deliverable 3.** `low_rem_steel` is already accepted by `pin_assets` validation (profile names resolve against `pin_core::profiles::BUILTIN_PROFILE_NAMES`, which lists it; the guide lint uses `builtin_profile`). No `pin_assets` change.
- **Measured (implementer test).** Held 300 ms on a full-power coil, then the coil off and a 0.5 m/s push: `low_rem_steel` leaves the 80 mm range at tick 177, `standard_steel` at tick 238 (61 ms later); `low_rem_steel` with the hooks is bit-identical to no hooks.

## 5.1 — PBR pipeline and pinball materials (Implementer)

Module `pin_render::pbr` (CPU part GPU-free; `PbrRenderer` always compiled, its tests behind `gpu-tests`).

- **Decision (frames and units).** World = table frame, z up, metres (as `playfield.glb` from 4.3). View space right-handed, camera looks down −Z (`pbr::math::look_at`), wgpu clip depth 0..1, column-major `[[f32; 4]; 4]` (`m[col][row]`). A `PointLight` of intensity I gives irradiance `I·colour/d²`, windowed to 0 at `range` by `(1 − (d/range)⁴)²`; a white Lambert surface reflects at most `I/(π d²)`. **Rationale:** matches the physics/asset frame; a hard window makes conservative clustering exact (no light leaks past its sphere). **Alternatives:** y-up render frame (would need a basis change for every asset).
- **Decision (one shading model, material = parameter table).** `MaterialParams` (64 B GPU layout): base colour, emissive, metallic, roughness, `specular` (dielectric F0 = 0.04·specular; 0 = pure Lambert, F90 derived from F0), clear coat + roughness (F0 0.04, Kelemen visibility, attenuates the base by 1 − Fc), `transmission` (blended pass, premultiplied, diffuse scaled by 1 − T, alpha = 1 − T(1 − F)), `subsurface` (wrapped diffuse), `gi_emissive` (adds gi.rgb·gi.a·k: `pbr_uv_emissive`), `lamp_emissive` (adds base·k·per-draw lamp level: `pbr_light_insert`). `MaterialKind::params()` holds the table for the six materials; the shader never branches on a material. **Rationale:** rule 6 (no named special cases); 5.2/5.4 extend one model. **Alternatives:** one pipeline per material.
- **Decision (warm-up).** First-order response, exact discretisation `L ← C + (L − C)·e^(−dt/τ)` (libm `expf`), τ = 4.5 ms incandescent (0.892 at 10 ms, 0.964 at 15 ms), 0.25 ms LED (0.982 at 1 ms); same τ for cool-down. `LampBank` advances all 256 lamps from snapshot ticks (1 tick = 1 ms); first call only records the tick, a tick going backwards snaps to the commanded levels. **Alternatives:** asymmetric filament cool-down (no data; brief asks for first order).
- **Decision (clustering).** 16 × 9 tiles × 24 exponential depth slices (`DEFAULT_CLUSTER_DIMS`), CPU assignment each frame by sphere vs. cluster view-space AABB, sphere grown by 0.1 % + 0.1 mm against CPU/GPU rounding; the shader computes its cluster from `frag_coord` and view depth with the same formulas (`ClusterGrid::cluster_of`). Lights past 128 are dropped and counted (`LightList::rejected`, `ClusterAssignment::ignored`, `PbrStats::lights_rejected`), never a panic. Lights are a 4 KiB uniform array; cluster ranges/indices are read-only storage buffers. **Alternatives:** GPU compute clustering (more code, harder to test GPU-free).
- **Decision (HDR target and API).** `PbrRenderer::new(&GpuContext, w, h)` owns an `Rgba16Float` colour target (`hdr_texture`/`hdr_view`, usages RENDER_ATTACHMENT | TEXTURE_BINDING | COPY_SRC, for 5.2) and `Depth32Float`; `encode(&mut encoder, &FrameDesc)` or `render_to_image(&ctx, &FrameDesc) -> HdrImage` (linear f32 RGBA readback). Output clamped to 60 000 (f16 range) with a NaN guard. Placeholder environment `Environment {sky, horizon, ground}` (z-up gradient) gives the ambient and mirror terms until 5.2's probes. Meshes are uploaded from plain slices (`upload_mesh_data(positions, normals, indices)`) so `pin_render` does not depend on `pin_assets`; the caller maps glTF materials with `MaterialKind::from_shader_name` + `MaterialParams::from_gltf_factors`. **Alternatives:** a `pin_assets` dependency (pulls pin_physics/gltf/image into the renderer).
- **Decision (image tests, D30).** `tests/gpu_pbr_render.rs`: analytic checks first (per-pixel Lambert vs. the analytic radiance within 3 %, bound `I/(π d²)`; 120 clustered lights on a floor vs. the analytic sum at every pixel; chrome peak at the computed mirror pixel; UV glow follows the tint, dark with tint 0; clear-coat peak vs. bare wood; insert emission adds exactly base·k; acrylic shows the red card behind it), then PNG snapshots `tests/fixtures/pbr/*.png` (tolerance: mean |Δ| ≤ 3/255, ≤ 2 % of pixels off by > 16/255; `PIN_UPDATE_SNAPSHOTS=1` rewrites). The light-insert snapshot uses lamp level 0.08 (full level saturates 8-bit).
- **Decision (naga).** `naga =30.0.1` (the version wgpu 30.0.1 resolves) as a dev-dependency with wgsl-in/spv-out/msl-out/hlsl-out; `tests/pbr_naga.rs` validates (all flags, no capabilities) and translates both modules (`pbr_brdf`, `pbr_forward` = brdf + forward) via `shader_sources()`.
- **1.4 follow-ups.** N-a: `try_configure` records each failed (re)configuration in a per-surface `ConfigureLog` (count + first error logged once to stderr); `SurfaceSet::configure_failures()` / `configure_failures_by_surface()`. N-c: the debug renderer also draws the two end points of lines shorter than `JOINT_POINT_MAX_PX` = 3 px as 1-px points (longer lines unchanged), closing joint gaps of small circles (sweep test fails without it on lavapipe). N5: every device wait is bounded (`gpu::wait_for_device`; teardown under the lifecycle lock 10 s with a logged timeout, readback 60 s → `GpuError::Readback`, configure retries 2 s).

## 4.3 — fix round 1 (review B1, B2, N1–N6, O1)

- **B1, N1, N2 (lane rails use the loader's own check).** New public `pin_assets::validate::check_spline(index, &SplineDef) -> (errors, warnings)` runs exactly the `load_table` spline check (construction, 10 m / 65 m bounds, 4096 points, spacing and guide lints; lint (c) skipped: it needs the table's ball profiles and applies to sloped guides only). The `Checker` gained an optional bundle (`None` for a lone spline); no behaviour of `load_table` changed. `pin_assets` is now a normal dependency of `pin_tablegen`. Every generated rail must pass with no error **and no warning**, else its lane is refused (`lanes[i]: the left rail would not load cleanly: <loader message>`). A rail polyline over 65 m is refused before it is built. Hairline: lane ends are refused below 1.01 mm (the loader measures 1 mm in f32 metres). **Alternative:** a separate plan-view intersection test in pin_tablegen (would drift from the loader).
- **B2 (glTF budgets).** Each node added checks the running mesh/node count, vertices, indices and BIN bytes against `pin_assets::GltfLimits::default()`; the first element over a budget is named (`posts[16383]: the layout needs 16385 nodes, more than the 16384 …`). After writing, the JSON chunk is checked against `max_json_bytes` (named after the element kind with the most nodes), and the merged `table.toml` against `validate::MAX_MANIFEST_BYTES` (named `lanes`). `MAX_SPLINE_POINTS` / `MAX_SPLINE_LENGTH_M` are now public in `pin_assets::validate` and used rather than copied.
- **N3.** Module docs and AUTHORING say wireform tubes (1.5 mm) are centred on the zero-thickness physics rails; the 0.02 mm claim is limited to colliders and guide ribbons.
- **N4.** The CLI refuses `--table` = `<out>/table.toml` unless that file starts with the generator's header (an in-place re-run of the tool's own output, which loses nothing: the Tester's spec requires that re-run) or `--force` is given.
- **N5.** `libm::hypot` for the lane end gap. **N6.** AUTHORING: the tool validates only what it generates and the base splines ramps reference; run `load_table` on the output.
- **O1 (VFS, 4.1).** `normalize_path` now rejects a drive letter that only appears after `.` components are dropped (`.\a:` → `a:`), so every accepted name normalises to itself and `list()`/`exists()`/`read()` agree; folder bundles list only names that normalise to themselves (`a:` or `x\y` file names on Unix are not listed). The spec_fuzz regression seed is kept.

## 2.2-S — fix round 1 (review B1, B2, N1–N4, N6, N7)

Signatures are unchanged; this round tightens the contract and the solver behind it. The "2.2-S — moving-body and force-context seams" entry above remains the API reference; where it differs, this entry wins (it replaces that entry's "recommended" prediction scheme and its two-obligation "Stops and one-sided constraints" bullet).

- **B1 — Decision (inverse mass along the impulse change the body actually receives).** `solve_body_contact` asks `inverse_mass_at` with `−n` when the normal impulse grows (`u_target − u_n ≥ 0`) and with `+n` when the accumulated solve takes some back; the kinematic shortcut (`w_n = w_t = 0`) uses the same rule; friction asks along the slip and, when the clamp turns the friction change around, again along the change the body receives. **Rationale:** a stop-engaged rotor reported 0 for `−n` but moved freely when impulse was taken back, so each sweep multiplied the error by `−w·m` (> 1 beyond ~35 mm on a 1e-4 kg·m² bat) and the ball went to 1e37 m/s. **Alternatives:** symmetric mass (wrong at stops); forbidding take-back for bodies (breaks resting contacts).
- **B1 — Decision (divergence guard).** After every two-body normal impulse (and after a "kinematic" solve whose body nevertheless moved), the solver re-queries `velocity_at`; if the relative normal velocity overshot the target by more than before (> 1e-6 m/s), it corrects the impulse with the measured response (secant: `ΔJ = residual / k_obs`) or, if the response is unusable, takes the impulse back. At most one extra query and impulse per solve call. **Rationale:** an owner that under-reports its mass must not be able to drive the sim thread to infinity. Proven by `tests/ball_seam_regress.rs` with the guard alone (the old `−n` query) and with the direction fix alone, both bounded; neither alone is relied on.
- **B1 — "engaged" defined:** at the stop and not moving away from it.
- **B2 — Decision (owner obligation 3: speculative stops).** An owner never predicts more travel toward a stop than the stop allows within the tick: at `begin_tick` and on re-prediction after an impulse at `time`, the velocity toward a stop is limited to `(stop − pose)/(dt − time)`. Recommended prediction: `ω₁ = ω₀ + α·dt`, then `ω₁ ← min(ω₁, (θ_stop − θ₀)/dt)` toward a stop, then `θ(t) = θ₀ + ω₁·t`. **Rationale:** predicting the full `α·dt` and clamping the position reported a surface speed at `t = 0` the stop cancels microseconds later; a cradled ball at the default `FlipperConfig` numbers (I = 1e-4) was hit by it every tick (0.33–1.8 mm gaps, thrown off at 5 N·m). With the obligation the ball rests at ~1e-7 m/s for hold torques 0.15, 0.5, 1.2 and 5 N·m. Implemented in the reference `Rotors` (`RotorBody::stop_clamped`, switchable by `speculative_stops` for tests).
- **The three owner obligations (binding on 2.3/2.4):** (1) one-sided `inverse_mass_at` for the direction of the impulse the body receives, queried with `±n` and along the friction change, 0 into an engaged stop; (2) `velocity_at` is the real velocity, 0 on an engaged stop including the instant an impulse drove it there, the value *after* any velocity jump; (3) speculative stops as above.
- **N1 — Decision (stall early-out).** In the boundary solve and the mid-sub-step re-solve, a ball whose touching contacts are all static surfaces, other free balls and kinematic bodies (at least one body; zero inverse mass both ways along its normal) and whose change has not halved between pass 4 and pass 8 stops iterating at pass 8 (of 64); the next boundary projects the penetration out. Lists without a body never qualify, so the no-body solver is unchanged (digests still match). Kicker pinch, release: 1 ball against a wall 2 mm behind at 10–20 m/s worst 1.9 ms → 0.25–0.29 ms per step; 3 balls worst 5.3–5.6 ms → 0.84–0.88 ms (the rest is the 12-events-per-sub-step budget and casts). One later run showed a single 13 ms tick in the 3-ball, 0-gap case with the mean unchanged; this was taken as host noise (other agents building) and left as an open item for the 2.4 review. **Alternatives:** STALL_PASSES = 4 (only ~25 % less on the worst tick, less accurate); lowering the per-sub-step event cap (changes 2.2 behaviour).
- **N2–N4, N7 — docs.** In `ball::body` rustdoc ("Owner obligations", "Notes for owners"): pinned or cradled balls often use up their 12 events per sub-step and wait for the next boundary (owners see several small impulses or none in that window); a one-shot impact's friction reaction can change the body's normal velocity, so post-impact normal speed is not exactly `e·approach`; front-loading `α·dt` adds the drive's work at the start of the tick (at most `τ·Δθ` per tick); `velocity_at` returns the value after a jump; `inverse_mass_at` may be asked with `±n`.
- **N6.** The "Correction / review polish (4.6 N1–N6)" bullet that had landed under the 2.2-S heading was moved, unchanged, to the end of "4.6 — Placeholder asset kit".
- **Tests added:** `tests/ball_seam_regress.rs`: the Reviewer's B1 and B2 repros; a B1 sweep (landing point, speed, flip timing, torque 1.2/3 N·m, I 1e-4/2.5e-4, with and without obligation 3); squeezes by two limited rotors and by a rotor against a wall; a proptest of a ball on a stop-engaged rotor (I 5e-5–5e-4); the cradle at hold 0.15/0.5/1.2/5 N·m; a unit test of the speculative-stop prediction. `tests/ball_seam_perf.rs` gained the kicker-pinch timing report.

## Orchestrator — 2.3 and 2.4 start during the 2.2-S fix round
**Decision:** 2.3 and 2.4 start now. The 2.2-S fix round keeps every signature. Its B1 fix is inside the solver, and its B2 fix is an owner obligation, which is written into both briefs. Disk space (18 GB free after 3.4 finished) allows separate target dirs `sp_2_3` and `sp_2_4`. The two subphases adapt to the fix-round docs when they land and report gaps instead of editing `ball/`.
**Rationale:** Waiting would leave the machine idle on the wave's critical path. The only risk is rework if the fix round changes behaviour, and both reviewers will check against the final seam.

## 2.7 — Review polish (N1, N3–N5)

- **N1 (decay τ with overlapping magnets).** On a power tie among in-range magnets, the magnet that last held the ball keeps it if it is still in range (before: lowest index). So an idle, never-powered magnet overlapping a holder no longer gives its τ to the decay after the holder switches off; the result no longer depends on magnet index order (regression test `overlapping_idle_magnet_does_not_take_over_the_decay_tau`, both orders bit-identical, M ≈ e^(−0.1/0.2) after 100 ms).
- **N3 (double registration).** The shared cells carry an `AtomicBool` claim. The first `Remanence` instance to receive a solver call (`ball_added` or `force_in`) claims it with one compare-exchange and records its role in a plain field; every other instance sharing the cells (a second registered clone, or a clone registered with another solver) is inert: no force, no change to `M`. Clones start unclaimed (manual `Clone`). Lock-free and allocation-free. No `debug_assert`: the inert behaviour is defined and tested (`a_second_registered_clone_is_inert`). Use a fresh `Remanence::new()` per solver.
- **N4 (correction to "Decision (exact "no force")").** The rationale given there is not true for the current seam: the solver's accumulator starts at `+0.0` and the seam skips an exactly-zero total, so a hook returning `+0.0` would also be bit-identical. The `-0.0` convention is harmless and kept, but not required.
- **N5.** The residual pull comes from every magnet whose range holds the ball, powered or not (pole-piece reading: the remanent ball is attracted by any pole iron in range), not only from the magnet that magnetised it.

## Orchestrator — stale builds in shared target dirs
**Observation:** When two agents share one `CARGO_TARGET_DIR` and one of them edits a source file while the other is building, cargo can keep a fingerprint from before the edit. A later test run then uses a stale library. It happened once in the 2.7 polish check: two new tests failed until the file was touched and rebuilt. When a result contradicts an agent's report, the Orchestrator re-runs after `touch`-ing the changed sources before treating it as a defect.

## Orchestrator — 2.4 sling and bumper exit windows: coil latency
**Decision:** The Appendix C kick windows (bumper and sling exit 1.5–4 m/s) are guaranteed for a coil fired on the tick after the switch edge. That is the engine's latency under PLAN §2.2: edges collected in tick N reach the script in tick N+1, whose coil commands apply in that tick. Exit speed must vary continuously with contact phase. A coil fired later than that is outside the guarantee.
**Rationale:** Real machines fire slings and bumpers from fast switch rules, and the engine's fixed tick order gives exactly one tick of latency.

## 5.1 — review polish

- **N1 (wrapped diffuse).** `wrapped_n_o_l` is now `max(cos + w, 0) / (1 + w)²`. Its integral over the sphere is π, the same as Lambert's `max(cos, 0)`, so a wrapped surface reflects the same total energy as a Lambert surface. The light only spreads past the terminator. Test: `wrapped_diffuse_reflects_no_more_than_lambert` puts a white wrap-0.6 sphere among 128 evenly spread lights; wrapped/Lambert is 1.004 at the centre and 1.002 on average (before the fix, about 1.6). The implementer's `light_insert.png` was re-blessed after the analytic checks passed. **Alternatives:** divide by `(1 + w)` (not energy-conserving).
- **N3 (frame inputs).** `PbrRenderer::encode` sanitises the GI tint, the environment radiances and the clear colour: NaN, ±inf and negative values become 0, and large finite values are kept. Test: `non_finite_frame_inputs_are_sanitized` (`gi = [1, 1, 1, inf]` renders exactly like intensity 0; NaN environment and NaN clear colour stay finite).
- **N6 (map wait).** `gpu::finish_map` runs the bounded poll and then `recv_timeout` on the remaining readback budget, with at least 100 ms. It is used by both `pbr::render_to_image` and `debug::read_texture`. A callback that never runs, or is dropped, now returns `GpuError::Readback`. Test: `gpu::tests::map_result_wait_is_bounded`.
- **N5 (configure back-off).** After a failed configuration, `try_configure` skips the next 1, 2, 4, … up to 64 unforced attempts. Skipped attempts return `false` immediately, without taking the submit lock. Success resets the back-off. Forced attempts (`try_configure_now`) ignore it: initial setup, pixel-size changes, and presenter hand-offs of a new size. Only real failures are counted. Test: `retries_back_off_exponentially_and_reset_on_success`.

## 2.3 — Flipper solver (standard, upper, zipper, Thing Flip)

API reference for 6.0 wiring and Subphase 2.6: rustdoc of `pin_physics::flipper` (module docs carry the per-tick sequence). Everything is in `crates/pin_physics/src/flipper/` (`mod.rs`: `FlipperSet`, `FlipperError`, `FlipperTuning`, `EosEdge`, `plane_axes`; `shape.rs`: `BatShape`, `BatMotion`, `BatPoint`, `StraightCapsule`).

- **Decision (per-tick sequence).** (1) inputs in any order: `set_energised(id, on)`, `set_zipper(id, closed)`, `on_switch_edge(switch, closed, tick)`; (2) `step_tick(tick)` runs the Thing Flip schedules for the tick about to be simulated; (3) `BallSolver::step_with_bodies(&geo, &mut [&mut flippers, &mut mechs], dt, &mut events)` (flippers are set 0; the solver calls `begin_tick`/`end_tick`), or `FlipperSet::advance(dt)` without a ball solver; (4) outputs: `eos_edges()` (EOS switch edges of this tick, to be delivered as switch edges), `angle_deg`, `angular_velocity`, `eos_closed`, `base_offset_mm`. Body index = position of the config in the slice given to `new`. **Rationale:** the seam fixes that all drive is predicted in `begin_tick`; the only thing left for a per-tick hook is the tick-numbered Thing Flip schedule. **Alternatives:** scheduling in `begin_tick` with an internal tick counter (not tick-exact against the sim's own tick number after a pause or reset).
- **Decision (bat = one-DOF rotor with speculative stops and deferred bounce).** Travel `q ∈ [0, |sweep|]`; per tick the drive acts as one angular impulse at the start (`ω ← ω + (τ_coil − τ_spring)/I · dt`, the seam's recommended scheme), then the tick is one constant-velocity segment per impulse. A segment that would pass a stop is replaced by one that reaches the stop exactly at the end of the tick (obligation 3) and remembers the momentum it arrived with; `end_tick` commits `ω = −stop_restitution × that`, unless the drive would press the bat back within a tick (then 0: a held bat is at rest, `angular_velocity == 0`). A push into a stop that holds the bat, or that the bat is running into within this tick, gets inverse mass 0 and is ignored by `apply_impulse` (obligations 1 and 2). **Rationale:** no phantom surface speed on a held bat (a cradled ball rests: 0.8 mm drift in 3 s), no energy from a stop, and the bounce exists (needed for the feel of a bat arriving at the end of its stroke). **Alternative tried and rejected:** constant acceleration within the tick (exact work `τ·Δθ`): the ball solver's contacts are velocity-level, so a bat that keeps accelerating into a ball it carries produces a new micro-impact every few microseconds, exhausts the 12 events per sub-step and loses the ball's push (cradle shots dropped below 1 m/s).
