- `struct EnvironmentReflections` (implements `ReflectionSource`): no probes, every draw uses the
  placeholder environment (the 5.1 look); `stats()` reports zero updates.

**`pin_render::pbr` hooks (5.2):**
- `struct ReflectionBinding<'a> { bind_group: &'a wgpu::BindGroup, draw_layers: &'a [u32] }` and
  `const NO_PROBE: u32 = u32::MAX`: `draw_layers[i]` is the atlas layer draw `i` samples for its mirror /
  ambient-specular term (`NO_PROBE` or a missing entry = placeholder environment).
- `PbrRenderer::reflection_layout() -> &wgpu::BindGroupLayout` (group 2: binding 0 `texture_2d_array<f32>`
  (filterable float), binding 1 filtering sampler; fragment stage).
- `PbrRenderer::encode_reflected(&mut self, encoder, frame: &FrameDesc, reflections: Option<&ReflectionBinding>)`
  (`encode` = `encode_reflected(.., None)`; `render_to_image` unchanged).
- `PbrRenderer::render_reflected_to_image(&mut self, ctx, frame, Option<&ReflectionBinding>) -> Result<HdrImage, GpuError>`.
- `PbrRenderer::encode_to_target(&mut self, encoder, frame, target: &ExternalTarget)` with
  `struct ExternalTarget<'a> { color: &'a wgpu::TextureView, depth: &'a wgpu::TextureView, width: u32, height: u32 }`
  (single-sample `HDR_FORMAT` colour + `DEPTH_FORMAT` depth; used for probe faces).
- `PbrRenderer::set_msaa_samples(&mut self, samples: u32) -> Result<(), GpuError>` (1 or 4; 4 renders into
  multisampled targets resolved into `hdr_texture`) and `msaa_samples() -> u32`. Default 1 (5.1 look).

**`pin_render::post`:**
- `struct PostConfig { exposure: f32, bloom: BloomConfig, tone_map: ToneMap, motion_blur: MotionBlurConfig, aa: AaMode }`
  (`Default`: exposure 1, bloom on, ACES, motion blur on, MSAA 4×). `PostConfig::neutral()`: exposure 1, bloom and motion blur off, `ToneMap::Linear`, AA off (the identity on 0..1). Also `fn luminance(rgb) -> f32` (Rec. 709), `MAX_BLUR_BALLS = 16`, `PostChain::{size, output_format, output_texture, output_view}`, `OctahedralProbes::{config, draw_layers}`, `ProbeScheduler::{budget, is_captured, is_stale, capture_position, last_updates}`, `reflections::TRACK_MAX_JUMP_M`, `shader_sources()` (post chain + probe remap, for naga).
- Errors: `PostChain::new` and `OctahedralProbes::new` build their shaders, pipelines and render targets inside a wgpu validation error scope and return `GpuError::InvalidArgument` instead of panicking in wgpu's default handler.
- `struct BloomConfig { enabled: bool, threshold: f32 (1.0), knee: f32 (0.5), intensity: f32 (0.15),
  radius: f32 (0.65), firefly_clamp: f32 (64.0), max_levels: u32 (6) }`. Model (energy conserving, in
  exposed radiance): `bright = bloom_prefilter(c)` (soft-knee threshold on luminance, then scaled so its
  luminance ≤ `firefly_clamp`); `out = c − intensity·bright + intensity·blur(bright)`, where `blur` is a
  mip chain (levels of ceil(n/2) texels; separable binomial [1 5 10 10 5 1]/32 down and its transpose up,
  on texel indices, levels mirrored at the image edge; level i weighted (1−radius)·radiusⁱ, last level radiusᴸ⁻¹;
  the weights sum to 1). So Σ luminance(out) = Σ luminance(in) at every position and image size, exactly at
  image edges that stay even through the bloom levels (otherwise up to ~±30 % of the scattered part of a
  pixel next to the edge: see "5.2 — fix round 1"), and only the above-threshold part
  moves; a uniform image is unchanged. `threshold = 0, knee = 0` blooms the whole image.
- `enum ToneMap { AcesFilm, Linear }`: `AcesFilm` = Narkowicz 2015 fit per channel
  `clamp(x(2.51x + 0.03) / (x(2.43x + 0.59) + 0.14), 0, 1)` with no implicit pre-scale (exposure is
  separate); `Linear` = clamp to 0..1. CPU reference: `fn aces_film(x: f32) -> f32`.
- `fn bloom_prefilter(rgb: [f32; 3], cfg: &BloomConfig) -> [f32; 3]` (CPU reference of the shader).
- `struct MotionBlurConfig { enabled: bool, shutter_s: f32 (default 1/120), samples: u32 (16),
  max_length_px: f32 (64) }`. `struct MotionBall { position: [f32; 3], velocity: [f32; 3], radius: f32 }`
  (from `pin_core::snapshot::BallState` via `MotionBall::from_state(&BallState, radius)`).
  `fn screen_motion(camera: &PbrCamera, ball: &MotionBall, shutter_s: f32, width: u32, height: u32) ->
  Option<ScreenMotion>` with `ScreenMotion { center_px: [f32; 2], radius_px: f32, displacement_px: [f32; 2] }`
  (displacement = projected motion over the shutter, clamped to `max_length_px` in the shader). Blur is
  per ball: a pixel shows the mean over the shutter of the ball (translated along the displacement) over
  the background; a still ball (zero velocity) leaves the image bit-identical.
- `enum AaMode { Off, Msaa4 }`: MSAA 4× in the PBR pass (`PbrRenderer::set_msaa_samples`), hardware
  resolve into the HDR target. Applied by `FrameRenderer`; a bare `PostChain` only records it.
- `struct PostFrame<'a> { camera: &'a PbrCamera, balls: &'a [MotionBall] }`.
- `struct PostChain`: `PostChain::new(ctx, width, height, output_format: wgpu::TextureFormat) -> Result<_, GpuError>`
  (output 8-bit RGBA/BGRA, sRGB or not: a non-sRGB format gets the sRGB curve in the shader);
  `config() -> &PostConfig`, `set_config(PostConfig)`, `resize(w, h)`, `stats() -> PostStats`;
  `encode(&mut self, encoder, input: &wgpu::TextureView, frame: &PostFrame, output: &wgpu::TextureView)`;
  `render_to_ldr(&mut self, ctx, input, frame) -> Result<LdrImage, GpuError>` (display image);
  `render_to_hdr(&mut self, ctx, input, frame) -> Result<HdrImage, GpuError>` (the linear composite
  before tone mapping: exposure · motion blur · bloom). Input is any `HDR_FORMAT` view of the chain's size.
- `struct HdrInput { texture, view }`, `HdrInput::from_image(ctx, &HdrImage) -> Result<HdrInput, GpuError>`
  (feed a CPU-made HDR image into the chain without the PBR pass).
- `struct LdrImage { width: u32, height: u32, pixels: Vec<[u8; 4]> }` (RGBA, row 0 top): `at(x, y)`,
  `region_mean(x0, y0, x1, y1) -> [f32; 4]` (0..255), `to_rgba8() -> Vec<u8>`.
- `struct PostStats { frames: u64, bloom_levels: u32, blurred_balls: u32, max_blur_px: f32 }`.
- **Whole frame** — `struct FrameRenderer` (PBR + reflections + post): `FrameRenderer::new(ctx, w, h,
  PostConfig) -> Result<_, GpuError>` (octahedral probes, MSAA per `config.aa`, output `Rgba8Unorm`);
  `with_reflections(ctx, w, h, PostConfig, make: impl FnOnce(&PbrRenderer) -> Result<Box<dyn ReflectionSource>, GpuError>)` (a closure, because a source binds the PBR renderer's `reflection_layout()`, which exists only once the renderer does); `set_config(PostConfig) -> Result<(), GpuError>` (also applies the AA mode), `resize(w, h)`; `pbr()` / `pbr_mut()` (upload meshes
  here), `post()` / `post_mut()`, `reflections()`; with `struct SceneBall { draw: usize, position: [f32; 3],
  velocity: [f32; 3], radius: f32 }`:
  `step(&mut self, ctx, frame: &FrameDesc, balls: &[SceneBall]) -> Result<ProbeStats, GpuError>` (probes +
  PBR + post, no readback), `render(&mut self, ctx, frame, balls) -> Result<RenderedFrame, GpuError>` with
  `RenderedFrame { ldr: LdrImage, hdr: HdrImage /* PBR output with reflections, before post */, probes: ProbeStats }`. `hdr` is raw: no firefly clamp (a roughness-0.06 chrome highlight reaches ~1.5·10⁴); anything that shows or encodes the frame goes through the post chain (`ldr`, or `PostChain::render_to_hdr`), `hdr` is for analytic radiance checks only.
  Test recipe: `let mut fr = FrameRenderer::new(&ctx, 128, 128, PostConfig::default())?; let ball =
  fr.pbr_mut().upload_mesh(&MeshGeometry::uv_sphere(r, 48, 32))?;` build `DrawItem`s (ball i at draw k_i),
  `FrameDesc::new(&cam, &lights, &draws)`, then `fr.render(&ctx, &frame, &balls)` per frame and read
  `.ldr`, `.hdr`, `.probes` (or `fr.reflections().stats()`).

## 4.4 — Menu UI public API

Published early for the Independent Tester; kept current by the 4.4 Implementer. Everything is in
`pin_app::ui` (re-exported at the module root) unless marked `pin_render::ui`.

- **State machine.** `MenuState::new(AppConfig) -> MenuState` (closed, `Panel::Tables`, no tables, one
  monitor, profile choices `["auto", <builtin ids>]`; the config is sanitized like `set_config`);
  `Default` = `new(AppConfig::default())`.
  Queries: `is_open()`, `panel() -> Panel`, `focus() -> usize` (0 = tab bar), `row_count()`,
  `focused_setting() -> Option<SettingRow>`, `focused_table() -> Option<usize>` (index into `tables()`),
  `tables() -> &[TableEntry]`, `visible_tables() -> &[usize]`, `filter()`, `config() -> &AppConfig`,
  `load_state() -> &TableLoadState`, `notice() -> Option<&str>`, `profile_choices()`, `display_count()`,
  `monitor()` / `monitor_mut() -> &mut InputMonitor`, `pending_commands() -> &[UiCommand]`.
  Mutators: `apply(MenuInput)`, `apply_all(iter)`, `open()`, `close()`, `set_tables(Vec<TableEntry>)`,
  `set_config(AppConfig)`, `set_profile_choices(Vec<String>)`, `set_display_count(u32)`,
  `drain_commands() -> Vec<UiCommand>`, `request_load(&Path) -> Result<(), LoadRefusal>`,
  `request_load_index(usize) -> Result<(), LoadRefusal>`, `report_load_result(Result<(), String>) -> bool`,
  `acknowledge_load()`, `route_cabinet(CabinetInput, pressed: bool) -> InputRoute`,
  `route_axis(CabinetAxis, f32) -> InputRoute`, `forwards_to_sim() -> bool`,
  `pending_load_id() -> Option<u64>`, `report_load_result_for(id: u64, Result<(), String>) -> bool`,
  `cancel_load() -> bool` (review round 1).
- **`MenuInput`**: `Toggle, Up, Down, Left, Right, Select, Back, ShowPanel(Panel), FocusTable(usize),
  LoadTable(usize), SetFilter(String), Set(SettingChange), Rescan`. Every input except `Toggle` is ignored
  while closed. Row 0 is the tab bar: `Left`/`Right`/`Select` there switch panels (wrapping
  Tables → Display → Input). `Up`/`Down` clamp (no wrap). Tables rows `1..` = visible tables; `Select`
  requests the load. Settings rows `1..` = `SettingRow::DISPLAY` / `SettingRow::INPUT`; `Left`/`Right`
  step (numeric rows snap to the step grid and clamp; choice rows cycle with wrap), `Select` cycles choice
  rows forward and toggles `Wireframe` (no-op on numeric rows). `Back` on a row → tab bar; `Back` on the
  tab bar closes the menu.
- **`UiCommand`**: `MenuOpened, MenuClosed, LoadTable(PathBuf), ConfigChanged(Box<AppConfig>), Rescan,
  ReleaseToSim { buttons: Vec<CabinetInput>, axes: Vec<CabinetAxis> }` (see Cabinet routing).
  `ConfigChanged` is emitted only when a value actually changed and is coalesced (at most one pending,
  the latest settings, moved to the end of the queue).
- **`Panel`**: `Tables, Display, Input` (`ALL`, `label`, `next`, `prev`).
- **`SettingRow`** (`DISPLAY`: DisplayMode, PlayfieldDisplay, BackglassDisplay, ScoreDisplayMonitor,
  BackglassPipCorner, BackglassPipWidth, ScorePipCorner, ScorePipWidth, CameraPreset, ScoreDisplay,
  DecodedAudioBudget (added for the decoded-audio ruling: range 64…8192 step 64 MiB);
  `INPUT`: InputProfile, StagingMode, DualStageLower, DualStageUpper, PlungerDeadzone,
  PlungerRestTracking, PlungerReleaseMinSpeed, PlungerReleaseWindow, NudgeHighPass, NudgeGain, NudgeCurve,
  NudgeDeadzone, NudgeMax, Wireframe) with `label`, `range() -> Option<NumericRange{min,max,step}>`,
  `number(&AppConfig)`, `value_text(&AppConfig)`. **`SettingChange`** has one variant per row
  (e.g. `CameraPreset(CameraPreset)`, `ScoreDisplay(ScoreDisplayKind)`, `PlungerDeadzone(f32)`,
  `PlungerReleaseWindow(u32)`, `BackglassDisplay(Option<u32>)`, `InputProfile(String)`, `Wireframe(bool)`,
  `DecodedAudioBudget(u32)`);
  values are clamped to the row range, non-finite → the default; an unknown profile id is refused (notice).
- **Lifecycle.** `TableLoadState::{Idle, LoadRequested(PathBuf), Loading(PathBuf), Loaded(PathBuf),
  Failed { path, error: String }}` (`is_busy()`). Request → `LoadRequested` + `UiCommand::LoadTable`;
  `drain_commands()` that returns the `LoadTable` → `Loading`; `report_load_result(Ok(()))` → `Loaded`,
  `config().last_table = Some(path)`, `ConfigChanged` + `MenuClosed` (menu closes);
  `report_load_result(Err(e))` → `Failed` (menu stays open). `report_load_result` returns `false` when
  nothing is pending. `Loaded`/`Failed` → `Idle` on `acknowledge_load()`, on any `apply` while open, on
  `open()`, or on a new request. A request while `LoadRequested`/`Loading` is **refused**:
  `LoadRefusal::Busy(pending path)`; also `LoadRefusal::Broken(error)` for a broken listed table and
  `NoSuchTable(i)`. Each accepted request gets a new id (`pending_load_id()`); `report_load_result_for(id, r)`
  ignores a stale id (returns `false`). `cancel_load()` abandons a pending/running load (→ `Idle`, the undrained
  `LoadTable` is dropped, notice "… cancelled"), so a loader that never answers cannot block the menu.
- **Cabinet routing.** `route_cabinet` / `route_axis` return `InputRoute::Sim` while closed and remember
  what the sim holds (buttons pressed, axes ≠ 0.0). When the menu opens, if anything is held, the command
  stream gets `UiCommand::ReleaseToSim { buttons, axes }` immediately **before** `MenuOpened` (buttons in
  `CabinetInput::ALL` order, axes in `CabinetAxis::ALL` order); the app sends the sim a release per button
  and 0.0 per axis, and the held set is cleared. A button still physically held when the menu closes
  reaches the sim on its next press. While open both return
  `InputRoute::Menu` (never forwarded) and a press applies `cabinet_menu_input(input)`: LeftFlipper → Up,
  RightFlipper → Down, LeftUpper → Left, RightUpper → Right, Start/Launch → Select, Action → Back,
  Tilt → none. Menu toggles: `MENU_TOGGLE_SCANCODE = 41` (Esc), `MENU_TOGGLE_BUTTON = GamepadButton::Guide`,
  `is_menu_toggle_key(u32)`, `is_menu_toggle_button(GamepadButton)`.
- **Scan.** `scan_tables(root: &Path) -> Vec<TableEntry>` (missing/unreadable root → empty).
  `TableEntry { path: PathBuf, file_name: String, status: TableStatus }` with `info()`, `error()`,
  `is_ready()`, `title()`, `sort_key()`; `TableStatus::{Ready(TableInfo), Broken(String)}`;
  `TableInfo { id, name, author, version, players_max, features: Vec<TableFeature>, preview:
  Option<PreviewArt>, preview_error: Option<String>, warning_count }`; `PreviewArt { file, width, height,
  rgba }`; `TableFeature` badges (`LuaRules, WasmRules, UpperFlippers, BananaFlippers, Ramps, Magnets,
  Multiball, Spinner, Vuk, Kickback, Diverter, NewtonBall, Backbox, LcdDisplay, DmdDisplay, Dof`, sorted,
  `label()`, `TableFeature::of(&LoadedTable)`); `inspect_bundle(&Path) -> TableStatus`;
  `decode_preview(&[u8]) -> Result<PreviewArt, String>`.
- **Config.** `AppConfig { display_mode: DisplayMode{Single,Multi}, playfield_display: u32,
  backglass_display: Option<u32>, score_display_monitor: Option<u32>, backglass_pip: PipConfig,
  score_pip: PipConfig { corner: PipCorner{TopLeft,TopRight,BottomLeft,BottomRight}, width_fraction: f32 },
  camera_preset: CameraPreset{Desktop,Cabinet,TopDown}, score_display: ScoreDisplayKind{Dmd,Lcd},
  input_profile: String ("auto" default), staging_mode: StagingMode{Table,Dedicated,DualStage},
  calibration: CalibrationConfig { plunger: PlungerCalibration{deadzone, rest_tracking_per_s,
  release_min_speed, release_window_ms: u32}, nudge: NudgeCalibration{high_pass_hz, gain, curve_exponent,
  deadzone, max}, dual_stage_lower, dual_stage_upper }, wireframe: bool, audio: AudioConfig {
  decoded_budget_mib: u32 /* default 512, 64..=8192; decoded_budget_bytes() */ }, tables_dir: Option<PathBuf>,
  last_table: Option<PathBuf>, extra: toml::Table }`. TOML keys = field names; enums snake_case
  (`"single"`, `"multi"`, `"top_right"`, `"dmd"`, `"lcd"`, `"desktop"`, `"cabinet"`, `"top_down"`,
  `"table"`, `"dedicated"`, `"dual_stage"`). `AppConfig::load(&Path) -> ConfigLoad { config, warnings:
  Vec<ConfigWarning> }` (never fails; missing file → defaults, no warning; a wrongly typed or
  unknown-variant known value is dropped alone → that setting's default + `InvalidValue { key: dotted path }`;
  only unparsable TOML / non-UTF-8 is `Corrupt` (message ≤ `config::MAX_WARNING_CHARS` = 512 chars + `…`);
  when anything was dropped/reset or the file was corrupt, `load` first copies the file to
  `<name>.bak` unless that already exists; a too-large file is not copied; a non-table value at a known
  table key, e.g. `[[calibration]]`, is a dropped value);
  `AppConfig::from_toml_str(&str) -> ConfigLoad`, `from_toml_bytes(&[u8]) -> ConfigLoad`,
  `to_toml_string() -> Result<String, toml::ser::Error>`, `save(&self, &Path) -> std::io::Result<()>`,
  `topology_mode() -> pin_render::topology::TopologyMode`, `sanitize(&mut Vec<ConfigWarning>)`.
  `ConfigWarning::{Unreadable(String), TooLarge(u64), Corrupt(String), InvalidValue{key, message},
  UnknownKey(String)}`. `MAX_CONFIG_BYTES = 1 MiB`, `CONFIG_FILE = "config.toml"`, `AUTO_PROFILE = "auto"`,
  `DEFAULT_DECODED_BUDGET_MIB = 512`, `MIN_DECODED_BUDGET_MIB = 64`, `MAX_DECODED_BUDGET_MIB = 8192`. An
  `[audio] decoded_budget_mib` that is out of range *or* unparsable (wrong type) resets alone to 512 with
  `InvalidValue { key: "audio.decoded_budget_mib" }`; a non-table `audio` resets with key `"audio"`.
- **View / painter.** `pin_app::ui::MenuView::{new, run_frame(&egui::Context, egui::RawInput, &mut MenuState)
  -> (egui::FullOutput, Vec<MenuInput>), show(&mut egui::Ui, &mut MenuState)}`. `pin_render::ui`: re-exports
  `egui`, `egui_wgpu`; `BACKDROP`, `menu_panel_rect(screen) / menu_panel_rect_px(size_px, ppp)`,
  `raw_input(size_px, ppp, events, time)`, `overlay_style(ctx)`, `show_overlay(ui, f)`,
  `OverlayFrame::new(ctx, FullOutput)`, `OverlayPainter::{new(device, format), paint(device, queue, encoder,
  view, size_px, frame)}`, `paint_offscreen(ctx, w, h, background, frame) -> Result<Vec<u8>, GpuError>`.
- **Live input.** `InputMonitor::new()`, `set_button(source: u8, CabinetInput, pressed)`,
  `is_pressed(CabinetInput)` (OR over sources), `pressed_mask() -> u8`, `clear_source(u8)`,
  `set_axis/axis`, `set_plunger(PlungerOutput, rest)`, `plunger() -> PlungerReading`, `set_nudge/nudge`.

## Orchestrator — Wave 5 restart: disk budget
**Observation:** the container restarted mid-batch-A; all eight role agents stopped, and the four target dirs had grown to 25 GB (2.5 alone 12 GB of debug test binaries), leaving 3.9 GB of the session's disk allowance. **Decision:** target dirs deleted; every cargo command now also sets `CARGO_INCREMENTAL=0` and `CARGO_PROFILE_{DEV,TEST}_DEBUG=line-tables-only` (COMMON.md ENVIRONMENT). The roles are relaunched with resume prompts and continue from the files in the working tree (WIP snapshot commits). No code change.

## 2.5 — script backend construction
**Decision:** `Simulation::load` builds the rules backend as a concrete `LuaScriptBackend::new(src, names, cfg.lua)` or `WasmScriptBackend::new(src, names, cfg.wasm)` chosen by the `meta.script_entry` extension (`.lua` / `.wasm`), with `SimConfig::default()` = `LuaConfig::engine()` / `WasmConfig::engine()` (100 000 instructions, 2 MiB). Any other extension is passed to `pin_script::load_backend_with(.., cfg.lua, cfg.wasm)` so the error text is the same as the library's. `load_backend()` is never used.
**Rationale:** `load_backend_with` returns `Box<dyn TableScriptBackend>`, which hides the fault state, fault count, last error and queue-overflow counter the brief requires the sim to expose (`script_faulted`, `script_fault_count`, `script_error`, `script_queue_overflows`). The concrete constructors are exactly what `load_backend_with` calls, with the same configs, so behaviour is identical.
**Alternatives:** an additive trait method in `pin_script` for these counters (outside owned paths; can be done later).

## 2.5 — tick order details, switch composition and test API semantics
**Decision:**
- Switch state each tick = (cabinet buttons | physics bits) overridden by switches forced with `Simulation::set_switch` (which lasts until the additive `release_switch`). Every change of the composed state is an edge (ascending switch number); every closing edge increments `switch_hits`.
- Cabinet edges produced in step 1 are delivered in step 2 of the same tick (step 1 precedes step 2 in §2.2). Physics edges (step 5) and `set_switch` edges are delivered in step 2 of the next tick, so an `on_switch_edge` in tick N sees physics edges from tick N−1. Within one delivery edges keep the order they happened in. Flippers also see every delivered edge (`FlipperSet::on_switch_edge`, Thing Flips).
- A forced cabinet switch also acts as its button for flipper staging.
- ~~`score(player)` uses the script's numbering: `pinball.set_score(1, …)` is `score(1)`; `score(0)` is `None` (the queue stores `player − 1`).~~ **Superseded by the Orchestrator ruling (Wave 5, 2.5):** `score(player)` is 0-based, the index `HostCommandQueue::scores` stores (pin_core is the contract): Lua `pinball.set_score(1, …)` is `score(0)`. `MAX_PLAYERS = 4`; `score(n)` for `n ≥ 4` is `None`, and queue reports for such players are ignored.
- Haptic events: one `HapticsTelemetryEvent { source = coil id, strength 1 }` per coil pulse, coil hold switched on and flipper energise rising edge, in a 32-entry per-tick buffer (`haptic_events()`); overflow is counted.
- Drains: a ball whose centre enters a `kind = "trough"` switch sphere (position + radius, centre lifted by 13.5 mm) on a matching layer is removed after physics (counted `balls_drained`); a ball outside the table's bounding box + 50 mm, or non-finite, is removed (`balls_escaped`). Ball handles carry a generation, so a stale handle returns `None` even if the slot is reused.
- Nudge axes: `NudgeX/NudgeY` deflection × 0.03 m/s per tick is applied to the balls through `MechSet::nudge` (also feeds the tilt bob). Plunger axis → `MechSet::set_plunger`.
- Script `spawn_ball(dispenser, profile)` places the ball at the dispenser exit with its exit velocity on layer 0; a full arena or unknown id is counted in `spawns_failed`.
**Rationale:** PLAN §2.2 order read literally; script numbering avoids an off-by-one between tests written against Lua and the host API.
**Alternatives:** delay cabinet edges one tick like physics edges (adds 1 ms of flipper latency for nothing).

## 2.5 — diverters, gates and Newton balls
**Decision:** mechs that 2.4 leaves inert are wired in `pin_sim` by glTF collider node names: for a diverter `<id>`, nodes named `<id>` or `<id>.on` are enabled only while the diverter is on and `<id>.off` only while it is off (`StaticWorld::set_enabled`, driven by `HostCommandQueue::diverters`; initially off). A gate's nodes `<id>` / `<id>.on` are solid while closed and disabled while its coil is pulsed (for `ms` ticks) or held; its switch (if any) reads "open". A `newton_ball` becomes a one-ball `NewtonChain` on a straight path of `length` mm (default 50) in the plan direction `angle_deg` (default 90°, up the table) starting at the mech position lifted by one ball radius; its switch is closed while the captive ball moves faster than 0.05 m/s.
**Rationale:** the schema has no collider references for mechs; node names are already unique and authored in the glb. 
**Alternatives:** an additive `collider` field on mech defs (pin_core CR) — deferred until a real table needs it.

## 2.5 — guide wall height
**Decision:** every `kind = "guide"` spline gets the playfield wall height from `layout.toml` `[playfield] wall_height` (mm) when the bundle has one, else 28 mm (the Appendix A.3 example). This applies to generated `tablegen:` guides, which carry no height. Each spline collides only on its own layer.
**Rationale:** brief obligation; A.1 guides have no height field.
**Alternatives:** per-guide height field (schema change).

## 2.5 — state hash, table hash and replay file format
**Decision:**
- Hash: FNV-1a 64 (offset `0xcbf29ce484222325`, prime `0x100000001b3`), integers little-endian, `f32` as exact bits.
- `state_hash` covers, in order: tick; for each of the 16 slots a presence byte and, for a ball, position, velocity, angular velocity, orientation (x,y,z,w), layer, profile id; the 256 switch-hit counts; the 4×u64 switch bitset; every reported score (player index + value); each flipper's relative angle and angular velocity (body order); the next `u64` of a clone of the sim's `Pcg32`.
- `table_hash`: over every file of the bundle by sorted path (path bytes, NUL, length, contents), so a folder and a zip with the same files hash alike.
- Replay file v1: magic `KHZREPL\0`, version u32, seed, table hash, ticks, event count (u64 each), 24-byte events (tick, timestamp ns, kind, code, 2 zero bytes, value bits), FNV-1a 64 checksum trailer. `from_bytes` checks the length against the count before allocating, so allocation is bounded by the input length. Events are validated (known codes, 0/1 buttons, finite axes, ticks 1..=ticks non-decreasing).
- State injected through the test API is not recorded; recordings cover input events only (plus seed and table).
**Rationale:** D9 (same binary + machine); fixed iteration order; no `HashMap`.

## 2.5 — CLI input pattern
**Decision:** `pin_sim <bundle> --ticks N` feeds a fixed built-in input pattern (`cli::pattern_events`: start at tick 20–120, left flipper 90 ticks every 337, right flipper 80 ticks every 421, a one-tick `NudgeX = 0.5` every 2500) so the recorded CI fixture has flippers firing from recorded inputs; the additive `--idle` flag feeds none. The binary runs the sim on a thread with a 16 MiB stack (≥ `MIN_HOST_STACK_BYTES`).
**Rationale:** the brief's CLI has no input source other than a recording; a deterministic pattern makes `--record-inputs` meaningful.
**Alternatives:** record nothing (a recording without inputs would not exercise the flipper path).

## 4.4 — Menu UI decisions

**Decision:**
- **Load while busy is refused, not queued.** `request_load*` while `LoadRequested`/`Loading` returns `LoadRefusal::Busy(pending)` and sets a notice. A queued switch would fire after the player has already seen the first table start; a refusal is visible at once. The lifecycle moves `LoadRequested → Loading` when the consumer drains the `LoadTable` command; success records `last_table`, emits `ConfigChanged` and closes the menu; failure keeps it open with the error.
- **Unknown `config.toml` keys are preserved** (top-level via `#[serde(flatten)] extra`, nested ones re-read from the raw table into `extra` at their path) and written back by `save`; each one is also reported as `ConfigWarning::UnknownKey`. A file that is not TOML, not UTF-8, larger than 1 MiB or has a wrongly typed known value gives the defaults plus one warning (`Corrupt` / `TooLarge` / `Unreadable`); a well-typed out-of-range value is reset alone (`InvalidValue`). `save` writes `.<name>.tmp`, `sync_all`, renames; the temp file is removed on error.
- **Decoded-audio budget (human ruling):** `[audio] decoded_budget_mib`, default 512, accepted 64…8192. Unlike other keys, a wrong type (string, float, negative, > u32) also falls back alone with an `InvalidValue` warning instead of making the file corrupt, as the ruling says "out-of-range or unparsable values fall back to 512". It is the last row of the Display panel (`SettingRow::DecodedAudioBudget`, step 64 MiB) under an "Advanced · audio" heading, not a fourth panel, so the three-panel tab order stays as the brief and the published API describe. `AudioConfig::decoded_budget_bytes()` gives the `DecodeBudget` size for 2.5/6.0.
- **Scan:** direct children only (folders, `.zip`, `.pinbundle`; dot-names skipped), sorted by (ASCII-lower-cased file name, file name), at most 4096 candidates; broken bundles are listed with the `AssetError` text. Preview = `preview.png|jpg|jpeg` at the bundle root, ≤ 8 MiB, ≤ 4096 px per side, box-downscaled to ≤ 256 px; a bad preview is dropped and noted, the table stays loadable. Feature badges are derived from the validated manifests (`TableFeature::of`).
- **Menu toggles:** `Esc` (scancode 41) and the gamepad Guide button (Start/Back are cabinet inputs in the built-in profiles). While open, cabinet presses map to navigation (flippers up/down, upper flippers left/right, Start/Launch select, Action back, Tilt nothing) and are never forwarded.
- **Live input display** ORs per-source pressed bits per cabinet input (`InputMonitor`, 3.2 review N5); the platform-layer OR is 6.0's.
- **Rendering split:** `pin_app::ui::view` builds the egui widgets on the CPU (focused row highlighted so cabinet navigation is visible); `pin_render::ui` owns layout constants, the semi-opaque backdrop (≈ 88 % near-black), `raw_input` with `pixels_per_point` = display content scale (DPI), and `OverlayPainter` (egui-wgpu, `LoadOp::Load` over the playfield). The panel is drawn directly in the root `Ui` (no `Area`) so frames have no fade-in and nothing is drawn outside the panel rect.
- **Missing keys are not corruption (tester D1).** Every nested table takes per-key defaults: a key missing from `[backglass_pip]` / `[score_pip]` takes that PiP's own default (the two PiPs differ, so they deserialize through an all-optional `PartialPip`), and `[calibration]`, `[calibration.plunger]`, `[calibration.nudge]`, `[audio]` use `#[serde(default)]` per field. (Superseded for wrong types by "4.4 — review round 1" below: wrong types now fall back per field.)
- **Step grid anchored at 0 (tester N1).** `Left`/`Right` move a numeric row to the neighbouring multiple of its step (snapping an off-grid value first), clamped to the range, so round defaults stay on the grid (release window 100 → 110 → 100; at the bottom it clamps to 1, then 10).
- **Unknown keys holding `nan` (tester N3).** They are preserved verbatim in `extra`; since `NaN != NaN`, two such configs compare unequal under `PartialEq`. Kept (they are the user's data), but the menu's change detection compares the settings *without* `extra` (an edit never touches it), so a preserved `nan` never causes a spurious `ConfigChanged`/save. Other consumers that diff configs by `PartialEq` should do the same.
- **Extra pin_app dependencies.** Besides `egui`, `pin_app` needs `pin_assets` (scan/validate), `serde` + `toml` (config) and `image` (preview decode), all existing workspace pins. The brief limits the Cargo.toml edit to egui lines; these are recorded here for the Reviewer.

**Rationale:** Consistent with D5/D8 (no I/O outside the I/O worker; deterministic order), D19/D20 (GPU work only behind `gpu-tests`), D31/D32 (no waiting on a person; settings are user-owned files).

**Alternatives:** queue one pending load (rejected above); drop unknown keys (loses newer builds' settings); a fourth "Advanced" panel for the audio budget (changes tab wrapping that tests and the published API rely on).

## 5.2 — Reflection probes and HDR post (Implementer)

- **Decision (probes).** One octahedral probe per ball (slot = the ball's index in the frame's ball list, up to `MAX_PROBES` = 16) in a 128² × 16-layer `Rgba16Float` atlas (2 MiB). A capture renders six 64² cube faces with the PBR renderer from the ball centre (near plane 0.5 · radius, so the ball's own back faces are not seen; every material uses the placeholder environment inside a capture, no recursion), then one remap pass writes the octahedral layer. `ProbeScheduler`: at most `PROBE_BUDGET` = 2 captures per frame, round-robin continuing from the previous frame; with n balls every age ≤ ⌈n/2⌉ − 1; an uncaptured ball (or one past 16) samples the captured probe nearest to it (ties → lower slot). The forward shader picks the layer per draw (`draw.emission.y`, 0 = placeholder environment) and replaces only the mirror term (`env_radiance(refl)`) by the probe; the irradiance term stays the placeholder. **Rationale:** cube faces reuse the 5.1 pipeline unchanged (no second renderer); 64² faces resample to 128² octahedral at about one face texel per probe texel at the equator; a per-draw layer keeps one bind group for the whole pass. **Alternatives:** direct octahedral rasterisation (non-linear projection, needs tessellation); dual paraboloid (seams at the horizon where the playfield is); prefiltered roughness mips (chrome is roughness 0.06; left for 5.4 / later).
- **Decision (bloom).** Energy conserving in exposed radiance: `out = c − k·bright + k·blur(bright)`, `bright` = soft-knee threshold on luminance (threshold 1, knee 0.5) with a firefly clamp (bright luminance ≤ 64), blur = 6-level mip chain (binomial down/up since fix round 1, see below) with weights (1 − 0.65)·0.65ⁱ summing to 1; k = 0.15. Measured on lavapipe: total luminance −0.06 % (threshold 0: −0.06 %), halo radially symmetric about the spot. **5.1 N7:** the firefly clamp bounds what a single 3000–15 000 HDR chrome pixel feeds into the bloom (test `firefly_clamp_bounds_the_halo`: halo 9.26 against the analytic bound 9.6). The raw PBR target is still not clamped (only to f16 range); consumers that display or encode the frame go through the post chain, `RenderedFrame::hdr` is for analytic checks only. **Alternatives:** additive bloom (adds energy; fails criterion 2 by construction); clamp in the PBR shader (would bias the analytic 5.1 radiance tests).
- **Decision (tone mapping, 5.1 N4).** Exposure (a plain scale, `PostConfig::exposure`, default 1) then the Narkowicz ACES fit per channel with no hidden pre-scale, then the sRGB curve (in the shader for non-sRGB outputs). The 5.1 golden snapshots encode the raw HDR (they test the PBR pass) and stay as they are; the "no clipped flat regions" check is made on the post output: 5.1's `light_insert_lit` scene at full lamp level has 1 saturated pixel of 16 384 after ACES (5218 ≥ 1 before), `ceramic_pearl` 0, `chrome_steel` 2. **Alternatives:** Hill's RRT+ODT fit (matrix pair; brief names "ACES" without a fit, Narkowicz is the usual reference and easy to check at sample points).
- **Decision (motion blur).** Per ball, analytic: the screen displacement over a centred shutter (default 1/120 s, clamped to 64 px) is projected from the snapshot velocity; a pixel in the swept capsule shows the mean over `samples` shutter times of the static ball image translated along the displacement, over the background found where the line along the motion leaves the disc. Whether a translated position is "on the ball" is decided by the centre of the pixel it falls in (the rasteriser's own test), so the rim is not lost: footprint mass is conserved to 0.03 % and its variance grows along the velocity by the box-blur value s²/12 (measured 5.49 against 5.28 px² at s = 8 px; across the velocity −0.002 px²). A still ball leaves the image bit-identical. **Alternatives:** a full-screen velocity buffer (needs a second PBR output; only balls move fast enough to matter).
- **Decision (anti-aliasing).** MSAA 4× in the PBR pass (`PbrRenderer::set_msaa_samples(4)`, hardware resolve into `hdr_texture`), not SMAA. Mean edge error against the analytic coverage of a disc: 0.205 without AA, 0.068 with MSAA 4×. **Rationale:** geometric edges (ball silhouettes, inserts) are the aliasing that matters; MSAA needs no extra pass or lookup textures and is exact on lavapipe. **Alternatives:** SMAA (lookup textures, a three-pass chain, and it blurs the probe-sampled chrome).
- **Decision (5.1 N2, grazing albedo).** The diffuse and the base layer under the coat are scaled by (1 − F(n·v)) and (1 − Fc(n·v)) instead of (1 − F(v·h)); the coat lobe uses height-correlated Smith visibility instead of Kelemen. White-furnace albedo at n·v 0.02–0.08 is now 0.885 (white dielectric r 0.1), 0.59 (pearl), 0.59 (wood), was 1.72 / 1.35 / 1.14. 5.1's own analytic tests and implementer goldens (`tests/fixtures/pbr/*.png`) pass unchanged; three 5.1 spec snapshots moved at the rims only (mean |Δ| 0.34–0.40 of 0.5 allowed, 0.7–1.0 % channels > 12 of 0.5 % allowed), so after their analytic assertions passed they were re-blessed (`SPEC_5_1_BLESS=1`, only `golden_ceramic_pearl`, `golden_playfield_wood`, `golden_light_insert_glows_and_warms_up`): `tests/fixtures/spec_5_1/{ceramic_pearl,playfield_wood,light_insert_unlit}.rgba8` (`light_insert_lit` came out byte-identical). **Alternatives:** a multiple-scattering energy-compensation term (needs a DFG lookup texture; more than the review asked).
- **Decision (acrylic).** The blended pass draws each transparent draw twice: back faces first (front faces culled), then front faces (back culled), so the far side is always under the near side and the result does not depend on triangle order (test: reversed index order gives a bit-identical image, and the red card shows through). Refraction is left to 5.4 (ray queries). **Alternatives:** depth-sorted triangles (CPU sort each frame); weighted blended OIT (approximate for two layers that are known).
- **Decision (errors).** `PostChain::new` and `OctahedralProbes::new` create their shaders, pipelines and targets inside a wgpu validation error scope (fix round 1: the post chain's pipelines and targets had been created after the scope was popped) and return `GpuError::InvalidArgument` on failure (no new `GpuError` variant: `gpu/` is not 5.2's). The PBR pipeline (5.1) still uses wgpu's default handler; all shader modules are validated GPU-free by `pbr_naga` / `post_naga`.
- **Test harness note.** The implementer's post/reflection test images go to `target/<dir>/tmp/5_2_images/*.png` (`CARGO_TARGET_TMPDIR`); goldens in `tests/fixtures/{post,reflections}/*.png` are written only with `PIN_UPDATE_SNAPSHOTS=1` and checked after the analytic assertions (mean |Δ| ≤ 3/255, ≤ 2 % px off by > 16/255, and 8-bit energy within 2 % for bloom).

## Orchestrator — 2.5 Tester round 1 rulings
- **`Simulation::score(player)` is 0-based**, the `HostCommandQueue::scores` index that 6.0 also reads; Lua's 1-based `set_score(1, …)` is `score(0)`. Supersedes the Implementer's "script numbering" entry. Rationale: one numbering on the Rust host side; `pin_core` is the contract.
- **CLI:** `--ticks N` feeds a built-in input pattern; `--idle` is the input-less run. Accepted.
- **Recorder:** `Recorder::new(Simulation)` and `Recorder::recording()` are pinned (brief amended).
- **Injected overlap with a bumper** pushes the ball through the floor in one tick (2.4 de-penetration direction); only reachable by injecting an overlapping state → HUMAN_QUEUE note for 2.4/Gate A1, no 2.5 change.

## 2.5 — allocation guard and the Lua arena
**Observation:** over 60 000 guarded ticks of the replay fixture (Lua rules that create no objects) the Lua VM still allocates now and then (372 times; first around tick 2 000): Lua's collector frees half of the unused `CallInfo` records (`luaE_shrinkCI` from the GC's thread traversal) and the next deeper call regrows them (`luaE_extendCI` → `luaM_realloc_` → mlua's allocator → the global allocator). It is a Lua-VM allocation inside its capped arena (2 MiB), not engine code.
**Decision:** PLAN §2.3 rule 4 exempts the Lua VM's arena. `pin_sim::alloc_guard` has an `ExemptScope` (a thread-local depth counter; entering and leaving never allocates). `Simulation` opens it around its calls into a **Lua** backend in tick step 2 (edge delivery and `on_tick`). Allocations inside it are counted separately (`count_allocs_split`) and do not fail `assert_no_alloc` / `count_allocs`; everything else in `step_1ms()` must allocate nothing. Wasm backend calls are not exempt (their guarded runs allocate nothing). The guard test asserts 0 engine allocations over 60 000 ticks for Lua and Wasm, and that the exempt Lua count stays ≤ 1 per 100 ticks.
**Rationale:** the frames are `luaM_*` (the Tester classifies the same frames as the rule-4 exemption); the exemption scope is as narrow as the host can mark without changing pin_script. Its width (the whole Lua callback, which also covers pin_script's Rust host functions) is acceptable because pin_script's own `tests/lua_alloc.rs` guards those.
**Alternatives:** have pin_script pre-reserve the call-info list or keep the GC from shrinking it (outside owned paths; queued as a request) — then the exemption could be dropped.

## 2.5 — replay fixture geometry
**Decision:** the in-repo fixture `crates/pin_sim/tests/fixtures/replay_table` (generated with `pin_tablegen` from its `layout.toml`) has outlanes wider than a ball (outer walls end at x = 110 / 404 mm, 45 mm from the flipper pivots) and a V-shaped catch wall whose bottom is the trough switch. With the earlier layout (walls ending 7 mm from the pivots, flat catch wall) the ball wedged between a wall end and the left flipper pivot and never drained, so the 10 000-tick run did not exercise drains and relaunches. Now the fixture drains about every 2 000 ticks.

## Orchestrator — Wave 5 disk: shared pin_render target dir
With four target dirs at 21 GB and 6.7 GB of allowance left, the 5.2 Reviewer and both 5.3 roles share `target/sp_5_2_rev` (cargo's lock serialises their builds; all three build the same shared tree). Stale-build rule as before: `touch` and rebuild before calling a contradiction a defect. 2.6 and 2.8 start only when a passed subphase's target dir is deleted.

## 5.3 — Displays public API

Published early for the 5.3 Tester; kept current by the 5.3 Implementer. Everything is in
`pin_render::displays` and re-exported at that module's root (`use pin_render::displays::*`). GPU-free parts
(score formatting, scenes, DMD frames, LCD draw lists, routing, change detection) need no adapter; GPU types
take the crate's `GpuContext` and a target `wgpu::TextureFormat`.

**Images.** `struct RgbaImage { width: u32, height: u32, pixels: Vec<u8> }` — straight-alpha RGBA8,
display-encoded (sRGB values as stored in PNGs), row 0 = top. `RgbaImage::new(w, h)` (transparent black),
`from_gray(w, h, Vec<u8>)` (grey → opaque grey), `from_rgb(w, h, Vec<u8>)`, `from_rgba(w, h, Vec<u8>)` (each
`Option`, `None` on a length mismatch or a zero size), `pixel(x, y) -> [u8; 4]`.

**Score formatting (GPU-free).** `const MAX_PLAYERS: usize = 4`.
- `fn format_score(score: i64) -> String`: decimal with `,` thousands separators (`0`, `999`, `1,000`,
  `-1,234,567`; `i64::MIN` works). `fn format_score_with(score: i64, separator: Option<char>) -> String`
  (`None` = no grouping).
- `struct ScoreState { scores: [i64; 4], players: u8, current_player: u8, ball: u8, credits: u32 }`
  (`players` 1..=4, `current_player` 0-based, `ball` 0 = no game in progress). `Default`: 1 player, all 0.
  `apply_commands(&mut self, q: &HostCommandQueue) -> bool` applies `q.scores` in order (`(player, score)`,
  player 0-based; players ≥ 4 ignored; `players` grows to cover the highest player set) and returns whether
  anything changed.
- `fn score_lines(state: &ScoreState) -> ScoreLines`; `struct ScoreLines { rows: Vec<PlayerRow>, status:
  String }`; `struct PlayerRow { player: u8 /* 1-based */, text: String /* format_score */, current: bool }`.
  One row per player (`players` clamped to 1..=4). Status: `ball == 0` → `"CREDITS <n>"`; one player →
  `"BALL <b>  CREDITS <n>"`; several → `"PLAYER <p>  BALL <b>"` (`p` 1-based).

**Scenes (GPU-free; format documented in "5.3 — Display scene format").**
- `Scene::from_toml_str(&str) -> Result<Scene, SceneError>`, `Scene::from_bytes(&[u8])` (UTF-8 + size
  limit `MAX_SCENE_BYTES` = 64 KiB; never panics on any input). `Scene { duration_ms: u64, repeat: bool,
  background: [u8; 3], layers: Vec<SceneLayer> }`.
- `PLACEHOLDER_SCENES: &[(&str, &str)]` (id, TOML text): `scene_attract`, `scene_score`, `scene_game_over`,
  `scene_powerball_mania`, `scene_ball_saved`, `scene_jackpot`. `fn placeholder_scene(id: &str) -> Option<Scene>`.
- `struct SceneLibrary`: `new()`; `insert(&mut self, id: SceneId, name: &str, scene: Scene)`;
  `get(SceneId) -> Option<&Scene>`; `id_of(&str) -> Option<SceneId>`; `len()`;
  `SceneLibrary::load(defs: &[pin_core::schema::table::SceneDef], read: impl FnMut(&str) -> Result<Vec<u8>, String>)
  -> Result<SceneLibrary, SceneError>` — `SceneId(i)` = declaration index i (as `pin_assets` interns them);
  a file that cannot be read falls back to the placeholder of the same id (if any), else `SceneError::Read`.
  `SceneLibrary::placeholders()` = the placeholder set with ids in `PLACEHOLDER_SCENES` order.
- `struct ScenePlayer`: `show(&mut self, id: SceneId, now_ms: u64)`; `apply_commands(&mut self, q: &HostCommandQueue,
  now_ms: u64)` (the last entry of `q.display_scenes` wins); `active<'a>(&self, lib: &'a SceneLibrary, now_ms) ->
  Option<(&'a Scene, u64 /* elapsed ms */)>` (a scene with `duration_ms > 0` and `repeat = false` ends after its
  duration; `repeat` wraps elapsed modulo duration; unknown ids show nothing); `clear()`.
- Time is the caller's monotone ms clock (the snapshot tick; D8 — never wall time).

**Score display model.** `enum ScoreDisplayConfig { Dmd(DmdSpec), Lcd(LcdSpec) }`,
`ScoreDisplayConfig::from_manifest(&pin_core::schema::backglass::ScoreDisplay) -> Result<_, DisplayError>`
(missing `dmd`/`lcd` table for the chosen mode → defaults 128×32 orange / 1920×1080).
`struct ScoreDisplayModel`: `new(config, library: SceneLibrary)`; `scores()` / `scores_mut()`; `scenes()` /
`scenes_mut() -> &mut ScenePlayer`; `library()`; `images_mut() -> &mut ImageSet`; `config()`; `apply_commands(&mut self, q, now_ms)`; `compose(&self, now_ms) ->
DisplayContent` with `enum DisplayContent { Dmd(DmdFrame), Lcd(LcdDrawList) }`. With no active scene the
display shows the built-in score view (`scene_score`: one `kind = "score"` layer).

**DMD.** `struct DmdSpec { width: u32, height: u32, color: pin_core::schema::backglass::DmdColor }`;
`DmdSpec::new(w, h, color) -> Result<DmdSpec, DisplayError>` (w 16..=512, h 8..=256); consts `DMD_128X32`
(orange) and `DMD_192X64` (orange) sizes. `struct DmdFrame { width, height, dots: Vec<[u8; 3]> }` (row-major,
row 0 top; per-dot drive level 0..=255 per channel; mono modes store the same value in all three channels,
quantised to 16 levels: `v = round(b·15)·17`). `DmdFrame::new(w, h)`, `get(x, y)`, `set(x, y, [u8; 3])`,
`lit_count()`, `DmdFrame::compose(spec, scene, elapsed_ms, scores, images: &ImageSet)`. `enum DmdFont { Font5x7, Font8x16 }`.
- Text on the DMD uses the kit's bitmap fonts (`pin_assetgen::fonts`, the glyphs of the `font_dmd_5x7` /
  `font_dmd_8x16` sheets), glyph spacing 1 dot, at integer scale: a text layer of height `size·H` dots uses
  8×16 at scale ⌊size·H/16⌋ when that is ≥ 1 and the text fits the width, else 5×7 at scale
  max(1, ⌊size·H/7⌋) (shrunk until it fits, min scale 1). `font = "5x7" | "8x16"` forces a font.
  `fn dmd_text_dots(text: &str, font: DmdFont, scale: u32) -> (u32, u32)` gives the text's size in dots.
- Shading (CPU reference = shader): `fn dmd_tint(color: DmdColor) -> [f32; 3]` (orange `[1.0, 0.42, 0.06]`,
  red `[1.0, 0.12, 0.08]`, rgb `[1, 1, 1]`); `const DMD_UNLIT_LEVEL: f32 = 0.10` (an unlit dot is the tint at
  10 %); `fn dmd_dot_color(color, drive: [u8; 3]) -> [f32; 3]` = tint · (UNLIT + (1 − UNLIT)·drive/255) per
  channel (rgb mode: tint = drive colour); background between dots `DMD_BACKGROUND = [0, 0, 0]`.
  Geometry: `fn dmd_geometry(spec, viewport: Rect) -> DmdGeometry { origin: [f32; 2], pitch: f32,
  radius: f32 }` — pitch = min(vw/W, vh/H) (not rounded), grid centred in the viewport, dot radius
  `DMD_DOT_RADIUS = 0.40`·pitch, 1-px anti-aliased edge; `DmdGeometry::dot_center(x, y) -> [f32; 2]` in
  target pixels.
- `struct DmdRenderer`: `new(ctx: &GpuContext, format: wgpu::TextureFormat, spec: DmdSpec)`; `spec()`;
  `set_frame(&mut self, queue: &wgpu::Queue, frame: &DmdFrame) -> bool` (uploads only on change, returns
  whether it changed); `encode(&mut self, encoder: &mut wgpu::CommandEncoder, view: &wgpu::TextureView,
  target_size: (u32, u32), viewport: Rect)` (paints the whole viewport: background, unlit and lit dots).

**LCD.** `struct LcdSpec { width: u32, height: u32 }` (canvas, default 1920×1080 = 16:9; `LcdSpec::new` →
`Result`, 16..=8192 each). `struct LcdDrawList { width, height, background: [u8; 3], items: Vec<LcdItem> }`
with `enum LcdItem { Rect { rect: [f32; 4] /* x0, y0, x1, y1 canvas px */, color: [u8; 4] }, Image { file: String,
rect: [f32; 4], color: [u8; 4] /* tint, alpha = opacity */ }, Text { text: String, origin: [f32; 2] /* top-left */, cell: f32 /* glyph cell
height px */, color: [u8; 4] } }` in back-to-front order; `LcdDrawList::compose(spec, scene, elapsed_ms,
scores, images: &ImageSet)` (images only for the aspect of `h = 0` image layers). `fn vector_text_width(text, cell) -> f32`,
`GLYPH_ADVANCE_UNITS = 6`, `GLYPH_STROKE_HALF_WIDTH = 0.42`. Text is drawn with the "vector" font: the 5×7 glyphs as strokes (lit dots joined to lit
neighbours, diagonals only where both orthogonal neighbours are unlit), stroke half-width 0.42 cell units,
anti-aliased, scalable; a glyph is 5×7 units, advance 6 units, unit = cell/7 px.
- Compositing: premultiplied "over" in display-encoded space, back to front; item colour alpha and image
  alpha multiply. `struct LcdRenderer`: `new(ctx, format, spec)`; `set_image(&mut self, ctx, file: &str, image:
  &RgbaImage) -> Result<(), GpuError>` (same file again → replaced in place); `set_draw_list(&mut self, queue, list: &LcdDrawList) -> bool`; `encode(&mut self, encoder,
  view, target_size, viewport)` (canvas fitted with `fit_aspect`, letterbox black).

**Backglass.** `enum LayerBlend { Over, Additive }`; `struct LayerLamp { lamp: LampId, mask: RgbaImage /* red
channel = mask */, blend: LayerBlend }`; `struct BackglassLayer { image: RgbaImage, lamp: Option<LayerLamp> }`;
`struct BackglassDesc { layers: Vec<BackglassLayer> }` (back to front; the first layer's size sets the aspect;
every layer is stretched over the whole translite).
`BackglassDesc::from_manifest(m: &BackglassManifest, resolve_lamp: impl Fn(&str) -> Option<(LampId, LampKind)>,
load_image: impl FnMut(&str) -> Result<RgbaImage, String>) -> Result<BackglassDesc, DisplayError>` (layers
sorted by `depth`, stable; a mask whose lamp is `LampKind::Flasher` → `Additive`, else `Over`).
- Blend (per pixel, display-encoded, k = mask·clamp(lamp, 0, 1)·layer alpha): `Over`: out = dst + (rgb − dst)·k;
  `Additive` (flasher): out = dst + rgb·k (clamped by the target). Layers without a lamp: plain alpha-over.
  So the output is linear in the lamp level inside the mask and unchanged where mask = 0.
- `struct BackglassRenderer`: `new(ctx, format, &desc) -> Result<_, GpuError>`; `aspect() -> (u32, u32)`;
  `lamps() -> Vec<LampId>`; `needs_redraw(&self, lamps: &[f32]) -> bool` (any referenced lamp moved by ≥
  `LAMP_REDRAW_EPSILON` = 1/1024 since the last `encode`, or never drawn, or `mark_dirty()` called);
  `encode(&mut self, encoder, view, target_size, viewport: Rect, lamps: &[f32])` (indexed by `LampId`;
  missing entries = 0; letterboxed black).

**Routing.** `struct DisplayRoute { role: SurfaceRole, window: usize, viewport: Rect, dedicated: bool }`;
`fn display_routes(plan: &TopologyPlan) -> Vec<DisplayRoute>` (Backglass then Score, each where the plan shows
it: `dedicated` = the role fills its own window); `fn fit_aspect(viewport: Rect, aspect_w: u32, aspect_h: u32) ->
Rect` (largest centred rect of that aspect, rounded down). `struct DisplaySet { backglass:
Option<BackglassRenderer>, score: Option<ScoreRenderer> }` with `enum ScoreRenderer { Dmd(DmdRenderer),
Lcd(LcdRenderer) }`; `DisplaySet::encode_window(&mut self, window: usize, routes: &[DisplayRoute], encoder,
view, target_size, lamps: &[f32])` draws every display routed to that window into its viewport.

**Argument passing.** Specs are passed by value to `render_dmd_to_image` / `render_lcd_to_image` /
`DmdRenderer::new` / `LcdRenderer::new` / `ScoreRenderer::new` (configs are `Copy`) and by reference to
`dmd_geometry`, `DmdFrame::compose` and `LcdDrawList::compose` (which takes five arguments: spec, scene,
elapsed_ms, scores, `&ImageSet`). `fit_aspect` never panics (positions saturate at `i32::MAX`). A `score`
layer draws only inside its box (DMD: dots whose centres lie in the box; LCD: text shrunk to 96 % of the box
width, `TEXT_FIT`). `from` must have exactly two numbers.

**Change detection.** Displays redraw only on change: `DmdRenderer::set_frame` / `LcdRenderer::set_draw_list`
return `false` for content equal to the last one; `ScoreRenderer::update(&mut self, queue, &DisplayContent) ->
bool`; `BackglassRenderer::needs_redraw(lamps)`. `struct ChangeTracker` (GPU-free): `observe(&mut self,
lamps: &[f32], content: &DisplayContent) -> DisplayDirty { backglass: bool, score: bool }` for callers without
renderers (lamp ids registered with `ChangeTracker::new(lamp_ids: Vec<LampId>)`).

**Offscreen rendering for tests.** `fn render_dmd_to_image(ctx, spec, frame: &DmdFrame, width, height) ->
Result<RgbaImage, GpuError>`, `fn render_lcd_to_image(ctx, spec, list: &LcdDrawList, images: &ImageSet, width,
height)`, `fn render_backglass_to_image(ctx, desc: &BackglassDesc, lamps: &[f32], width, height)`: each renders
into an `Rgba8Unorm` texture with the viewport = whole image and reads it back (RGBA8, row 0 top).
`struct ImageSet` (file → `RgbaImage`, `insert`, `get`) supplies scene images. Errors: `DisplayError`
(`Display`, `std::error::Error`). `fn shader_sources() -> [(&str, String); 2]` (WGSL for naga).
Encode calls take no queue: per-call uniforms are fresh buffers, so a renderer may be encoded several times
per submit (e.g. into two windows). `set_frame`/`set_draw_list` upload through the queue (effective at the
next submit). Score layout helpers: `score_view_layout(&ScoreState) -> Vec<ScoreText>`, `expand_text(&str,
&ScoreState) -> String` (scene text placeholders).

**Fix round 1 additions.** `RgbaImage::is_valid()` (sides ≥ 1, `pixels.len() == w·h·4`; `RgbaImage::new` uses
checked size maths and yields an invalid empty image on overflow). GPU uploads (`LcdRenderer::set_image`,
`BackglassRenderer::new`, `render_lcd_to_image`, `render_backglass_to_image`) refuse invalid images and any
side above the device's `max_texture_dimension_2d` with `GpuError::InvalidArgument` (D25: bundle images are
untrusted); CPU composition skips invalid images. Lamp masks and images are premultiplied by their alpha on
upload (a mask's value = red × alpha). `DmdRenderer::mark_dirty`, `LcdRenderer::mark_dirty`,
`ScoreRenderer::mark_dirty` (next `set_frame` / `set_draw_list` / `update` reports a change; setting an LCD
image also does). `LcdRenderer::texture_count()`. `OTHER_PLAYER_EMPHASIS` (0.5) is exported.


## Orchestrator — Wave 5 batch B: 2.6 and 2.8 share a physics target dir
2.6 and 2.8 start once disk is back at 11 GB free. Both build only `pin_physics` (+ `pin_tablegen`/`pin_assets` for 2.8), so their four roles share `target/sp_phys` (cargo locks it; stale-build rule applies). Reviewers will use `target/sp_phys_rev`.

## 5.3 — Display scene format

**Decision.** A display scene is one TOML file per `[[scenes]]` entry of `table.toml` (`SceneId(i)` =
declaration index, as `pin_assets` interns names). Coordinates and sizes are fractions of the display, so
one scene works on 128 × 32, 192 × 64 and any LCD canvas. Keys (all optional unless noted):
- Top level: `schema_version = 1` (required), `duration_ms` (0 = until replaced; ≤ 1 h), `repeat` (loop the
  timeline; needs `duration_ms > 0`), `background = "#rrggbb"`, `[[layers]]` (≤ 64, back to front).
- Layer: `kind` (required) = `text` | `rect` | `image` | `score`; `text` (kind text only, ≤ 256 chars,
  placeholders `{score}` `{score1}`…`{score4}` `{player}` `{ball}` `{credits}`); `file` (kind image only;
  a bundle path, decoded by the caller into an `ImageSet`); `x`, `y` (anchor, ±4; default 0.5/0.5 for
  text, 0/0 otherwise); `w`, `h` (box, 0..=4, default 1 × 1; image `h = 0` keeps the image's aspect);
  `align` = left|center|right, `valign` = top|middle|bottom (defaults: text centre/middle, others
  left/top); `size` (text glyph-cell height, fraction of display height, default 0.5); `font` =
  auto|5x7|8x16 (DMD only); `color` (default white; mono DMDs use the brightest channel); `alpha` 0..=1;
  timeline `start_ms`, `end_ms` (0 = scene end), `blink_ms` (on/off half period), `fade_in_ms`,
  `fade_out_ms` (before the end), `from = [x, y]` + `move_ms` (linear move to `x, y`). Unknown keys are errors.
- `score` layers draw the score view (`score_view_layout`) inside their box. The layout box is the layer box ∩ the display: a partly off-screen score layer is laid out in its visible part, not cropped. Without an active scene the
  display shows `scene_score` (the library's, else the placeholder).
- Player: the last `display_scenes` entry of a tick wins; a timed scene ends after `duration_ms` and the
  score view returns. Time is the caller's ms clock (snapshot ticks, D8).
- Placeholders (D32, 4.6 N7 hand-off), embedded in `pin_render` (`src/displays/scenes/*.toml`):
  `scene_attract`, `scene_score`, `scene_game_over`, `scene_powerball_mania`, `scene_ball_saved`,
  `scene_jackpot`. `SceneLibrary::load` uses the placeholder of the same id when a scene file cannot be read
  (the A.1 example's `display/powerball_mania.toml` need not exist yet).
**Rationale.** Fractional coordinates keep tables independent of the display mode the player picks (D32:
real scenes replace placeholders by id). Limits bound untrusted bundle content (D25); the parser never
panics (proptest on random and mutated bytes).
**Alternatives.** Pixel coordinates per mode (a scene per mode and size); frame-by-frame bitmaps (no LCD
scaling, large files); Lua-driven display code (puts display logic on the sim thread).

## 5.3 — Backglass and score display (Implementer)

- **Colour space.** Art and compositing stay in display-encoded (sRGB-valued) space, like 2D art tools; the
  1.4 surfaces are non-sRGB formats, so values pass through unchanged. On an `*Srgb` target the shaders decode
  their output (blending then happens in linear, approximately right). This makes lamp-mask blending exactly
  linear in the lamp level in stored values (verification criterion).
- **Lamp layers.** `out = dst + (rgb − dst)·a·mask·lamp` (premultiplied alpha-over with a scaled source);
  layers whose mask lamp is `LampKind::Flasher` (from `table.toml`) are additive, `out = dst + rgb·a·mask·lamp`
  (no bloom; 5.2 owns bloom). `backglass.toml` has no flasher keyword, so the lamp kind decides. Lamp levels
  are the snapshot `lamps[LampId]` (callers may pass 5.1 `LampBank` warm-up levels instead), clamped 0..=1.
- **DMD look.** Tints orange `[1, 0.42, 0.06]`, red `[1, 0.12, 0.08]`, rgb = drive colour; unlit dots at 10 %
  of the tint (lit/unlit luminance ratio 10); dot radius 0.40 pitch, 1-px AA edge, black background; pitch
  not rounded, grid centred (letterboxed in a 4:1 PiP or any window). Mono modes quantise to 16 levels.
  Fonts: the kit's 5×7 / 8×16 bitmaps (`pin_assetgen::fonts`, the same glyphs as the `font_dmd_*` sheets)
  — `pin_assetgen` became a `pin_render` dependency (light: no GPU, no pin_assets). The 8×16 smearing of
  dense glyphs (4.6 N4/N7) is left as is.
- **LCD.** Canvas from `[score_display] lcd` (default 1920 × 1080), fitted with `fit_aspect` (letterbox). The
  "vector font" is the 5×7 glyph set drawn as anti-aliased strokes by a signed-distance shader (dots joined
  to neighbours), so it scales to any size without a font file (D32).
- **Change detection.** CPU content (DMD frame / LCD draw list) is compared with the last uploaded content;
  backglass lamps redraw on a change ≥ 1/1024 or on reaching 0 / 1. `ChangeTracker` does the same GPU-free.
  Animated scenes therefore redraw only while something visibly changes.
- **Routing.** `display_routes(&TopologyPlan)` + `DisplaySet::encode_window` draw each display into its PiP
  rectangle or dedicated window (scissored to the viewport, aspect kept). Replacing the 1.4 grey PiP
  placeholder outline in `gpu::SurfaceSet` with `DisplaySet` is 6.0's integration (outside 5.3's paths).
- **Readback.** `render_*_to_image` use their own copy of the bounded 1.4 readback (`debug::read_texture` is
  crate-visible only under `cfg(test)`).
**Rationale.** D7 (latest snapshot, no interpolation), D19 (secondary surfaces redraw only on change), D30
(analytic checks first), D32 (code-generated assets). **Alternatives:** CPU compositing of the LCD (slow at
1080p while animating); MSDF font atlases (needs a font file and a generator).

## 2.6 — Banana flipper public API

Published early for the 2.6 Tester; kept current by the Implementer. Everything is in `pin_physics::flipper_banana` (`crates/pin_physics/src/flipper_banana/`). Units: metres and radians in the type, mm and degrees in `FlipperShape`.

- **Using it through the solver.** `FlipperSet::new` / `with_tuning` now accept `FlipperShape::CurvedBanana { length_mm, arc_radius_mm, arc_sweep_deg, tube_radius_mm }`; `FlipperError::ShapeNotSupported` is no longer returned (the variant stays for source compatibility). A bad banana is `FlipperError::NonFinite { field }` / `OutOfRange { field }` with `field` one of `"shape.length_mm"`, `"shape.arc_radius_mm"`, `"shape.arc_sweep_deg"`, `"shape.tube_radius_mm"`. Everything else of `FlipperSet` (per-tick sequence, EOS, zipper, Thing Flip, `MovingBodies`) is unchanged; no solver code changed.
- **Geometry convention (bat frame of 2.3: origin at the pivot, `x` = bat angle 0 direction turned by the bat angle, `z` = rotation axis, `y = z × x`).**
  - The bat is the set of points within `tube_radius` of a circular **centreline arc** in the mounting plane, extruded along `z` from the mounting plane (`z = 0`) to `FlipperTuning::bat_height_m` (30 mm) with flat top and bottom faces, exactly like `StraightCapsule`. Both ends are round caps (half discs of `tube_radius`), the base cap centred on the pivot.
  - **Where the pivot sits:** the pivot is the **start of the centreline arc**, and the arc leaves the pivot **tangent to bat-frame +x**. So `rest_angle_deg`, `angle_deg()` and `bat_direction()` describe the tangent of the bat at its pivot (the straight bat it would be without the bend), not the chord. The arc then curls toward its concave side.
  - **`length_mm` is the chord:** straight-line distance from the pivot to the centre of the tip cap. **Arc angle** `φ = 2·asin(length / (2·arc_radius))` (the angle the centreline subtends at its centre of curvature; arc length `arc_radius·φ`). The tip-cap centre is at `length·(cos(φ/2), s·sin(φ/2), 0)`, the centre of curvature at `(0, s·arc_radius, 0)`, with `s = ±1` the concave side. `reach = length + tube_radius`, the same as a `StraightCapsule` of that length whose tip radius is `tube_radius`. As `arc_radius → ∞` the bat becomes the `StraightCapsule { length_mm, base_radius_mm = tip_radius_mm = tube_radius_mm }`.
  - **Which side is concave:** with `arc_sweep_deg ≥ 0` the **concave face is the leading face of the swing** (the face that strikes the ball when the coil fires): bat-frame `+y` for `sweep_angle_deg ≥ 0`, `−y` for `sweep_angle_deg < 0`. So a left flipper (`rest −30°, sweep +50°`) and its mirror image (`rest 210°, sweep −50°`) with the same `CurvedBanana` values are mirror images, both with the pocket facing up the table and the tip curling up the table. `arc_sweep_deg < 0` puts the convex face in front (tip curls back, away from the swing). `0.0` and `−0.0` count as `≥ 0`: concave leads.
  - **`arc_sweep_deg` magnitude is not used.** The contract carries three numbers for a two-parameter arc (chord, radius, sweep); `length_mm` and `arc_radius_mm` fix the arc (criterion 2 varies the radius at equal length), and only the sign of `arc_sweep_deg` is read (bend direction). It must be finite. See "2.6 — Banana flipper decisions".
  - **Valid:** all finite; `length > 0`; `tube_radius > 0`; `arc_radius ≥ length/2` (the arc is at most a half circle); `arc_radius > tube_radius` (the concave face exists).
- **Types.**
  ```rust
  pub enum ConcaveSide { PlusY, MinusY }               // bat-frame side of the centre of curvature
  impl ConcaveSide { pub fn sign(self) -> f32;          // +1.0 / −1.0
                     pub fn leading(sweep_angle_deg: f32, arc_sweep_deg: f32) -> ConcaveSide; }
  pub enum BananaFace { Concave, Convex }
  pub enum BananaError { NotBanana, NonFinite { field: &'static str }, OutOfRange { field: &'static str } }
  pub struct CurvedBanana { .. }                        // Copy, Clone, Debug, PartialEq; implements BatShape
  impl CurvedBanana {
      /// chord, arc radius, tube radius, z-extent [bottom, top], all metres. None unless valid (see above, and bottom < top).
      pub fn new(length: f32, arc_radius: f32, tube_radius: f32, concave: ConcaveSide, bottom: f32, top: f32) -> Option<Self>;
      /// From the contract: `shape` must be `FlipperShape::CurvedBanana`; `sweep_angle_deg` is the flipper's
      /// (`FlipperConfig::sweep_angle_deg`, decides the leading side); z-extent [0, bat_height_m].
      pub fn from_shape(shape: &FlipperShape, sweep_angle_deg: f32, bat_height_m: f32) -> Result<Self, BananaError>;
      pub fn from_config(config: &FlipperConfig, bat_height_m: f32) -> Result<Self, BananaError>;
      pub fn length(&self) -> f32;        // chord, m
      pub fn arc_radius(&self) -> f32;    // m
      pub fn tube_radius(&self) -> f32;   // m
      pub fn arc_angle(&self) -> f32;     // φ, rad, in (0, π]
      pub fn arc_length(&self) -> f32;    // arc_radius·φ, m
      pub fn concave_side(&self) -> ConcaveSide;
      pub fn center_of_curvature(&self) -> Vec3;        // bat frame, z = 0
      pub fn tip_center(&self) -> Vec3;                 // bat frame, z = 0
      /// Centreline point at `fraction` of the arc length from the pivot (0 = pivot, 1 = tip-cap centre; clamped), z = 0.
      pub fn centerline_point(&self, fraction: f32) -> Vec3;
      /// Unit tangent of the centreline there (pivot → tip).
      pub fn centerline_tangent(&self, fraction: f32) -> Vec3;
      /// Surface point of `face` at `fraction` along the arc and its unit outward normal (bat frame, z = 0):
      /// centreline_point ± tube_radius·normal. "Contact at 25 % along the arc" = a ball touching this point.
      pub fn face_point(&self, fraction: f32, face: BananaFace) -> (Vec3, Vec3);
  }
  impl BatShape for CurvedBanana { closest_point (analytic), reach, height_range;
      surface_velocity = ω ẑ × p and cast_sphere = conservative advancement in the bat frame (the trait's defaults). }
  ```
  To place a ball in the world from a bat-frame point `p` of flipper `id` (flat mounting plane, normal +z): `pivot_m(id) + x·p.x + y·p.y + z·p.z` with `x = bat_direction(id)`, `y = normal × x`.

## 2.8 — Clearance validator public API

Module `pin_tablegen::clearance` (Subphase 2.8). All lengths in the API are **mm** (table units) unless the name says `_m`; geometry is read in SI from the loaded table and scene. Kept current while 2.8 is in progress.

- **Entry points.**
  ```rust
  pub fn validate_clearance(table: &pin_assets::LoadedTable, scene: &pin_assets::GltfScene) -> ClearanceReport;
  /// load_table(vfs) + load_gltf(vfs, meta.playfield_mesh) + validate_clearance.
  pub fn validate_bundle(vfs: &pin_assets::Vfs) -> Result<ClearanceReport, pin_assets::AssetError>;
  /// Same checks with given diameters (m), each unrestricted, named "<d> mm ball" (e.g. "79.40 mm ball"); for sweeps.
  pub fn validate_clearance_diameters(table: &LoadedTable, scene: &GltfScene, diameters_m: &[f32]) -> ClearanceReport;
  // CLI (args after "validate"); errors are pin_tablegen::layout::CliError (Usage → exit 2, Failed → exit 1):
  pub fn run_validate_cli(args: &[String]) -> Result<String, CliError>;            // summary + "\nwarning: ..." lines
  pub fn validate_cli_output(args: &[String]) -> Result<(String, Vec<String>), CliError>; // (summary, warnings)
  pub const VALIDATE_USAGE: &str;
  pub const MIN_CLEARANCE_MM: f32 = 1.0; MIN_HEADROOM_GAP_MM: f32 = 1.0; WIREFORM_MIN_CONTACT_DEG: f32 = 20.0;
  pub const WIREFORM_MAX_CONTACT_DEG: f32 = 75.0; VUK_EXIT_TOLERANCE_MM: f32 = 0.5;
  ```
- **Result types.**
  ```rust
  pub enum Feature { Lane, FlipperGap, Wireform, Scoop, VukExit, DeckHeadroom, Restriction }   // Copy, Eq, Debug
  impl Feature { pub fn label(self) -> &'static str }  // "lane", "flipper gap", "wireform", "scoop", "vuk exit", "deck headroom", "restriction"
  pub struct ClearanceError {           // Clone, Debug, PartialEq
      pub feature: Feature,
      pub name: String,      // offending feature: lane base name ("tablegen:lanes[0]"), spline id, "<flipper id a> / <flipper id b>" (`[[flippers]] id`s, e.g. "lower_left / lower_right"), mech id, glTF node name, or "balls.restrict[i]"
      pub profile: String,   // ball profile name, e.g. "jumbo_xl_resin"
      pub file: String,      // "table.toml" or meta.playfield_mesh (e.g. "playfield.glb")
      pub path: String,      // key path like pin_assets::ValidationError: "splines[3]", "flippers[0]", "mechs[2]", "mechs[2].exit_offset", "meshes[4]" (GltfScene::meshes index), "balls.restrict[1].area", "layer_transitions[0]"
      pub required_mm: f32,  // what the profile needs (0.0 where no length applies: restriction findings, the flipper-build warning; fix round 1 N5, was NaN)
      pub actual_mm: f32,    // what the geometry offers
      pub message: String,   // names the feature, the profile and both numbers
  }
  impl ClearanceError { pub fn to_validation_error(&self) -> pin_assets::ValidationError }  // same file/path/message
  impl Display for ClearanceError  // "{file}: {path}: {message}"
  pub struct ClearanceReport { pub errors: Vec<ClearanceError>, pub warnings: Vec<ClearanceError> }  // each sorted by (file, path, profile, message)
  impl ClearanceReport {
      pub fn is_ok(&self) -> bool;                                  // errors.is_empty()
      pub fn into_result(self) -> Result<Vec<pin_assets::ValidationError>, Vec<pin_assets::ValidationError>>; // Ok(warnings) / Err(errors)
  }
  ```
- **Notes kept from the Tester's checks (N3).** A layer-only restriction (`layers = [0]`) of the XL ball still fails under a 65 mm deck over layer 0 (the floor runs under the deck); an `area` is what keeps it out. Profiles named only in `balls.restrict` or dispenser `contents` are checked like `balls.profiles`.
- **Which profiles.** Every profile the table declares: `balls.profiles`, `balls.restrict[*].profile`, dispenser `contents` (as 4.1-H lint (c)); duplicates once; checked with the profile's diameter `d` (any value works, nothing is special-cased by name).
- **Restrictions (layers and areas).** `[balls] restrict = [ { profile, layers = [..], area = [[x, y], ...] } ]`. `area` (mm, plan view, optional, CR-005 additive in `pin_core::schema::table::BallRestriction`) is a polygon (≥ 3 points, even-odd rule) the ball **centre** stays inside; omitted/empty = the whole layer. A profile with at least one restrict entry may only be on the union of its entries (layers × area); a profile with none is unrestricted (every layer, everywhere). A feature is checked for a profile only where the profile may be: its layer is allowed and its reference point(s) lie in the area (see each check). A bad area (< 3 points, non-finite, zero area) is an error `Feature::Restriction` at `balls.restrict[i].area`.
- **Checks (errors), each with `MIN_CLEARANCE_MM = 1.0`:**
  1. **Lane** — guide pairs `<base>.left` / `<base>.right` on one layer (the generator's `tablegen:lanes[i].left/right`; hand-written guides named the same way also count). Width = smallest **plan-view** distance from the left rail's samples (≤ 2 mm apart) to the right rail (a raised rail does not widen the lane); required `d + 1`. Reference points: the midpoints between each left sample and its closest right point; one counts when the ball's disc can cover it (area contains it or comes within `r` of it). Only guides named `<base>.left` / `<base>.right` are lane-checked; other guide pairs are not. Error at `table.toml` `splines[<index of the .left rail>]`, `name` = `<base>`.
  2. **Flipper gap** — every two flippers on the same `collision_layer`, both at rest (zipper open): smallest distance between the bat surfaces (from the physics `FlipperSet` shapes); a gap `0 < g < d + 1` wedges the ball. Touching/overlapping bats are not a gap. Reference point: the middle of the gap. Error at `flippers[i]` (the lower index), `name` = `"<id a> / <id b>"` from `[[flippers]] id` (e.g. `"lower_left / lower_right"`). A gap g with `0 < g < d + 1 mm` is an error; the gap counts when the ball's disc can cover its middle point (area contains it or comes within `r`). A flipper whose shape the physics cannot build yet is skipped with a warning.
  3. **Wireform** — every wireform spline with `gauge` g (rail centre spacing; rails are lines): the ball sits on the rails at contact angle α from vertical, `sin α = (g/2)/r`. Required `WIREFORM_MIN_CONTACT_DEG = 20° ≤ α ≤ WIREFORM_MAX_CONTACT_DEG = 75°` (below: perched on top and rolls off sideways at 0.36 g; above: drops through or nearly). `required_mm`/`actual_mm` report the gauge window `2r·sin 20°…2r·sin 75°` vs g. Reference points: the centreline samples. Error at `splines[i]`.
  4. **Scoop** (`[[mechs]] kind = "vuk"`): the held ball (centre at `position + r·ẑ`, capture `layer`, default 0) must clear the pocket: opening = 2 × distance from that centre to the nearest colliding geometry on the capture layer that is a wall (triangle steeper than 45°) or above the centre, or a guide wall on that layer; required `d + 1`. A scoop on open floor has an infinite opening. The VUK's `radius` is the 2.4 capture radius of the ball centre, not a hole size, so it is not used. Reference point: the scoop position, counted when the ball's disc can cover it. Error at `mechs[i]`.
  5. **VUK exit**: the ejected ball (centre at `position + exit_offset`, on `exit_layer`) must not overlap colliding geometry or guide walls of `exit_layer` by more than `VUK_EXIT_TOLERANCE_MM = 0.5`. Error at `mechs[i].exit_offset`, `actual_mm` = distance to the geometry, `required_mm` = r − 0.5. Also an error (same path, message "no surface …", fix round 1 N2): an exit point with no walkable collider of `exit_layer` below it (surface at most `r − 1 mm` below the centre) and no guide/wireform of that layer within 100 mm (plan + height, straight segments between through-points) — e.g. below the playfield or off a deck.
  6. **Deck headroom** (sphere test since fix round 1, Reviewer B2) — for every walkable collider triangle (normal within 45° of ±z) on a layer the ball may use (the *support*) and every non-steep collider triangle on a **different** layer (the *ceiling*, normal within 60° of ±z) whose plan box comes within `r + 1 mm` of the support's: sample support points (grid ≤ min(r/2, 10 mm), at most 1024 per axis and 40 000 per pair, the overlap centre, the ceiling's corners, and for a restricted profile points of its areas: vertices, edges and an interior grid) where the ball centre may be (area), that are not inside a `[[layer_transitions]]` zone between the two layers, and where a ball resting on the support clears the support layer's wall triangles (steeper than 60°) by its radius. The resting ball (centre at support + r) must keep `r + 1 mm` from the ceiling triangle (exact closest point), i.e. headroom `r + distance ≥ d + 1 mm` (= the vertical gap under a flat deck). Ceiling points less than `MIN_HEADROOM_GAP_MM = 1.0` above the support are ignored. So a deck edge the ball can reach from the side, a sloped ceiling and a low vertex all count; an area must stay `r + 1 mm` clear of a deck edge unless a wall of the support layer keeps the ball away. Error at `<meta.playfield_mesh>` `meshes[<ceiling index>]`, `name` = ceiling node name (e.g. `decks[0]`), reporting the smallest headroom. Same-layer overhangs are not checked (the ball collides with them).
- **Warnings (do not fail validation).** `Feature::Restriction`: a `[[layer_transitions]]` zone the restricted profile can reach (from-layer allowed, zone overlapping the area) leading to a layer it may not use (`layer_transitions[i]`); a VUK that captures the profile and ejects it onto a layer it may not use (`mechs[i].exit_layer`). Flippers the physics cannot build (`flippers[i]`).
- **CLI.** `pin_tablegen validate <bundle_dir_or_.pinbundle>`: exit 0 with the summary line on **stdout** and each warning (`warning: file: path: message`) on **stderr** when the bundle loads and passes (Orchestrator ruling on Tester D2); exit 1 with every error (`pin_assets` load errors or clearance errors, one per line, `file: path: message`) otherwise; exit 2 for usage errors. Any other first argument keeps the 4.3 generator command line (so a layout file literally named `validate` must be given as `./validate`).

## Orchestrator — 5.2 fix round 1: probe staleness must not change capture order
The N3 fix (stale probe after a ball drains) made the scheduler capture moved balls first, which starved others at ≥ 0.3 m/frame and reported age 0 for uncaptured balls (Tester D3). **Ruling:** staleness only changes which probe a ball samples (fallback to the nearest captured probe); captures remain strict round-robin by ball index with the published age bound, count changes may not restart or starve the cursor, and `ages()` reports true ages. After two balls swap places each samples the probe captured nearest its current position; the Tester amends the one spec that expected the old index-based answer.

## Orchestrator — 2.5 review round 1 (pass) rulings
- **Lua-VM exemption accepted.** The engine's guarded ticks see only `luaM_realloc_` frames (≤ 64 B, ~1 per 75–160 ticks), capped by `LuaConfig::engine()` (2 MiB); Wasm is not exempt. The spec allocation test classifies by call stack independently of `ExemptScope`. The VM memory is capped but not pre-reserved; pre-reserving the call-info list in `pin_script` is a follow-up (HUMAN_QUEUE).
- **Cabinet edges in the same tick (review N2) accepted.** Edges from inputs drained in step 1 reach the script in step 2 of the same tick; edges produced by physics in step 4/5 reach it the next tick. This is the lowest-latency reading of §2.2 that keeps the order deterministic, and it is tested.
- **Polish round** for N1, N3, N5, N7, N8 (no behaviour change). N4, N6, N9 → HUMAN_QUEUE for 6.0.

## 2.5 — review polish (N1, N3, N5, N7, N8)
**Decision:**
- **N7, accepted deviation:** `Recorder::recording(&self)` returns `&Recording`, where the amended brief pins `-> Recording`. A borrow is cheaper, and callers that need ownership can call `.clone()` or `Recorder::finish`.
- **N3:** a recording may declare at most `replay::MAX_TICKS` = 2³² ticks (about 49.7 days of simulated time). `Recording::from_bytes` (after the checksum) and `replay()` refuse more with `ReplayError::TooManyTicks`, so a valid 52-byte file declaring `u64::MAX` ticks cannot run forever. The file format is unchanged.
- **N8:** `replay()` hashes the bundle's files and compares the hash with the recording before building physics or running `on_init`. A wrong bundle is reported as `TableMismatch` even if it would not load; the check after load is kept. Replay uses the default engine script limits (`SimConfig::default()` with the recorded seed). The limits are not stored in the recording, because v1 carries only the seed and the engine limits are fixed by ruling; a run recorded with other `SimConfig` limits is documented as not replayable.
- **N1:** the Lua `ExemptScope` covers only the script calls (`on_switch_edge`, `on_tick`), not the flipper edge handling between them.
- **N5:** `Simulation`'s rustdoc now states the measured Lua behaviour, matching "2.5 — allocation guard and the Lua arena".
**Rationale:** review of 2.5. Stepping behaviour does not change, and the fixture hash is unchanged.

## 5.2 — fix round 1 (review B1, N1, N3, N4)

- **B1 (bloom energy at odd sizes).** The 13-tap bilinear downsample used `uv = frag / floor(src/2)`, which stretched the sample lattice on odd sizes so that alternate source pixels fed nothing or double into the bloom. **Decision:** bloom resampling works on texel indices with `textureLoad` and explicit weights: level sizes are ceil(n/2); the 1-D kernel is binomial [1 5 10 10 5 1]/32 at offsets −2..3 around the texel pair (2i, 2i+1), separable (36 taps down, 9 taps up = the transposed kernel ×2).
