The kernel's even and odd taps each sum to 1/2, so with stride 2 every source texel feeds the same total whatever its position or the size's parity (energy), and every output texel's weights sum to 1 (a uniform image stays uniform). Measured (`bloom_is_energy_conserving_at_every_position_and_odd_size`, one 41-luminance pixel on black, firefly clamp off, 7 positions covering every parity on 127×93, 96×64, 1365×767 and 1919×1079): total luminance −0.08 … −0.17 % (f16 rounding), halo / (intensity · 40) 0.967–0.974 at every interior position, spread < 0.001 per size (the rest of the scattered light lands back on the source pixel). **Alternatives:** keep bilinear taps at `uv = 2·frag/src` (still needs special odd-edge handling; texel weights are easier to prove).
- **N1 (borders).** Outside the image each level is mirrored about the image edge, located on that level's grid (`level_edge`: full size / 2ˡ; the mirror pivots on the nearest texel boundary or texel centre). An edge is exact for both energy and uniform images only while the image size stays even through the bloom levels that reach it (single pixel in the corner or next to such an edge: −0.5 % of the scattered part, f16). Where a level's size turns odd, the edge of the deeper levels falls a quarter texel off their grid, so a bright pixel next to that edge is off by up to ~±30 % of its scattered part, smaller on large targets (measured: −28.5 % in the far corner of 97×65; +8.8 % / −3.6 % on 127×93, −12.9 % / −7.4 % on 1365×767, +9.3 % / +24.2 % on 1919×1079 next to / in the far corner). Common display sizes are affected too, along the edge whose size turns odd deeper down: 1920×1080 +2.0 % along the bottom edge (1080 → 540 → 270 → 135), 1366×768 +4.5 % on the right edge (1366 → 683). A 2×2 bright block on such an edge has the same relative error as a single pixel. At ±30 % × intensity 0.15 that is ≤ ≈4.5 % of the pixel's above-threshold light; the residual is a smooth function of position (no flicker), and uniform images stay exact everywhere. The old clamp-to-edge gain (+21 % for a 4×4 corner spot, +33.5 % for a bright last column) is gone. Accepted and logged. **Alternatives:** per-source renormalised weights (exact energy at every edge, but a uniform image darkens or brightens by up to 14 % at the borders, which spec `bloom_of_a_uniform_image_is_the_image` rejects); padding to a multiple of 2ᴸ (same quarter-texel misalignment).
- **N4 (error scope).** `PostChain::new` now pops its validation scope after the six pipelines and the render targets are created (before, only the shader module was covered).
- **N3 (probe identity), with the Orchestrator ruling "staleness changes sampling only".** Slots stay owned by list index. A slot's capture is *stale* for its ball when the ball at that index moved more than `TRACK_MAX_JUMP_M` = 0.25 m since the last frame, or the nearest ball of the last frame to it had another index (a drained ball shifted the list, or two balls swapped). A ball with a stale or missing capture samples the captured probe nearest to its current position (ties → lower slot); the next capture of its slot makes it fresh. Capture order never looks at positions (Orchestrator ruling after Tester D5): a cyclic cursor over the ball indices, the next `budget` indices after the last captured one, modulo the current count; a count change keeps the cursor. (An oldest-first order tried in this round was not a round robin for counts that are not a multiple of the budget: 3 balls / budget 2 gave [0,1],[2,0],[1,0].) Test `odd_ball_counts_share_the_budget_cyclically`: n = 1..16, shares differ by ≤ 1 over every window of n frames, ages < ⌈n/2⌉, the cursor survives 7 → 5 balls. `ages()` are frames since the ball's own slot was captured. Tests: `draining_ball_does_not_hand_its_probe_to_the_next_ball`, proptest `probes_follow_balls_through_count_changes` (random arrivals/departures: budget every frame, a ball samples its own slot only while it is fresh and captured at its own place, otherwise the nearest capture; ages < ⌈n/b⌉ once steady). An intermediate version of this round tracked balls by position and let moved balls jump the queue; the Tester's D3 caught the starvation and the ruling above replaced it. **Alternatives:** ball ids in the snapshot (a `pin_core` contract change, refused mid-wave).
- **Goldens.** `tests/fixtures/post/bloom_spot.png` re-blessed after the analytic bloom tests passed (the new kernel: mean |Δ| 0.44/255 against the old golden, 8-bit energy −1.1 %). The Tester re-blessed the four spec scene snapshots after the analytic checks.

## Orchestrator — 5.2 probe capture order: cyclic cursor (Tester D5)
"Oldest capture first, ties → lower index" favours low indices when the ball count is not a multiple of the budget (3 balls / budget 2: ball 0 every frame). **Ruling:** captures take the next `budget` indices after the last captured one, modulo the current ball count; the cursor survives count changes (clamped, never reset). This is the plain reading of "round-robin across balls" in the brief. Staleness still only changes which probe a ball samples.

## Orchestrator — 3.5 review round 1 rulings
- **B1** (wall-clock-flaky 2 ms assertion) → fix 1: latency asserted as the minimum over several replays of the per-replay p100, non-allocating sink, non-parallel test collection. Same hardening for the Tester's timing specs.
- **Fix 1 also:** N1 (equal-tick sender restart not resynced — correctness per protocol §6), N4 (receive-thread exceptions), N5 (incompatible DirectOutput.dll → exit 4), N6 (sanitise the untrusted table name passed to DOF, D25), N7 (map file create_new), N9 (CI coverage line).
- **N8 ratified:** `pin_audio` (default-features = false) as a `pin_app` dev-dependency for the fixture generator — tests only.
- N2, N3, N10, N12 → HUMAN_QUEUE; N11 → 6.0 brief.

## Orchestrator — 4.4 review round 1 rulings
- **B1 fonts:** the root `egui` pin may enable `default_fonts` (same version; logged by 4.4). A glyph-level analytic test is required — the bright-pixel smoke test could not see tofu.
- **B3 held inputs:** the menu state machine tracks cabinet inputs it forwarded as pressed (and non-neutral axes) and emits their releases/neutral values to the sim side when the menu opens, so 6.0 cannot wire a stuck flipper. Supersedes the "a release does nothing while open" behaviour; the Tester's spec is amended.
- **N1 config fallback:** per-field (bad value → that field's default + warning) with a one-time `config.toml.bak` copy before the first save that would drop a value. Refines the D1 ruling for wrongly typed values.
- Fix 1 also takes B2, N4, N5 (abandon a load), N6 (`box_downscale` bounds), N12. N2, N3, N7, N9, N10 → HUMAN_QUEUE.

## 2.6 — Banana flipper decisions

Code: `crates/pin_physics/src/flipper_banana/` (`arc.rs`: `CurvedBanana`), one variant in the private `Bat` enum of `flipper/mod.rs` plus its construction. No solver change. API: "2.6 — Banana flipper public API" above.

- **Decision (pivot at the start of the arc, arc tangent to the bat angle there).** The flipper's angle is the direction in which the bat leaves its pivot; the tip curls away from that line toward the concave side. **Rationale:** (1) it is how a banana flipper is built: an ordinary flipper at the shaft whose tip curls up the table; (2) the face normal then turns the same way all along the bat (by `fraction × arc angle`), which is what criterion 1 asks for ("each shifted in a consistent direction"); (3) rest and end angles keep their meaning from the straight bat, and the bat goes over into it as the radius grows. **Alternative rejected:** the chord on the bat axis (pivot and tip on angle 0, the arc sagging between them): the face normal turns one way inboard of the middle and the other way outboard, so the release-angle shift against the straight bat changes sign along the bat.
- **Decision (`length_mm` and `arc_radius_mm` fix the arc; only the sign of `arc_sweep_deg` is read).** The contract has three numbers for a two-parameter arc, and existing fixtures are inconsistent (`length 80, radius 100, sweep 30°` in the `pin_core` tests and the 2.3 spec test: that chord on that radius subtends 47.2°). Criterion 2 varies the radius "at equal length", so chord and radius are primary and the arc angle is `2·asin(length / 2R)`. The sign of `arc_sweep_deg` chooses the bend: `≥ 0` concave face leading the swing, `< 0` convex face leading. The leading side follows the sign of `sweep_angle_deg`, so mirror-image flippers carry the same shape values. **Alternatives:** rejecting inconsistent triples (rejects the contract's own fixtures); using radius and sweep and ignoring the length (no limit at equal length). HUMAN_QUEUE asks whether a later contract change should drop the redundancy or have the table validator warn about it.
- **Decision (constant tube radius, flat top and bottom, round end caps).** The contract has one `tube_radius_mm`. The outline is every point within that radius of the centreline arc, extruded from the mounting plane to `bat_height_m` like `StraightCapsule` (a rolling ball of any size is struck horizontally). Limits: `arc_radius ≥ length/2` (at most a half circle, so the tip is the farthest point and `reach = length + tube`), `arc_radius > tube_radius` (the concave face exists).
- **Decision (closest point in `f64`, written without cancellation).** Distance to the centreline is `(ρ² − R²)/(ρ + R)` with `ρ² − R² = x² + y² − 2yR` in the arc's own frame, and the wedge test uses the tip's tangent instead of angles. **Rationale:** with a large radius the distance is a small difference of large numbers; in `f32` the bat would roughen by micrometres (the cast's skin is 10 µm) long before it is straight. With this form a radius of 1e9 m (and `f32::MAX`) gives the straight capsule's distance to 0.1 µm. `libm` only; no allocation.
- **Decision (surface velocity and swept test are the `BatShape` defaults).** `surface_velocity = ω ẑ × r` and `cast_sphere` = conservative advancement evaluated in the bat frame, stepping by gap / (ball speed + pivot speed + `|ω|·reach`), are what deliverables 1 and 2 ask for and what 2.3 prepared for any rigid shape; both hold for a concave outline because they rely only on an exact outside distance and a true `reach`. parry is not involved. Overriding the cast with a second copy of the same loop would only let the two bats drift apart. Proven for this shape: balls at 1–80 m/s onto the concave face, the convex face and the tip cap of bats of 50/100/300 mm radius; bats up to 355 rad/s (29.8 m/s at the tip) for both bends; a proptest over bend, side, speed, direction, timing, coil, inertia, mounting plane and spin (1,500 cases run).
- **Verification numbers (Appendix C defaults, 76 mm chord, 8 mm tube, standard ball, 6.5°; `tests/banana_criteria.rs`).**
  1. *Release angles* of a ball lying against the resting bat, flipped: straight (76 mm, r 8) 91.1° / 88.8° / 80.0° at 25 / 50 / 85 %. Banana R 100 mm (arc 44.7°): 108.8° / 114.6° / 123.0°, shifts +17.6° / +25.9° / +42.9°; R 60: 120.6 / 133.4 / 146.7; R 80: 112.9 / 121.5 / 132.8; R 130: 105.0 / 108.4 / 113.9. Monotonic (also in 10 % steps from 20 % to 90 % for R ≤ 100), every shift the way the tip curls, larger farther out and for a tighter bend. The mirror-image flipper mirrors the shots. **Finding:** the straight bat's own spread runs the other way (later contacts release at smaller angles), so for slight bends the two trends cancel and the spread is flat and not monotonic in fine steps near R ≈ 200 mm (100.5 / 101.0 / 101.4 / 103.9 / 103.7 / 102.3° from 25 % to 85 %); the shift against the straight bat keeps its sign for every radius tried (60 mm … 1 m).
  2. *Convergence* of the 25/50/85 % shots to the straight capsule's (worst of the three): release angle off by 3.94° at R = 1 m, 1.95° at 2 m, 0.97° at 4 m, 0.48° at 8 m, 0.24° at 16 m, 0.12° at 32 m: order 1.00 in 1/R (the tip is off by chord²/2R). Stated tolerance: angle ≤ 70°·chord/R + 0.05°, exit speed ≤ 1.5 m/s·chord/R + 0.005 m/s; from R = 500 m on, within 0.05° and 0.005 m/s (the floor is a contact event falling into a neighbouring sub-step: 0.02° at isolated radii). At R = 1e9 m the surface is the capsule's within 0.1 µm and the timing-sweep rebounds agree to 4 digits.
  3. *Cradle.* (a) Bat raised to +20° with the 2.3 inlane guide: the ball rests where the guide meets the concave face; 0.0 µm moved in 2,000 ticks. (b) No guide: a bat raised to −15° (R 100) or −20° (R 70) holds the ball in its own pocket, where the face is level (0.354 of the arc against 0.336 level; 0.330 against 0.304: the difference is rolling resistance); < 1 µm in 2,000 ticks, bat on its end stop throughout.
  4. *Timing sweep* (±20 ms, time 0 = contact with the held bat, 3.5 m/s). Ball square onto the face, rebound = speed after contact: dead spot 31.4 % / 13.4 % of held at 50 % / 70 % for R 100; 32.2 / 3.6 % (R 70); 31.2 / 15.0 % (R 120); 31.1 / 14.8 % (R 200); straight bat on the same rig 28.9 / 14.9 %. The 2.3 rig as it is (ball straight down the table, rebound = speed up the table): held 0.38 / 0.40 m/s, minimum 0, maximum 3.4 / 3.6 m/s. Every step above 0.75 m/s is named (rigid-stop event or hit/miss of a rebounding ball); none unexplained. The 25 % depth (Gate A1's) is met at 70 % and not at 50 %, as on the straight bat.
- **Other.** Allocation guard over 2,990 ticks with three bananas (zipper, mirror, tilted Thing Flip with the convex face leading), a straight bat and three balls: 0 allocations; the same scenario is bit-identical over 10 runs (`tests/banana_alloc.rs`). Mirror flippers play mirror shots only to the solver's own mirror accuracy (0.03 mm / 0.06 m/s for a straight bat after 25 ms: 210° and −30° do not round alike), so that proptest allows 0.5 mm / 0.1 m/s.

## 2.6 — criterion 4 on an oblique face (Tester findings D1, D2)

Tester's rig: Appendix C defaults, R 120 mm banana, ball 200 mm up the table coming straight down at 3.5 m/s onto the centreline point at 70 % of the raised bat, rebound = the ball's speed when it has cleared the bat by 15 mm. Reproduced digit for digit on the Implementer's rig (`tests/banana_dead_spot.rs`): held 2.438 m/s, minimum 1.676 m/s at −20 ms = 68.7 %.

- **D1 — Finding: emergent, not a defect; nothing was changed or tuned.** That ball does not arrive square on a curved bat. Measured at the first impact of the dead flip (normal from `closest_point`):

  | bat | incidence off the normal | in: normal / along the face | out: normal / along the face | min / held |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | banana R 120, 70 % | 37.8° | 2.76 / 2.15 m/s | 0.53 / 1.59 m/s | 1.676 / 2.438 = 68.7 % |
  | straight (same length, r 8), 70 % | 15.5° | 3.37 / 0.94 | 0.58 / 0.80 | 0.991 / 3.474 = 28.5 % |
  | straight, flipper turned by 0.7 × arc angle (same face normal as the banana's 70 %) | 41.3° | 2.63 / 2.31 | 0.19 / 1.69 | 1.703 / 2.285 = 74.5 % |

  1. The bat's momentum can only act along the contact normal. The dead flip cancels that part alike on both bats: 0.53 m/s left of 2.76 on the banana, 0.58 of 3.37 on the straight bat.
  2. The part along the face is untouched by the cancellation. A ball that slides in without spin and is gripped by the rubber rolls on at 5/7 of it: 5/7 × 2.15 = 1.53 m/s predicted, 1.59 measured (straight: 0.67 predicted, 0.80 measured). That part alone is 63 % of the banana's held rebound, so by a speed measure the 50 % gate cannot be met at this incidence whatever the bat does along its normal; and the held rebound, the denominator, is itself smaller on the oblique face (2.44 against 3.47 m/s).
  3. A **straight capsule turned to the same contact normal** gives the same shallow curve (74.5 %, exit 0.19 / 1.69 m/s), so the depth follows the incidence, not the curvature, the concave-face code path, the surface velocity or the effective mass.
  4. With the ball **square onto the same face** (same bat, same contact point, same flips) the dead spot at 70 % is 15.0 % of held for the R 120 banana against 14.9 % for the straight bat (0.514 of 3.431 m/s; 0.492 of 3.295 m/s).
  5. No contact is missed or doubled: one impact at the dead flip, and the solver-level checks (surface velocity = ω ẑ × r, normals against brute force, two-body energy bound, no tunnelling) pass in both the Implementer's and the Tester's suites.

  `banana_dead_spot::an_oblique_face_leaves_the_tangential_part_of_a_dead_ball` asserts 1–3. **For the Orchestrator's ruling:** the "≤ 50 % of held at 50 % and 70 %" gate presumes a ball that meets the face near its normal (true for a straight bat at +20° under a ball coming down the table: 15.5°). Options: gate the curved bat with the ball square onto the face (met: 3.6–15 % at 70 %, 31–32 % at 50 % for R 70…200), or gate the normal component of the rebound, or move the 70 % down-table figure to Gate A1 as a reported number. Tuning the solver so that a rubber bat also stops a ball's motion along its face would be wrong physics.
- **D2 — the 0.817 m/s step between −9 and −8 ms at 70 % is a hit-or-miss at a rigid stop.** Both runs: first impact with the bat in mid stroke (ticks 55 / 56, 7.8 m/s closing; the bat is knocked back to −54 rad/s), second impact when the re-powered bat comes back (ticks 67 / 68), after which the bat chases the ball up the stroke. Flip −9: the bat reaches its end stop in tick 75 with the ball 0.37 mm ahead of it and never touches it again (2 impacts, 1.851 m/s). Flip −8: one tick behind in its stroke, it reaches the ball in tick 75 at 17.9°, before the stop (third impact at 1.09 m/s closing, 2.668 m/s). The Tester's classifier counted contact *episodes* separated by 3 mm of clearance; the third impact follows the second within 1.6 mm, so both runs showed "2 episodes". Counted as the solver reports impacts it is 2 against 3, and the stop ends the chase in one run only: both of the ruling's named causes. `banana_dead_spot::every_step_of_the_down_table_sweep_has_a_cause` names every step above 0.75 m/s of the Tester's rig at 50 % and 70 % and pins this one.

## 4.4 — review round 1 (Fable review B1–B3, N1, N4–N6, N8, N12)

**Decision:**
- **B1 fonts.** Root `Cargo.toml` egui pin becomes `egui = { version = "=0.37.0", features = ["default_fonts"] }` (Orchestrator ruling; adds `epaint_default_fonts 0.37.0`, OFL/Ubuntu-font licensed, fetched from crates.io). Without it every glyph was a placeholder box. New GPU test `ui_overlay_gpu::text_renders_distinct_glyphs`: "oooo" vs "xxxx" must differ in ≥ 25 % of the lit pixels, and "i i i" at 48 pt must give exactly 6 bright 8-connected blobs (a box per glyph gives 3).
- **B2 scrolling.** `MenuView` remembers `(panel, focus)` of the previous frame and calls `scroll_to_me` only on the frame the focus changed (and on reopening), so mouse-wheel scrolling sticks. Test `ui_view::wheel_scrolling_is_not_undone_by_the_focus` (60 tables, 1280×720, 5 × Line −5, 40 frames).
- **B3 held inputs.** The state machine tracks buttons forwarded pressed and axes forwarded ≠ 0.0 and emits `UiCommand::ReleaseToSim { buttons, axes }` just before `MenuOpened` (ruling: 6.0 must not remember this). Neutral is 0.0 for every axis (plunger rest, no nudge, flipper button up). Releases while open go to the menu; nothing is re-pressed on close.
- **N1 per-field fallback.** After the TOML parses, each known top-level value is checked alone; a failing known table is checked key by key (two levels deep), and only the failing leaves are dropped with `InvalidValue { key }`. Unknown keys are untouched. `load` copies the original to `<name>.bak` whenever content was dropped, reset, corrupt or too large (overwriting an older `.bak`; a failed copy is ignored), so the next `save` cannot lose the user's file.
- **N4** `Corrupt` / `InvalidValue` parser texts are cut at 512 characters (`config::MAX_WARNING_CHARS`, `…` appended).
- **N5** Each accepted load request gets an id; `report_load_result_for(id, …)` rejects stale reports; `cancel_load()` abandons a load. `report_load_result` (no id) still applies to whatever is pending.
- **N6** `box_downscale` is private and returns an empty 0×0 image for a slice that does not match `w·h·4`.
- **N8 portrait.** Table list + details and input settings + live readings stack vertically when the body is narrower than 700 points; badges never wrap inside themselves (`TextWrapMode::Extend`, the row wraps between badges). Layout test at 1080×1920 @1.5 checks the details sit below the list and badges are single lines.
- **N12** `MenuState::new` sanitizes like `set_config`.
- **Found while fixing (layout, visible in the review PNGs).** (a) The side-by-side columns were laid out with the parent's *horizontal* layout, so table rows sat in one line and the list could not scroll vertically (this, not only the focus snap, is why the wheel did nothing); columns are now fixed-size top-down UIs. (b) egui's `Slider::step_by` anchors its grid at the range minimum and re-snapped the 100 ms release window to 101 on every frame, emitting an edit that stole the focus; sliders no longer use `step_by` (integer rows use `integer()`), and `ui_view` asserts that drawing alone produces no inputs over several frames. (c) Badges are plain labels with a background colour, so the row wraps between them; `ui_view` asserts no text crosses the panel's inner margin.
- **Review images** (D30 c): `PIN_UI_SNAPSHOT_DIR=<dir> cargo test -p pin_app --test ui_snapshots` (needs a GPU adapter) writes `tables|display|input_{1024x768,portrait}.png`.

**Rationale:** Orchestrator rulings for B1–B3; the rest are cheap fixes that remove data loss or a hang.

**Alternatives:** queue releases in `route_cabinet`'s return value (would need a new return type; the command stream keeps one channel for 6.0); embed a font file in the repo (not needed, crates.io reachable).

## 2.8 — large balls: margins, flipper rim and validator choices (Implementer)

- **Decision (deliverable 1).** `jumbo_xl_resin` (79.4 mm) already exists in `pin_core::profiles` and `pin_assets` validation resolves profiles by name, so nothing in `pin_assets` changed. Any diameter 15.88–79.4 mm runs through `BallSolver::new` (tests build profiles of arbitrary diameter, mass ∝ d³).
- **~~Decision (deliverable 3: no change to `ball/` or `world/`).~~ Superseded by fix round 1 (Reviewer polish N6).** The round-0 audit (all CCD/broad-phase margins already radius-relative: sub-step travel `TRAVEL_FRACTION · r`, `2r` path padding, BVH queries by `r + LEAF_MARGIN`; µm contact tolerances tied to f32 precision) still holds for the static world, but `ball/` did change: see the correction below (`refine_substeps`, the cycling friction cut).
- **Correction (fix round 1, Reviewer B1) — ball-vs-moving-body overlap.** My round-0 explanation ("bat rim riding, cause in `flipper/`") was wrong. Two causes, both in the 2.2-S seam (`ball/`), found by logging every `MovingBodies` call during the bad ticks: (1) **the sub-step grid is planned at tick start** from each body's current `max_surface_speed`; a resting bat (speed 0) that an impact sets spinning (≈ 950 rad/s with a 5e-5 kg·m² bat) then sweeps a whole sub-step of `r/4`-scaled length between the ball's boundaries, and the boundaries are where a touching, non-closing body contact is solved (the casts leave it) — so the overlap grew with the radius (XL 2.9 mm, 27 mm 0.26 mm at 445 g). Fix: `BallSolver::refine_substeps` — after a body changes its trajectory, a ball **larger than 13.5 mm radius** whose remaining grid is too coarse for the bodies near it now gets a finer grid for the rest of the tick (body + ball travel ≤ `TRAVEL_FRACTION · r` per sub-step; the boundary grid becomes `t0 + k·h`, `t0 = 0` unless refined, so every no-refinement path is bit-identical). (2) **a normal/friction cycle** in `solve_body_contact`: on a light bat the friction impulse slows the bat's surface along the normal too, the next pass takes the normal impulse back to zero, the friction with it, and the solve cycles with period 2 until the event budget freezes the ball and the bat sweeps into it. Fix: once a contact's normal impulse has gone back to zero (`BodyContactState::cycling`), a friction change that would separate the contact beyond what the normal solve set is cut back to the part that does not (`FRICTION_SEPARATION_TOLERANCE` 1e-5 m/s). Contacts that never drop to zero are solved exactly as before. Results (light bat 3 N·m / 5e-5 kg·m², 445 g balls, random approaches): worst tick-end overlap 27 mm 0.223 mm, 50 mm 0.102 mm (was 0.81), 79.4 mm 0.227 mm (was 2.87); spec seed `c3_no_tunnelling_xl_vs_flipper_random` passes; 2.2-S seam digests and every pin_physics test unchanged (balls ≤ 13.5 mm radius never refine; the friction cut only acts on cycling contacts). An unconditional friction cut was tried first and changed 2.3's dead-spot sweep and 2.6's banana tests, so it was made conditional. **Scope of bit-identity (Reviewer polish N2):** for balls of radius ≤ 13.5 mm only the *sub-step grid* is unchanged; the cycling friction cut is not radius-gated — in the Reviewer's free-space sweep (3 N·m, 5e-5 kg·m² bat, 7680 runs per row) it changed 1 run with the 27 mm / 80 g ball and 8 with 27 mm / 445 g (exit speed +2–9 %), while the 2.2-S seam digests and all 2.3/2.4/2.6/2.7 suites are unchanged. Also, once refinement applies, `BallSolverConfig::max_substeps` is no longer a hard per-tick cap for that ball: each refinement adds up to `max_substeps − n_sub + left` boundaries (bounded; about 4× at 200 m/s). **Alternatives:** a radius-independent sub-step length for bodies at tick start (did not help: the bat is at rest then); scaling `TOUCH_TOL`/skins with `r` (moves digests, not the cause).
- **Note (Reviewer N6, world constants sized for small balls).** `world/wireform.rs` `MIN_SLOPED_GUIDE_REACH` (20 mm), `world/curve_bounds.rs` `BALL_RANGE` (0.1 m) and `LANE_SNUG` (0.04 m) are search bounds and tolerances of the guide closest-point / reach machinery, not collision margins: beyond them the code falls back to the slower global search ("exact whenever p is within the wall's reach … beyond it a search of the curve's segments"), so a large ball is still correct but its casts against tight sloped guides take smaller steps (more events per sub-step). Not measured for the XL ball on a tight sloped guide this round; 4.1-H lint (c) already warns when a declared profile's radius exceeds a sloped guide's tightest radius of curvature.
- **Decision (restriction areas, CR-005).** See "2.8 — Clearance validator public API". **Rationale:** criterion 2 needs "the main deck's open area"; layer 0 runs under the deck, so a layer list cannot say it. **Alternatives:** model the under-deck floor as its own layer (impossible for generated tables: one floor mesh); a separate clearance manifest (a second source of truth).
- **Decision (scoop = pocket geometry, not the VUK `radius`).** The 2.4 `radius` is the capture radius of the ball centre (default 12 mm), so reading it as a hole would reject the standard ball on every default VUK; the validator measures the pocket walls around the hold point instead, and checks the exit point against the exit layer. **Alternatives:** a new `MechDef` key for the opening (breaks the `MechDef` struct literals in `spec_2_4_api.rs`).
- **Decision (wireform rule).** Rails are lines; the ball's contact angle from vertical must be 20°–75° (`sin α = g/d`): below 20° it perches (rolls off at 0.36 g lateral), above 75° it nearly drops through. With 19 mm gauge: 27 mm 44.7° ok, 15.88 mm drops, 79.4 mm 13.8° rejected.


## Orchestrator — 4.4 `.bak` timing accepted
The 4.4 contract makes `config.toml.bak` when a file with a dropped value is loaded, not "before the first save" as the round-1 ruling said. Accepted: the backup exists before any save could overwrite the original; loading a bad file therefore writes one sibling file.

## Orchestrator — 2.6 criterion 4: gated at square incidence (Tester D1)
**Evidence (2.6 Implementer, "2.6 — criterion 4 on an oblique face"):** in the Tester's rig the ball meets the 70 % point of the curved face 37.8° off its normal. The normal component dies as on the straight bat (0.53 vs 0.58 m/s left); the tangential component keeps rolling at ~5/7 of its incoming value, which alone is 63 % of the held rebound. A straight capsule turned to the same contact normal gives 74.5 %. With the ball square onto the banana face the dead spot is 15.0 % (straight: 14.9 %). D2 (0.817 m/s step) is a hit-or-miss at the rigid stop (ball 0.37 mm ahead of the stopped bat vs caught at 17.9°), named by a test.
**Ruling:** for a curved bat, 2.6 criterion 4 is gated as the 2.3 sweep is — ball square onto the face at the contact point (incidence within the straight rig's ~15°): dead spot ≤ 50 % at 50 % and 70 %, every step > 0.75 m/s named. Oblique-incidence figures (e.g. the 68.7 % down-table case) are reported and handed to Gate A1 with the 25 % depth. This is the same physics test as 2.3, not a relaxation for the banana.

## Orchestrator — 5.3 review round 1 rulings
B1 (LCD image larger than `max_texture_dimension_2d` panics via wgpu's default handler; reachable from untrusted bundle art, D25) → fix 1, with N1a–c (malformed `RgbaImage` panics), N2 (hostile scene compose 70–431 ms per frame: clip glyph/score loops to the canvas), N4 (`set_image` leak on reload), N7 (`mark_dirty` on DMD/LCD for symmetry), N8 (checked size maths in `RgbaImage::new`; document mask premultiplication). `pin_assetgen` as a runtime dependency of `pin_render` accepted (light, acyclic; fonts are code per PLAN 4.6). N3, N5, N6, N9 → HUMAN_QUEUE.

## 5.3 — fix round 1 (review B1, N1, N2, N4, N7, N8)

- **B1/N1/N8 (image validation).** One check in the quad compositor (`check_image`) guards every GPU upload:
  sides 1..=`max_texture_dimension_2d`, exact byte length. Errors are returned, not logged-and-skipped,
  so the loader can report the bad file; a refused LCD replacement keeps the previous texture. CPU
  composition (DMD and LCD aspect) skips invalid images. `RgbaImage::pixel` never indexes past the buffer.
- **N2 (bounded composition).** DMD text visits only the clipped part of the text box, rasterises only the
  characters reaching the clip and fills lit runs per row; rects fill clipped runs; images sample a
  precomputed texel column table; score layers lay out in the layer box ∩ display. Worst parseable scenes
  (64 layers, 4 × 4-display boxes, 256-char texts), opt-level 1 test profile, 192 × 64: text 0.7 ms,
  8×16 0.9 ms, score 1.7 ms, half-transparent full-screen images 3.3 ms; 512 × 256: 3.5–36 ms (images).
  The image case is the per-dot blend floor (64 full-screen alpha layers); not reduced further. Printed by
  `displays_cpu::hostile_scenes_compose_with_bounded_work` (no wall-clock assertion, D8).
- **N4.** `LcdRenderer::set_image` on a known file replaces the texture in place and drops the cached bind
  groups that used it.
- **N7.** `mark_dirty` on the DMD, LCD and score renderers.

## Orchestrator — 2.6 review B1 is a 2.3 defect; fixed under 2.6
`flipper::plane_axes` (2.3, Wave 4) uses a fixed frame for normals with z < −0.999999, which is not orthonormal for normals up to ~0.08° off −Z (closest-point back-error ~0.1 mm, distance error up to 1.5e-5 m > BODY_SKIN); it affects straight bats too. The 2.6 Implementer (Fable, also the 2.3 implementer) gets ownership of that fallback for the fix; the 2.6 Reviewer (Opus) re-reviews. Gate A1 note from the review: the timing-sweep depth depends on incidence for straight bats as well (straight bat at 50 % fails the 50 % gate from β ≈ 25°); Gate A1 should state the incidence it gates at.

## GR-1 — flaky 2.4/2.7 proptests
Both failures are deterministic properties of the inputs, not of the build: the coverage run only happened to draw them; proptest saved both seeds (`mech_props.proptest-regressions` `6df1b90f…`, `spec_2_7_magnus.proptest-regressions` `7354f3a1…`), so since then each test fails on **every** run. Debug, release and `cargo llvm-cov` builds give bit-identical numbers (plunger launches 3.0983646 = `0x40464b9b` at depth 0.7304841 and 3.0887496 = `0x4045ae13` at 0.75067747; Magnus |v|² 12.443253168280549 vs 12.442924994886667), so class (c) is excluded: there is no fast-math and no FMA contraction, and the instrumentation does not change float results. Neither failure is a physics defect (class a). Both are class (b).
- **2.4 `mech_props::plunger_launch_is_monotone`: the property is wrong for its inputs.** The test pulls the rod over 0.2 s and releases 0.1 s later. Beyond depth ≈ 0.31 the rod outruns the ball, which rolls back down the 6.5° lane at ~0.8 m/s². At release the ball is still rolling or bouncing (for depth ≥ 0.71 it never touches the tip again before release: v_y = −0.233 m/s, gap 1.6 / 2.6 mm at the failing depths). The `mech` module docs already say the launch then depends on the tick phase of the contact, because the rod's velocity is constant within a tick and the spring changes it by ~1 m/s per tick near full pull. They also say the launch is monotone only with the ball resting on the tip. A scan of 1901 depths on 0.05..1 with the old timing: all 527 depths at which the ball rests on the tip at release (0.05–0.313) are strictly monotone. The others have 7+ reversals, the worst 4.7 % (0.600 → 0.601), so about 4.5e-4 of the test's pairs fail (≈ 0.7 % per 16-case run before the seed was saved). **Decision:** the test checks the documented property. The ball is held until tick 1500, 1.1 s after the pull ends, so that it rolls back and settles on the tip. With that timing all 1901 depths rest on the tip and are strictly monotone; with release at tick 1000 all of them already rest, at tick 900 326 do not. The test now asserts its premise too: the rod is at full pull, and the ball is in contact (|gap| < 0.1 mm) with |v| < 1 mm/s. The physics is unchanged, so no trajectory and no seam digest changes. **Alternatives:** sub-stepping the rod inside a tick, or an exact harmonic rod segment. Either would reduce the phase effect but changes the 2.2-S slider seam (one constant velocity per segment) and every sling and kickback, which is out of scope for a fix task. The in-flight case is left as the documented model limitation (≤ ~5 % reversals measured). A weaker tolerance property for it was not added, because a new tolerance-based proptest would be the next flake.
- **2.7 `spec_2_7_magnus::prop_free_flight` (Tester file, not edited): the kinetic-energy bound is wrong.** The bound `ke_bound = 2n(S/m·|ω|·dt)² + n·ε` allows one f32 rounding of |v|² per **tick** (n = 200). The solver takes `n_sub = ceil(|v|·dt / (0.25 r))` sub-steps per tick and adds the hook's increment to the f32 velocity once per sub-step. Each add can change |v|² by up to ε·|v|² (0.5 ulp per component), so the worst case is N_sub·ε. Minimal input: s = 2.7939423e-6, v = (−2.2705288, 1.0387479, 2.4917116), ω = (−171.76329, 125.194756, 150.64848), which = 0 (steel, m = 0.08, r = 13.5 mm), gravity = false. |v| = 3.53 m/s gives 2 sub-steps per tick, N_sub = 400, and the relative change is 2.637e-5 = 1.106·(200 ε) but only 0.553·(400 ε). The rounding is correlated because the per-sub-step increment is almost constant (φ = 4.5e-6 rad, ~38 ε·|v|), so it accumulates close to the worst case rather than as a random walk. The hook is optimal here: an f64 exact rotation increment, rounded to f32 and added in f32, gives a bit-identical drift (2.6374296559441275e-5), and with no Magnus the drift is exactly 0. The excess is therefore f32 storage rounding of the velocity. It is not work done by the Magnus term and not a hook error. Sweep of 20 000 random spec-distributed cases (gravity off, 200 ticks): the current bound fails 17 (0.085 %, i.e. ~2 % per 48-case run). (|ΔK/K| − quad)/(N_sub·ε) is at most 0.774. **Required Tester change:** in the gravity-off branch, use `2n(S/m·|ω|·dt)² + N_sub·ε`, with N_sub counted black-box by a registered counting `ExternalForce` hook that returns `Vec3::splat(-0.0)` (the solver calls it once per sub-step and adds nothing), or conservatively `n·max_substeps·ε`. Then remove the saved seed `7354f3a1…` only if the Tester wants to; with the corrected bound it passes.

## Orchestrator — 2.8 review round 1 rulings
B1 (XL–bat tick-end overlap grows with radius without rim contact, contradicting the "rim riding" explanation; a Tester spec now fails on a saved seed) is a deliverable-3 question until the cause is located → fix 1, seed file kept. B2 (deck headroom tested at the ball centre only; false accepts near deck edges and under sloped ceilings) → fix 1 with a resting-sphere test. Also fix 1: N1 (plan-distance lane width; disc not centre in area tests), N2, N4, N5, N6 (doc), N8. N3, N7 → HUMAN_QUEUE.

## 2.7 — spec prop_free_flight bound counts sub-steps (GR-1)
**Decision:** the kinetic-energy bound of `spec_2_7_magnus.rs` (`ke_bound`, used by `prop_free_flight` gravity-off and `v2_kinetic_energy_unchanged_by_magnus`) is now `2n·φ² + N_sub·ε·(1 + 8φ)`, with `φ = S|ω|·dt/m`, `n` ticks and `N_sub` the ball sub-steps of that run. `N_sub` is counted black-box by a `SubstepCounter` hook registered after the 2.7 hooks; it returns `Vec3::splat(-0.0)`. `prop_free_flight` also asserts `n ≤ N_sub ≤ n·max_substeps`. New tests: `substep_counter_does_not_change_the_run` (48 runs, bit-identical with and without the counter, with signed-zero velocities, gravity on and off, each registration) and `gr1_fast_ball_energy_drift_is_within_the_substep_bound` (GR-1's shrunk input as a fixed case). The saved seed `7354f3a1…` is kept and passes. No file outside the spec was edited.
**Rationale:** verified black-box (Tester, without reading `ball/` or `ball_ext/` sources; the `ExternalForce` rustdoc states one `force_in` call per ball sub-step, added as `F·h/m`). GR-1's input (steel, s = 2.7939423e-6, |v| = 3.53 m/s) makes 400 force calls in 200 ticks; relative |v|² change 2.6374e-5 = 1.106·(200 ε) = 0.553·(400 ε); with Magnus off the change is exactly 0. One f32 add `v_i ← fl(v_i + d_i)` is off by ≤ ε|v_i|/2, so it changes |v|² by ≤ ε|v|²: ε per add, N_sub·ε per run; the increments of successive sub-steps are nearly equal, so the errors are correlated and approach the worst case. The `8φ` term covers rounding of the increment itself (an f32 cross product times `h/m`: absolute error a few ε·φ|v| in any direction). Sweep of 19 999 spec-distributed cases (gravity off, 200 ticks): old bound fails 17, new bound 0, max (|ΔK/K| − 2nφ²)/(N_sub ε) = 0.948, max N_sub 800. A −0.0 force is exact for every velocity (`x + (−0.0) = x`, signed zeros included); a +0.0 force would not be for a −0.0 component.
The property stays meaningful: in the same sweep a throwaway hook `S(ω×v) + α·S|ω×v|·v̂` (does work) was flagged by the new bound in 90.8 % of cases at α = 1e-2, 68.1 % at 3e-3, 28.5 % at 1e-3 and 21.5 % at −1e-3, so a 48-case run (half gravity-off) fails with probability > 0.997 for |α| ≥ 1e-3. A force with fraction α along v changes |v|² by 2αφ per tick; the detection threshold is α ≈ N_sub·ε/(2nφ).
**Alternatives:** `n·max_substeps·ε` (max_substeps = 64): no counting hook, but 32–64× looser, which would hide a parallel component of up to ~5 %. Rejected. A looser constant such as `4n·ε`: wrong again as soon as a ball takes more than 4 sub-steps. Rejected. Comparing against an f64 rotation rounded per sub-step (bit-exact, per GR-1): would duplicate the solver's sub-step schedule in the spec; not black-box.

## 4.4 — review round 2 polish (N13–N15)

**Decision:**
- **N13** Every known table key (`backglass_pip`, `score_pip`, `calibration`, `calibration.plunger`, `calibration.nudge`, `audio`) must hold a table; anything else (including an array of tables such as `[[calibration]]`, which serde would accept for a struct) is dropped with `InvalidValue { key, "expected a table, found …" }`, the rest of the file is kept, and the file is backed up like any other lossy load.
- **N14** `load` never overwrites an existing `<name>.bak` (it keeps the oldest known-bad original; the user deletes it to get a fresh one) and never copies a file over `MAX_CONFIG_BYTES` (the `TooLarge` warning text says "not backed up"), so the backup is bounded at 1 MiB. No new `ConfigWarning` variant was added (API stability for the spec tests).
- **N15** Footer key hints use `view::HINT_TEXT` = rgb(200, 200, 205) at 0.8 × body height (was egui "small" + "weak"); contrast against the backdrop composited over white is ≈ 9:1 (test `ui_view::footer_hint_contrast`, ≥ 4.5:1). The footer reserves three text lines so the hints may wrap on narrow panels.

**Rationale:** Reviewer polish findings; no behaviour change for valid files.

**Alternatives:** a rotating `.bak.N` series (unbounded disk use); a new `BackupSkipped` warning variant (breaks exhaustive matches).
**Addendum (same file, found by the first 200 000-case run after the amendment):** `check_lateral`'s f32 resolution floor had the same per-tick count, `(4 + n)·ε·v_max/t`. It failed on s = 2.1234628e-5, v = (2.3000424, 0.55005735, −3.9588702), ω = (−105.66846, −19.719887, 181.39333), steel, gravity on, 40 ticks: error 7.73e-4 m/s² against a floor of 6.56e-4, all of it along gravity. Black-box: the same flight with no hook is already 3.9e-4 m/s² off `v0 + g·t` (80 sub-steps, the constant gravity kick rounds ~0.4 ulp the same way every add); with Magnus there are two adds per sub-step. The floor is now `(4 + N_sub)·ε·v_max/t` (two adds per sub-step, ≤ ε|v|/2 each as a vector), N_sub counted as above. The 1 % criterion itself is unchanged; for the fixed Glo-Ball cases the floor is ~0.15 % of the nominal acceleration. proptest saved the seed `52ece0b1…`; it is kept and passes.

## Wave 5 gate fix
CI run 294 (20f077c) failed five jobs. One fix each:
- **clippy (Windows): unused `Duration` in `pin_app/tests/dof_launcher.rs`.** It was used only by the `#[cfg(unix)] mod unix` process tests, so the import now lives in that module. Every other cfg block in `tests/dof_*.rs`, `tests/spec_3_5_*.rs` and `src/dof/**` was checked by hand, including uses inside and outside the cfg blocks: the only other block is `cfg(not(windows))` in `spec_3_5_launcher.rs`, and it uses nothing that is Windows-only-unused. `src/dof/launcher.rs`'s `cfg(windows)` block is self-contained.
- **core-tests (Linux): `tests/spec_ci` still asserted the 1.2 replay-hash placeholder.** `replay_hash_is_a_marked_placeholder_that_cannot_fail_on_cargo` is replaced by `replay_hash_records_and_replays_on_every_os_and_fails_only_in_machine`. It asserts the job Subphase 2.5 filled in: it runs on Windows, Linux and macOS; `cargo build --release -p pin_sim`; records with `--ticks` and `--record-inputs` for 10 000 ticks; replays with `--replay-inputs` 10 times; compares and exits non-zero on mismatch; has no `continue-on-error: true`; and sets `fail-fast: false`. Cross-OS results are report-only: no job `needs:` replay-hash, and the job says "report". A new helper, `job_text`, slices a job's YAML text, because the parsed `Job` has no env or strategy accessor. The other 19 tests are unchanged. Mutation-checked: TICKS 1000 fails it, and so does `continue-on-error: true`.
- **dof-bridge (Windows): `Garbage_on_the_socket_is_counted_and_ignored` timed out. Product defect.** When a datagram is larger than the receive buffer (1457 bytes), Windows fails `Socket.Receive` with WSAEMSGSIZE (`SocketError.MessageSize`). It still fills the buffer with the first bytes and discards the rest. Linux and macOS return the truncated length instead. `UdpBridgeHost` treated MessageSize like ConnectionReset and `continue`d, so on Windows an oversized datagram (the spec sends random junk up to 1499 bytes, then 4000 and 20 000 bytes) was neither processed nor counted. `Received` and `Stats.Rejected` disagreed across OSes, and the spec's wait for `Received >= sent` never finished. **Decision:** MessageSize now sets `n = buffer.Length` and falls through, so the core sees a datagram one byte over the maximum and rejects it, the same as on Linux. ConnectionReset (an ICMP port-unreachable, no datagram) is still skipped. The Implementer test `HostStopsCleanlyAndIgnoresOversizedDatagrams` now asserts `Rejected == 1` and `Received == 2` instead of `Rejected <= 1`. Only Windows CI exercises the WSAEMSGSIZE path.
- **gpu-tests (lavapipe): `displays_gpu` SIGSEGV after 7 of 11 tests.** This is the Wave 3 pattern again: every test made and dropped its own device on a parallel harness thread. The 1.4 lifecycle lock serialises creation and teardown, but rendering on other live devices still overlaps a teardown, and CI's lavapipe (LLVM 21) still crashed. It does not reproduce locally: 12 runs before the fix and 25 runs × 2 binaries after, all clean. **Decision:** as in `post_gpu`, `reflections_gpu`, `gpu_pbr_render` and `ui_overlay_gpu`, `displays_gpu.rs` and `spec_5_3_gpu.rs` now share one `&'static GpuContext` per test process through a `OnceLock`, so no device is torn down while tests run. In the Tester file only device acquisition changed: `context_for_tests` became `shared_context`, plus a file-level `allow(clippy::needless_borrow)` so the `&ctx` call sites stay as they were. No assertion changed.
- **gpu-tests (Metal): `ui_overlay_gpu` 4 of 5 failed. The test is fragile; the product is fine.** The failure counts are exact: 27 648 = 320·240 − 256·192 and 110 592 = 640·480 − 512·384, i.e. every pixel outside the panel. So the cleared background, not the painter, differed from the expected value. `BG` red was 0.9, and `0.9_f32 · 255` = 229.49999 sits on a unorm rounding boundary. The test expects 230 (f32 `round`), as lavapipe and WARP store; Metal stored 229 (it also holds if Metal converts through f16: 0.8999 → 229.47). The empty frame and the outside counts follow directly. The glyph count of 10 = 6 + 4 follows too: `bright_mask` counted any bright pixel that was `!= bg_u8()`, so the panel's four rounded corners, which show the playfield, became blobs. **Decision:** (a) `BG` is now exactly (230, 217, 153)/255, so every backend stores the same bytes, even through f16. The painter, its `LoadOp::Load` pass, the `Rgba8Unorm` target and the readback are unchanged and backend-agnostic. (b) `bright_mask` counts a pixel when it is bright (luma > 128) and grey (channel spread < 32). That matches white text on the near-neutral backdrop and never matches the strongly coloured playfield (spread 77), whatever byte the background rounds to. The "i i i" test still requires exactly 6 blobs, so tofu boxes (3) still fail. Verified on lavapipe: 5/5 pass. Only CI can confirm Metal.

## Orchestrator — gate fix review notes
The "3.5 — DOF bridge public API" line saying Windows `ConnectionReset`/`MessageSize` "are skipped" is superseded by "Wave 5 gate fix": `MessageSize` is now processed as a maximum-length datagram (always rejected by length), `ConnectionReset` is still skipped. The spec_ci replay-hash contract test's textual checks (gate-fix review N2) are acceptable for a conformance spec; tighten them if the job is restructured.
- **Round 2 (CI run 299, 9eaa3c8): the lavapipe SIGSEGV moved to `spec_5_2_gpu`.** It crashed after 2 of 20 tests. The crash follows whichever test binary still creates and drops a device per test. **Decision:** every GPU test binary whose purpose is not device creation now uses one context per process, held in a `OnceLock` and never dropped. Only device acquisition changed; no assertion did. The binaries:
  - `spec_5_1_gpu.rs` and `spec_5_2_gpu.rs`: `gpu()` returns `Option<&'static GpuContext>`. The old body is now `new_context()`. The `Scene`, `ReflScene` and `Chain` structs hold `&'static GpuContext`. A file-level `allow(clippy::needless_borrow)` keeps the `&ctx` call sites as they were.
  - `spec_debug_render.rs`: `two_contexts_can_coexist` still needs two separate contexts. So `gpu()` returns a `SharedGpu`, which derefs to the shared context and holds a read lock on a file-level `RwLock` for the whole test. `two_contexts_can_coexist` takes the write lock, then creates and drops its two contexts with `new_context()`. No device is therefore created or torn down while another test in the binary renders.
  - `pin_app/tests/ui_snapshots.rs`: this binary already had only one test and one context per process. It now uses a `OnceLock` too, for uniformity.

  **Left alone:**
  - `spec_gpu_options.rs`: it never creates a device. Its only constructor call has no backends enabled and fails with `NoAdapter`.
  - `gpu_context_concurrency.rs`: it is the 1.4 regression test, and concurrent create/render/drop is its purpose. Creation and teardown already run under `GpuContext`'s process-wide lifecycle lock. It runs as its own process and passed on lavapipe in runs 294 and 299 (it runs before the `spec_*` binaries). If it ever crashes on lavapipe, that is the driver bug it exists to show; the engine itself has one context.

  **Verified:** on lavapipe under Xvfb, 10 full runs of `cargo test -p pin_render --features gpu-tests` (497 tests each) plus `pin_app --test ui_snapshots` with `PIN_UI_SNAPSHOT_DIR` set: 0 failures. Only CI's Mesa/LLVM 21 has shown the crash; the local driver never has.

## CodeRabbit review round 1 (stacked PRs #3–#6) — Orchestrator notes

- **DOF bridge `--bind` is loopback-only.** The bridge does not authenticate senders, so `BridgeApp.Run` refuses a non-loopback `--bind` (exit 2; IPv4-mapped loopback allowed; `BridgeApp.IsLoopbackBind(IPAddress)` is public). `BridgeOptions.Parse` stays permissive. Revisit if a remote-telemetry use case appears (needs an authenticated transport first).
- **`DofBridge::stop` reaper-start failure.** See the 3.5 Rust API entry: the stop completes synchronously only when the reaper thread cannot be spawned.
- **Deferred findings that change simulation output** (pin_physics chain/end-stop, mesh vertex ownership, drop-target face check, slider/spinner/tilt-bob edge cases, spline bracket refinement) are left to the person; see the PR #4 threads.
- **pin_render (PR #6 fixes):** motion blur composites every ball covering a pixel per shutter sample (the deepest-in-radii ball wins on overlap); multiplayer score rows are fitted to `SCORE_COLUMN_WIDTH` (0.47 of the box) via `ScoreText::max_w`; `SceneLibrary` names are unique (a reused name moves to the new id) and `load` rejects duplicate ids; the fallback score view runs its own timeline from the end of the last timed scene. Deferred to the person: sRGB-target blending (documented approximation), detached presenter after join timeout (trade-off N4), occluded-ball blur (needs depth input).
- **Vendored Lua (KHZ patch 27):** the GC ignores `__mode`, so guest weak/ephemeron tables are strong; this closes an unbudgeted GC path (CodeRabbit PR #5).
